The Manifest Awakening of the Great Vairocana: The Extensive Blessing of Its Manifestations, a Dharma Discourse Called the King of the Lords of Sūtras (Mahāvairocanābhisaṃbodhivikurvitādhiṣṭhānavaipulyasūtrendrarājanāmadharmaparyāya)

The Manifest Awakening of the Great Vairocana, known in Sanskrit as the Mahāvairocanābhisaṃbodhi and in Tibetan as Nampar Nangdzé Chenpo Ngönpar Dzokpar Changchupa, is one of the foundational scriptures of esoteric Buddhism, presented in its full title as an extensive discourse on the manifestations and blessing of Vairocana's perfect awakening, a king among sūtras. It is Toh 494 in the Kangyur, where it belongs to the Tantra section among the tantras of conduct (caryā tantra). Set in Vairocana's vast vajra palace of the realm of phenomena, the text unfolds as a dialogue between Vairocana and the Lord of Secrets, Vajrapāṇi, teaching the mind of awakening rooted in compassion and the practice of secret mantra, together with the construction of maṇḍalas, the use of mantras and mudrās, rites for removing obstructors, and the duty of protecting this teaching and its practitioners. It was translated into Tibetan by the Indian preceptor Śīlendrabodhi and the Tibetan translator Paltsek.

In the language of India: mahābairocana abhisaṃbodhi bikurbbati adhiṣṭhāna baipulya sūtra indrarājā nāma dharmmaparyāya

In the language of Tibet: The Discourse of Dharma Called the King of the Lords of Sūtras, the Extensive Blessing of the Manifestations of the Complete and Perfect Awakening of the Great Vairocana.

The first fascicle.

Homage to the Blessed One, Lord of Speech.

Thus did I hear at one time. The Blessed One was dwelling within the great mansion of the king of jewels: the vast great vajra blessed by all the tathāgatas, the palace of the realm of phenomena (dharmadhātu), where all the vajra holders gather, which sports in manifold display through the inclination of the tathāgatas, which has neither edge nor center and whose pinnacles are lofty, and which arises superbly adorned with kings of jewels. There he was seated upon the lion throne of the bodhisattva's body, together with vajra holders as numerous as the particles of the atoms of ten buddha fields, namely: the vajra holders Stainless Space, Moving Through Space, Arising from Space, Wearing Various Garments, Moving in Various Ways, Abiding in the Equality of All Phenomena, Vajra Protecting the Limitless Realm of Beings, Possessing the Strength of Nārāyaṇa, Possessing the Strength of the Great Nārāyaṇa, Excellent Vajra, Bearer of the Force of the Supreme Vajra, Stainless Vajra, Holder of the Supreme Vajra, Armor of the Tathāgata, Arising into the State of the Tathāgata, Abiding Free of Elaboration, Born from the Ten Powers of the Tathāgata, Stainless Eye, and the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi, and others besides.

Surrounded also by the great bodhisattvas Samantabhadra, Maitreya, Mañjuśrī, Sarvanīvaraṇaviṣkambhin and others, and gazed upon by them from before him, he taught the Dharma of the Dharma door called "The Ground of the Equality of Body, Speech and Mind," having blessed the day of the Tathāgata that transcends the three times.

Then those bodhisattvas, Samantabhadra and the others, and those vajra holders, the Lord of Secrets and the others, through the blessing of the Blessed One Vairocana, entered the unfolding of the inexhaustible treasury of the array of the equality of body. In the same way they entered the unfolding of the inexhaustible treasury of the array of the equality of speech and of mind. Yet they perceived no entering into, nor any turning back from, the body, speech or mind of the Blessed One Vairocana.

Everywhere, moreover, all the activities of body, all the activities of speech and all the activities of mind of the Blessed One Vairocana were seen teaching the Dharma to all the realms of beings throughout, with these words in the way of secret mantra. That is to say: for beings whose lives have been produced by karma, taking them up from the first generation of the mind of awakening, in order to bring them to completion in this very life up to the abiding on the ten grounds; and also, for those whose karmic lives have been cut off, in order to bring about the sprout of existence, forms resembling the bodies of the vajra holders, and resembling the bodies of the bodhisattva Samantabhadra and of Padmapāṇi, were also seen in the ten directions teaching the Dharma with the pure words of the way of secret mantra.

Then the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi, who was seated in that very assembly, addressed the Blessed One with these words: "Blessed One, the tathāgatas, the arhats, the perfectly complete buddhas, having attained omniscient wisdom, distribute that omniscient wisdom among beings and teach it through various modes, various intentions and various means. To some they teach the way of the śrāvaka vehicle, to some the way of the pratyekabuddha vehicle, to some the way of the Great Vehicle, to some the way of the five superknowledges; to some they teach the Dharma for being born as gods, to some for being born as humans, and so on up to being born as mahoragas, nāgas, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas and kiṃnaras.

"Among those beings, those to be tamed by a buddha see it in the form of a buddha, some in the form of a śrāvaka, some in the form of a pratyekabuddha, some in the form of a bodhisattva, some in the form of Maheśvara, some in the form of Brahmā, some in the form of Nārāyaṇa, some in the form of Vaiśravaṇa, and so on, some seeing it as mahoragas, and as humans and nonhumans. They see it in the modes of speech of each of their own kinds and in various modes of conduct. Yet that omniscient wisdom is, in this way, of one taste, the taste of the liberation of the Tathāgata.

"Blessed One, for example, the element of space is free from all thought and all conceptualization; it does not think and does not conceptualize. Likewise, Blessed One, omniscient wisdom too is free from all thought; it does not think and does not conceptualize.

"Blessed One, for example, this great earth is the sustenance of all beings. Likewise, Blessed One, omniscient wisdom too is the sustenance of the world with its gods, humans and asuras.

"Blessed One, for example, the element of fire burns all fuel and is never sated. Likewise omniscient wisdom too burns all the fuel of ignorance and is never sated.

"Blessed One, for example, the element of wind clears away all dust. Likewise, Blessed One, omniscient wisdom too clears away all the dust of ignorance.

"Blessed One, for example, the element of water refreshes the bodies of all beings.

"Likewise omniscient wisdom too thoroughly refreshes the world with its gods. That being so, Blessed One, what is the cause of this wisdom? What is its root? What is its culmination?"

When he had made this request, the Blessed One Vairocana spoke these words to Vajrapāṇi: "Vajrapāṇi, excellent, excellent! Vajrapāṇi, it is excellent indeed that you ask me about this matter. Therefore listen, and keep it well and thoroughly in mind, and I shall explain it to you.

"The cause is the awakening mind. The root is great compassion. The culmination is skillful means.

"Lord of Secrets, what then is awakening? It is to know one's own mind fully, exactly as it really is, and that is unsurpassed, perfect and complete awakening. Lord of Secrets, in it there is not even an atom of any phenomenon, and nothing is perceived. Why is that? Because awakening has the characteristic of space. In it there is no one who awakens, and nothing whatsoever to be awakened to. Why is that? Because awakening has no characteristics. Lord of Secrets, all phenomena too are without characteristics; that is, they have the characteristic of space."

Then Vajrapāṇi again spoke these words to the Blessed One: "Blessed One, where should omniscience be sought? By what is awakening fully and perfectly attained?"

The Blessed One said: "Lord of Secrets, awakening and omniscience are to be sought in one's own mind. Why is that? Because that mind is by nature completely pure. It is not within, nor is it outside, nor is it perceived anywhere between the two.

"Lord of Secrets, that mind has not been seen by the tathāgatas, the arhats, the perfectly complete buddhas, nor will it be seen. It is not blue, it is not yellow, it is not red, it is not white, it is not madder colored, it is not crystal colored. It is not short, it is not long, it is not round, it is not square. It is not light, it is not darkness. It is not male, it is not female, it is not neuter.

"Lord of Secrets, that mind is not of the nature of the desire realm, it is not of the nature of the form realm, it is not of the nature of the formless realm. It is not of the nature of the shared classes of beings among the destinies of gods, nāgas, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas, kinnaras, mahoragas, humans and nonhumans.

"Lord of Secrets, that mind does not abide in the eye; it is not in the ear, it is not in the nose, it is not in the tongue, it is not in the body, and it does not abide in the mind. Why is that? Because that mind has the characteristic of space, and is therefore free from all thought and all conceptualization. Why is that? Because whatever is the nature of space is the nature of the mind, and whatever is the nature of the mind is the nature of awakening. Lord of Secrets, thus mind, the expanse of space and awakening are not two and cannot be made into two. Moreover, it has compassion as its root and is embraced by the perfection of skillful means.

"Therefore, Lord of Secrets, by this explanation too it should be understood that whatever teachings I give in the assembly of bodhisattvas, all of them are for the sake of purifying the awakening mind and of fully knowing the mind. Therefore, Lord of Secrets, a son or daughter of good family who wishes to know awakening fully should fully know their own mind.

"Lord of Secrets, if you ask how one should fully know one's own mind, it is like this: though one seeks it thoroughly as an aspect, or as a color, or as a shape, or as an object, or as form, or as feeling, or as perception, or as formations, or as consciousness, or as a self, or as belonging to a self, or as the grasped, or as the grasper, or as pure, or as impure, or as an element, or as a sense source, and so on through every aspect, it is not found.

"Lord of Secrets, this is the first way of the illumination of the Dharma, the door of the illumination of the Dharma called the door of the purification of the bodhisattva's awakening mind. Abiding in it, bodhisattvas attain with little difficulty the meditative absorption that dispels all obscurations. Having attained it, bodhisattvas come to dwell in the company of all the buddhas. They also attain the five superknowledges. They also attain the dhāraṇī of limitless languages, sounds and tones. They come to know the thoughts of beings, and, blessed by all the tathāgatas, they come to have a nature that never degenerates while in saṃsāra. They become untiring in working for the welfare of beings. They become firmly established in unconditioned moral discipline, they abandon wrong views, and they come to realize and penetrate the correct view.

"Furthermore, Lord of Secrets, a bodhisattva who abides in this meditative absorption that dispels all obscurations, through the power of devoted faith that has arisen, perfects all the qualities of a buddha with little difficulty. Lord of Secrets, in short, that son or daughter of good family comes to possess immeasurable good qualities."

Then Vajrapāṇi again questioned the Blessed One: "Blessed One, how is it that one speaks of generating the mind toward awakening?

"How should one know the characteristics
Of the mind that arises, from which awakening comes?
Supreme among those who know minds,
Great Hero, born of wisdom,
Please teach how many minds it passes beyond
And then comes to arise.
Protector, please explain at length
The characteristics of the mind and the times as well.
What kind of merit do they have,
And what kind of conduct do they come to have?
Great Sage, please teach also
The distinctions of mind upon mind."

When he had made this request, at that time
The Blessed One, the Great Vairocana,
Said these words to Vajrapāṇi of noble birth:
"Excellent, my son!
The supreme state of the Great Vehicle,
The characteristics of the mind's continuum,
The supreme secret of the perfect buddhas,
Which no logicians know,
I shall explain to you in full.
Gather your mind one-pointedly and listen.

"Passing beyond a hundred and sixty minds,
For the sake of awakening itself,
The essence from which the greatest merit comes,
That from which awakening arises, is born.
Measureless like space,
Unstained, it abides just as it is;
It is not moved by any phenomenon,
Primordially at peace and without characteristics.
Accomplished through measureless wisdom,
It is the perfect Buddha, the Teacher.
Because it is accomplished through offering and conduct,
It is held to be the first, for the sake of awakening.

"Lord of Secrets, in beginningless saṃsāra, childish ordinary beings, through clinging to the grasping at 'I' and the grasping at 'mine,' have conceived countless distinctions of the self. Lord of Secrets, without examining the very nature of the self, they rouse the grasping at 'I,' thinking 'this is my self.' Others conceive of it as arising from time, as emanated by a holder, as emerging with certainty; that is, as Īśvara, the Emitter, Time, the Principal (pradhāna), the agent, the non-agent, yoga, the measure, the pure, the impure, the inner self, the Person (puruṣa), the all-pervading lord, the life principle, the individual, consciousness, the all-base (ālaya), the knower, the seer, the grasped, the grasper, knowledge, the knowable, that which is to be realized, the Manu-born, the child of Manu, the expressible and the inexpressible. Lord of Secrets, these and other such distinctions of the self were conceived in the past by yogis who desired yoga, wishing for liberation.

"Furthermore, Lord of Secrets, among childish ordinary beings, who are like cattle, once in a hundred times a thought of the Dharma occasionally arises, like this: 'I shall keep the fasting observance.' They hold just that much in mind, and, gaining satisfaction from it, they cultivate it again and again. Lord of Secrets, that is the first mind, like the seed from which virtuous action grows.

"With that as its cause, on each of the days that bear that mark, one gives gifts to one's father and mother, sons and daughters, friends and relatives. This is the second, like a sprout.

"Furthermore, one gives that same gift also to those one does not know. This is the third, like a stalk.

"Furthermore, one seeks out worthy recipients and gives that same gift to them. This is the fourth, like leaves.

"Furthermore, one joyfully gives that same gift to teachers or to musicians. This is the fifth, like a flower.

"Furthermore, one gives that same gift to those who are dear to one's heart. This is the sixth, like a fruit.

"Furthermore, Lord of Secrets, one guards moral discipline in order to be reborn in the higher realms. This is the seventh, like its enjoyment.

"Furthermore, Lord of Secrets, while one wanders in saṃsāra with just such a mind, one hears these words from some spiritual friend:

"'These gods are great gods;
They grant happiness to all.
If you honor them with reverence,
All will become prosperity for you.
They are, namely,
Īśvara and Brahmā,
Likewise Viṣṇu, Śaṅkara,
Rudra and also Skanda,
The Sun, the Moon, Varuṇa himself,
Kubera, Dhanada, Śakra,
Virūpākṣa, Viśvakarman,
Yama, Kālarātri, Nirvāṇa,
The one called Chief of the World,
Agni, Garuḍa the son of Vinatā,
Likewise the goddess Umā,
Padma and the nāga Takṣaka,
Vāsuki and the one called Śaṅkha,
Karkoṭaka, Mahāpadma,
Kulika and Many-Hooded,
The Eternal One, Ananta, the Primordial God,
Likewise the gods and the supreme sages,
The Veda and the men of the Veda.
Make offerings to them, wise one!'

"Hearing such words from him,
One's mind becomes truly satisfied,
And then, filled with deep reverence,
One serves them devotedly.

"Lord of Secrets, this is the supreme relief for childish ordinary beings who wander throughout saṃsāra. This has become a strength, and the Victors have declared it the eighth mind.

"Furthermore, Lord of Secrets, with higher understanding, among those who abide distinctively in each of the distinctions proclaimed as described, an understanding toward the aim of isolation (kaivalya) arises. Yet, saying 'it exists, it exists,' they think of emptiness alone as an entity. Lord of Secrets, they do not truly know emptiness. Emptiness is not an entity, nor is it a non-entity; it is not existent, nor is it nonexistent, nor is it both. If there is neither conceptualizing nor non-conceptualizing about it, how could one conceive of it as 'empty'?

"Those who do not know emptiness
Do not know nirvāṇa.
Therefore, by destroying entity and non-entity,
Emptiness is to be known."

Then Vajrapāṇi again made a request to the Blessed One: "Blessed One, please explain those distinctions of mind." When he had made this request, the Blessed One spoke these words to the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi:

"Lord of Secrets, listen to the characteristics of the distinctions of mind. Lord of Secrets, these minds are: the mind of attachment, the mind free of attachment, the mind of aversion, the mind of loving-kindness, the mind of delusion, the mind of wisdom, the decisive mind, the mind of doubt, the darkened mind, the illuminated mind, the gathering mind, the mind of strife, the mind of dispute, the mind without dispute, the mind of a god, the mind of an asura, the mind of a nāga, the mind of a human, the mind of a woman, the mind of a lord, the mind of a merchant, the mind of a farmer, the mind of a river, the mind of a pond, the mind of a well, the guarding mind, the mind of avarice, the mind of a dog, the mind of a cat, the mind of a garuḍa, the mind of a mouse, the mind of song, the mind of dance, the mind of the cymbal, the mind of a house, the mind of a lion, the mind of an owl, the mind of a crow, the mind of a rākṣasa, the mind of a thorn, the mind of the underground, the mind of wind, the mind of water, the mind of fire, the mind of mud, the mind of turbidity, the mind of pigment, the mind of a plank, the mind of error, the mind of poison, the mind of a noose, the mind of a fetter, the mind of a cloud, the mind of a field, the mind of salt, the mind of a razor, the mind like Mount Meru, the mind like the ocean, the mind like a cave, and the mind that has been born.

"Lord of Secrets, what is the mind of attachment? It is to engage with phenomena with attachment. What is the mind free of attachment? It is to engage with phenomena free of attachment. What is the mind of aversion? It is to engage with phenomena with hostility. What is the mind of loving-kindness? It is to engage with phenomena endowed with loving-kindness. What is the mind of delusion? It is to engage with phenomena without examining them. What is the mind of wisdom? It is to engage with phenomena so as to realize distinction upon distinction. What is the decisive mind? It is to act exactly in accordance with the command and words of one's teacher. What is the mind of doubt? It is to engage in hesitation about certainties, however they are held. What is the darkened mind? It is to perceive doubt where there is nothing to doubt. What is the illuminated mind? It is to engage without doubt where there is nothing to doubt. What is the gathering mind? It is to make many into one. What is the mind of strife? It is mutual contention. What is the mind of dispute? It is to dispute self against self. What is the mind without dispute? It is to view all alike, with evenness.

"What is the mind of a god? It is to be proud of oneself in one's mind. What is the mind of an asura? It is to disparage saṃsāra itself. What is the mind of a nāga? It is to think of acquiring great wealth. What is the mind of a human? It is to think for the sake of that very other. What is the mind of a woman? It is to engage with phenomena solely for the sake of desire. What is the mind of a lord? It is to be proud, thinking, 'I have conceived everything.' What is the mind of a merchant? It is to gather, multiply, and afterwards enjoy. What is the mind of a farmer? It is to seek many kinds of learning and afterwards make use of them. What is the mind of a river? It is to engage while looking to the two extremes. What is the mind of a pond? It is to engage with craving. What is the mind of a well? It is to think what is not deep to be deep. What is the guarding mind? It is to think, 'This alone is true; anything else is delusion.' What is the mind of avarice? It is to keep things for oneself and not give to others. What is the mind of a cat? It is to engage while looking for honors. What is the mind of a dog? It is to be satisfied even with little. What is the mind of a garuḍa? It is to engage with the Dharma by holding to a faction. What is the mind of a mouse? It is to think of cutting through all bonds.

"What is the mind of song? It is to think, 'I shall win over beings with songs of various melodies.' What is the mind of dance? It is to think, 'Through various miracles I shall bring others to embrace what is right.' What is the mind of the cymbal? It is to think, 'I shall beat the drum of the Dharma.' What is the mind of a house? It is to engage with things in order to protect one's own body. What is the mind of a lion? It is to engage in overpowering all. What is the mind of an owl? It is to think at night. What is the mind of a crow? It is to think with fear of everything. What is the mind of a rākṣasa? It is to accomplish nonvirtue by means of virtue. What is the mind of a thorn? It is to think with regret about everything. What is the mind of the underground? It is to think for the sake of what lies beneath the earth. What is the mind of wind? It is to accomplish everything everywhere. What is the mind of water? It is to engage with things in order to wash away all nonvirtue. What is the mind of fire? It is to torment oneself with austerities. What is the mind of mud? It is to blame others for one's own faults. What is the mind of turbidity? It is to stir up turbidity in the mind by fixing on the faults of others. What is the mind of pigment? It is to engage with things through attachment to them. What is the mind of a plank? It is to engage while abandoning other virtues.

"What is the mind of error? It is to take hold of one thing and think it entirely another. What is the mind of poison? It is to think of utter non-existence. What is the mind of a noose? It is to remain bound up in oneself in every respect. What is the mind of a fetter? It is to think while staying on one's two feet. What is the mind of a cloud? It is to think of rain. What is the mind of a field? It is to tend one's own body. What is the mind of salt? It is to think further about whatever one has been thinking. What is the mind of a razor? It is to rely on mere going forth. What is the mind like Mount Meru? It is to think from a lofty height. What is the mind like the ocean? It is to remain holding one's own body as supreme. What is the mind like a cave? It is to be certain at first and afterwards to change by that very thing. And what is the mind that has been born? It is the mind that goes along with just whatever formation it was born together with.

Lord of Secrets, in this way, by the doubling of one, two, three, four, and five, one passes beyond the one hundred and sixty worldly minds over three eons, and the supramundane mind arises, namely:

This is nothing but the aggregates;
A self never exists at all.
Through the elements, the city of the senses,
The world is ruined, to no purpose.
These twelve links of dependent origination
Are set in motion by afflictions and karma;
Arising from the seed of ignorance,
They are free of any agent and the rest.
Thus they are peaceful and most profound,
Hard for all reasoners to understand,
Free of every fault,
As the buddhas taught of old.

Lord of Secrets, even in those who abide in the supramundane mind there arises the notion, "There is something in the aggregates." When they generate freedom from attachment to the aggregates, then, since the aggregates perish like foam, like bubbles on water, like an illusion, and like a mirage, they become liberated. That is, they come to realize the sphere in which the very nature of reality is at peace, free of the aggregates, the elements, the sense sources, the apprehended and the apprehender.

Lord of Secrets, this supramundane mind, connected with the eight distinctions of mind, inverted and not inverted, and free of the net of karma and afflictions, yogins pass beyond in one eon.

Furthermore, Lord of Secrets, in those who travel by the Great Vehicle and do not rely on others, the mind of the selflessness of phenomena arises. Why is that? It is because, as they practice the conduct practiced before, when the abode of the aggregates is destroyed, they fully understand essential nature, and so the perception arises that things are like an illusion, a mirage, a reflection, an echo, a whirling firebrand, and a city of gandharvas. Therefore, Lord of Secrets, with regard to their mind, even the selflessness of phenomena is to be abandoned: one's own mind is unborn from the very beginning. Why is that? Lord of Secrets, because they fully understand essential nature, neither a prior limit nor a later limit of mind is found. This too yogins pass beyond in two eons.

Furthermore, Lord of Secrets, in bodhisattvas who practice the bodhisattva conduct by way of secret mantra, who for immeasurable hundreds of thousands of koṭis of niyutas of eons have gathered immeasurable collections of merit and wisdom, who are embraced by immeasurable wisdom and means, who are honored by the world with its gods and asuras, who have entirely surpassed all the śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas, and who are honored by Śakra (Indra), by Brahmā the lord, by Upendra (Viṣṇu), and others, there arises the mind that is utterly without essential nature: the essential nature of emptiness, without substance, without characteristics, without signs, beyond all conceptual elaboration, immeasurable like space, the open ground of all phenomena, free of the element of the conditioned and the unconditioned, without action or deed, and free of eye, ear, nose, tongue, body, and mind. Lord of Secrets, the Victors have declared this to be the first awakening mind.

They are freed from afflictions and karma,
Yet they are also endowed with karma and afflictions.
They are worthy of the world's offerings;
They should always be worshiped and portrayed in images.

Furthermore, Lord of Secrets:

The ground of conduct through devoted interest
Is the cultivation of the three minds.
Through practicing the perfections
And the four means of gathering,
The ground of devoted interest is unequaled,
Immeasurable and inconceivable.
The arising of immeasurable wisdom
Is attained by means of the ten minds.
Whatever little I have spoken of
Is all attained through this.
Therefore the Omniscient One
Has called it devoted interest.
In a single eon the wise
Pass wholly beyond the grounds,
And by a quarter of this
They pass beyond devoted interest as well.

Then Vajrapāṇi again addressed the Blessed One:

Protector, all-pervading Lord,
Since you have taught the characteristics of mind,
How many kinds of solace must they gain
For those supreme-born ones to find solace?

When he had requested this, the Blessed One,
The Great Vairocana,
Then said to Vajrapāṇi,
"Listen to this with a collected mind."

Lord of Secrets, when childish ordinary beings abandon nonvirtuous actions and perform virtuous actions, at that time they find solace through virtue. When they fully understand the reality of the self just as it truly is, at that time they find solace by relying on the self. When, having grasped the aggregates completely and examined their own essential nature, they give up relying on a self, at that time they find solace through selflessness. When, having abandoned the aggregates, they abide in phenomena as their object, at that time they find the solace of phenomena. When, having abandoned phenomena, they abide in non-arising, at that time they find solace through the selflessness of phenomena. When the wisdom of emptiness arises, knowing that the aggregates, the elements, and all the sense sources, the apprehended and the apprehender, the self, the life principle and the rest, phenomena, and even the unborn self are without essential nature even as the essential nature of emptiness, at that time they find solace through the sameness of the essential nature of all phenomena.

Lord of Secrets, then bodhisattvas who practice the bodhisattva conduct by way of secret mantra, by meditating on dependent origination through ten similes, will come to understand and realize the way of secret mantra. What are the ten? They should meditate on it as like an illusion, a mirage, a dream, a reflection, a city of gandharvas, an echo, the moon in water, a bubble on water, an optical illusion, and a whirling firebrand.

Lord of Secrets, bodhisattvas who practice the bodhisattva conduct by way of secret mantra should examine these one by one, as follows. What is an illusion? Through the craft of those who work with medicines and mantras, various different things are displayed, captivating to the eye and the mind, things never seen before; yet they do not go to or come from one another in the ten directions.

Why is that? Because an illusion is empty by its intrinsic nature. In the same way, everything arises from the practice of those who apply secret mantra.

Furthermore, Lord of Secrets, a mirage is empty by its intrinsic nature; yet it is designated by the conventions of perception, and it also brings about conventional activity. In the same way, this perception too is designated by convention as "secret mantra," and it also brings about conventional activity.

Furthermore, Lord of Secrets, in a dream one sees oneself remaining for a day, an instant, a moment, a year, and so on, and experiencing many kinds of happiness and suffering; yet on waking one sees nothing at all. In the same way, this way of secret mantra too should be understood to be like a dream.

Furthermore, Lord of Secrets, the arising of the accomplishments of secret mantra should be known to be like a reflection. Just as the reflection of a face arises in dependence on the face and a mirror, so too the accomplishments of secret mantra should be known to be like that.

Furthermore, Lord of Secrets, the abode of the accomplished should be regarded as like a city of gandharvas.

Furthermore, Lord of Secrets, the sound of secret mantra should be known to be like an echo. Just as an echo arises in dependence on a sound, so too this way of secret mantra should be known to be like that.

Furthermore, Lord of Secrets, when the moon has risen, one sees its reflection in water and calls it "the moon in the water." In the same way, the mantra practitioner himself, like the moon in water, is called a vidyādhara, a holder of spells.

Furthermore, Lord of Secrets:

Just as, when rain falls,
Bubbles arise on the water,
So should the various kinds of emanation
Of one accomplished in secret mantra be understood.

Furthermore, Lord of Secrets:

There is no sentient being, no life principle,
And no agent either;
In all things, which are empty,
It is merely delusion that appears.

Furthermore, Lord of Secrets:

Just as when someone takes a firebrand
In his own hand
And whirls it, it appears as a wheel,
So too do things arise from what is empty.

Lord of Secrets, this is the ground of the Great Vehicle, the ground equal to the unequaled, the ground of certainty, the ground of the perfectly complete Buddha, the ground for gradually ascending the Great Vehicle.

One who knows this treasure of riches,
From which various wisdoms abundantly arise,
Also knows, truly and fully, just as they are,
The distinctions of the minds of all.

From The Manifest and Complete Awakening, Magical Display, and Empowerment of the Blessed Great Vairocana, this concludes the chapter on the successive distinctions of mind.

Then once again Vajrapāṇi, the Lord of Secrets, addressed the Blessed One: "Blessed One, the inconceivable sphere of reality, to which the Blessed One himself has fully and perfectly awakened, wholly transcends the mind. That the Blessed One has taught it to sentient beings through various means and approaches, in keeping with their dispositions and devoted interests, is truly marvelous. Blessed One, please also explain the way of secret mantra practice, the complete performance of the great king of maṇḍalas called Arising from the Essence of Great Compassion. In the future it will bring benefit, help, and happiness to many sentient beings."

Then the Blessed One Vairocana looked upon the maṇḍala of the entire assembly and said to Vajrapāṇi, the Lord of Secrets: "Vajrapāṇi, listen to this Dharma discourse teaching the way of conduct of the maṇḍala, which brings to completion the wisdom of omniscience."

Then the Blessed One Vairocana, his intention concerning the sphere of reality not yet fulfilled, and having made the vow to liberate the whole realm of sentient beings without exception, conferred together with all the tathāgatas and entered into equipoise in the samādhi called Arising from the Essence of Great Compassion. As soon as the Blessed One had entered equipoise, from all the limbs of his body there emerged tathāgata bodies that performed beneficial deeds for sentient beings, from the first generation of the mind up to the tenth ground. Having emerged, they circled throughout the ten directions, returned, and entered their own places within the Blessed One's body.

Then once again the Blessed One said to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi: "Vajrapāṇi, listen to the arrangement of the maṇḍala. First of all, the master should be one whose mind has entered into the awakening mind; endowed with wisdom; steadfast and compassionate; skilled in the arts; always skilled in the way of the perfection of wisdom; knowing the distinctions of the three vehicles; skilled in the meaning of the reality of secret mantra; knowing the dispositions of others; having faith in the buddhas and bodhisattvas; and authorized and empowered to perform the work of drawing the maṇḍala.

Of noble family, in the practice of secret mantra
Diligently striving, and having ascertained it,
Skilled in the rites and resolute,
He abides in the awakening mind.

Lord of Secrets, such a master is a child of the Victors, highly praised by the buddhas and bodhisattvas.

Lord of Secrets, whether others make the request to the master, or whether he does so of himself:

Having seen sentient beings
Who are fit vessels and free of faults,
Of great devotion, steadfast and faithful,
Delighting in benefiting others,
And recognizing such a disciple,
The master himself
Should summon him unasked
And, wishing to benefit him, speak thus:
"Since you are a vessel of the great way,
Come here, my son! The rite
Of the way of secret mantra practice
Of the Great Vehicle I shall truly teach you.
The perfect buddhas who have passed,
Likewise those who have not yet come,
And the protectors who have now appeared,
Who abide for the benefit of beings,
All of them, knowing this supreme
And excellent rite of secret mantra,
As heroes at the foot of the tree of awakening
Attained signless omniscience.
The practice of secret mantra is unequaled:
Through it the protector Śākyasiṃha
Vanquished the utterly terrible hosts of Māra,
Mighty armies though they were.
Therefore, to attain omniscience,
My son, make this your resolve."
With a mind suffused with compassion,
He should thus praise and encourage him many times.
Knowing the disciple to be steadfast,
He should carefully examine the ground.

Mountains with flowers and fruit,
Clean and pleasing places by water,
These are praised by all the buddhas
For the work of drawing the maṇḍala.
Where there are many flowing rivers
Adorned with geese and swans,
There the intelligent mantra practitioner
Should draw the maṇḍala Arising from Compassion.
The places frequented by the buddhas,
By the self-arisen protectors,
And likewise by the śrāvakas of the Victor
Are ever praised by the Sage.
In various other places,
Temples and fine monastic parks,
Houses and delightful upper chambers,
Ponds and beautiful gardens,
Stūpas, monastic quarters, cowsheds,
The confluences of rivers,
All the abodes of the gods,
And likewise empty houses as well,
Wishing to benefit the disciples,
He should diligently draw the maṇḍala.
Or else he may draw it
On any pleasing ground whatsoever.

Lord of Secrets, the ground to be used should be without stones, rubble, potsherds, or gravel; cleared of hair, chaff, charcoal, ashes, thorns, bones, rotten wood, and splinters; without ants, worms, or other creatures; and rid of every fault. Such a place is most auspicious for the gods.

Then, on a day endowed with the signs of an excellent lunar date, constellation, planet, karaṇa, and moment, in the morning, marked by good omens, one should pay homage to all the tathāgatas and proclaim two verses addressing the earth goddess, Pṛthivī:

"Of the distinctive way of conduct
Of all the protecting buddhas,
Of their grounds and their perfections,
Goddess, you have been the witness.
Just as the protector Śākyasiṃha
Vanquished the hosts of Māra,
So shall I be victorious
Over Māra, and I draw the maṇḍala."

Touching the ground with the hand
And addressing her thus many times,
Placing both knees upon the ground,
One should make offering with flowers and incense.
Having made offering, the mantra practitioner
Should then bow to the tathāgatas,
And then, by means of these rites,
The wise one should purify the ground.

Then Vajrapāṇi touched his head to the feet of the Blessed One and addressed the Blessed One thus:

"The Buddha is without signs
And abides in the body of Dharma.
Since you teach the Dharma that is unequaled,
Signless and unconditioned,
Why, O great hero,
Do you teach this rite endowed with signs,
The practice of secret mantra?
This is not the way of the nature of reality."

When he had requested this, the Blessed One,
The Great Vairocana,
Then said to Vajrapāṇi,
"Listen to this characteristic of Dharma.
Dharma is free of conceptualization;
It is the abandonment of all conceptual thought.
In it the conceptions of mind
And mental factors are entirely abandoned.
The Dharma to which I fully awakened
Has arisen from space;
Childish beings, whose domain
Is wrong conception, do not know it.
Those obscured by darkness
Accept place, time, signs, and the like;
In order to take them in,
This rite too is spoken.
There is no place and no time,
No deed and no doer either;
In all phenomena there is nothing whatsoever:
Phenomena are merely partial.
Nevertheless, Lord of Secrets,
In the future there will appear
Sentient beings of feeble intellect,
Deluded by mere activity,
Attached to the objects of things.
Not knowing a way such as this,
Through place, time, and deed,
And through signs of virtue and nonvirtue,
The deluded seek results.
For their sake, great being,
This rite is spoken."

Lord of Secrets, in whichever of the places described, the ground should be firmly tamped and smeared with cow dung that has not fallen to the earth, mixed with cow urine. Then one should sprinkle it for purification with this mantra and consecrate it:

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ apratisame gagana samatānugate prakṛti viśuddhe dharmma dhātu viśodhani svāhā

After that the mantra practitioner
Should rise and imagine me in its center,
Seated upon a white lotus seat,
Wearing a topknot and a crown,
Encircled on every side
By light rays of various colors.
Then the intelligent one should imagine
Four perfect buddhas in the directions.
The Tathāgata Ratnaketu
He should imagine like the rising sun.
In the south is the heroic Victor
Called Saṃkusumita,
Blazing with a golden color,
Abiding in the samādhi that vanquishes faults.
In the north is Akṣobhya,
Abiding in the samādhi free of sickness.
In the west is Amitābha.
Having imagined these five Victors,
The mantra practitioner, then
Remaining in the Victors' place at the center,
Should take possession of the ground
With the secret mantra, accomplishing all aims,
Of Acala, the great being,
Or else of Trailokyavijaya.
In the center, excellent maṇḍalas
Are to be made with sandalwood:
The first one is mine;
The second is that of all the protectors.

The third, to be made equal to it,
Is without doubt that of the Mother.
The fourth is that of Padmapāṇi,
The fifth is for Vajrapāṇi,
The sixth is that of the protector Acala;
And further, below, these should be well made.
Recollecting the tathāgatas,
Offering flowers, incense, and the like,
Composed in meditation, with reverence,
One should speak these words:
"Protectors, whose nature is compassion,
All of you, please heed me.
Tomorrow, when midday has passed,
Together with your heirs, take possession of this ground."
Having spoken these words,
One should recite this secret mantra:

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ sarbba tathāgatādhiṣṭhānādhiṣṭhate acale vimale smaraṇe prakṛti pariśuddhe svāhā

Then the mantra practitioner should rise,
And with a mind suffused with compassion,
Remaining on the western side,
His inner self thoroughly purified,
Should set his mind on awakening
And lie down in that very place.
Then the mantra practitioner should watch for a dream.
He may see a bodhisattva of great renown,
Or else a buddha, a blessed one,
For the sake of benefiting sentient beings,
Exerting himself with every effort
In the tasks of drawing the maṇḍala;
Or he may see one encouraging him,
Saying to him, "Great being,
My son, well done! Draw the maṇḍala quickly!"
Then on the following day,
Disciples who are faithful and of noble family,
Likewise having faith in the Three Jewels,
Endowed with profound intelligence,
Of great enthusiasm and moral discipline,
Patient and free of avarice,
Courageous and firm in their commitments,
Ten, or eight, or seven, or five,
One, two, or four, or even more,
Should be accepted without the need for examination.

Then once again Vajrapāṇi asked the Blessed One, "Blessed One, what is the name of this maṇḍala? Why is it called a maṇḍala?"

The Blessed One said, "Lord of Secrets, this maṇḍala is the source of the buddhas. Its center is called the essence, and its circle is completeness: since there is no other essence beyond it, it is therefore called a maṇḍala. Nevertheless, Lord of Secrets, this extensive maṇḍala, because it fully protects the indeterminate realm of sentient beings, is called Arising from the Essence of Great Compassion. It has been empowered by the tathāgatas, who accomplished unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening over countless eons. Therefore, Lord of Secrets, by this explanation as well, you should understand it thus: the tathāgatas do not fully and perfectly awaken for the sake of one sentient being, nor for the sake of two, nor for the sake of three. Rather, under the sway of great compassion, they fully and perfectly awaken in order to protect the entire realm of sentient beings without exception, and they teach the Dharma to sentient beings in accordance with the dispositions of the many kinds of beings.

"Lord of Secrets, when those who have not grown accustomed to the Great Vehicle see or hear of the way of secret mantra practice, not even a little joy or faith truly arises in their minds. Lord of Secrets, those who have in the past grown accustomed to accomplishing the infinite gateways of the way of practice of the secret mantra of the Great Vehicle are Vajrasattvas, and it is for their sake that this limit on numbers was set. Nevertheless, the master who is endowed with great compassion should simply vow to liberate the entire realm of sentient beings without exception, and therefore he should accept limitless sentient beings, so that it may become the cause of the awakening mind.

"Then, having summoned them, he should have them go for refuge to the Three, and also instruct them not to commit evil deeds. Then he should give them incense, flowers, and so on, and have them also take up the vow of wisdom that is unobstructed in the three times.

Having had them take it up, the mantra practitioner
Should then give them tooth-sticks as well.
Single-pointed and composed,
Facing east or north,
They should chew the tooth-sticks
Of udumbara or aśvattha,
Of very good quality, well protected,
And anointed with flowers, scents, and the like.
Whether a being is a fit vessel or not a fit vessel
Is to be examined from how the stick is thrown.
Then upon the left hand
A thread with three knots
Should be tied well
By him, composed in meditation.
To the disciples thus accepted,
Who are without fault,
In order to make their resolve firm,
He should arouse their enthusiasm thus:
"Great beings, you have today
Obtained an unequaled gain,
Because all the Victors with their heirs,
All the great beings,
Have accepted all of you
Into this teaching, and you are being born.
Therefore tomorrow you will be
Truly born into the Great Vehicle.
If in your dreams you see
Temples and fine monastic parks,
Various fine houses,
And likewise upper chambers,
Swords and wish-fulfilling jewels,
Parasols and various flowers,
Fine women dressed in white,
Dear kinsmen and children,
Volumes of scripture, likewise brahmins,
Buddhas and pratyekabuddhas,
Likewise the śrāvakas of the Victor,
And bodhisattvas, great beings;
If you see fruits being obtained,
Or lakes and oceans being crossed;
And likewise if from the sky
Auspicious and pleasing words resound
That the desired fruit will come about,
Such dreams as these
Are to be considered good.
The wise should know dreams
Contrary to these to be bad.
You of excellent discipline,
In the morning tell me everything."
Having instructed those disciples thus,
He should also explain the Dharma:
"This supreme path, from which
The Great Vehicle greatly arises, is glorious.
By having gone upon it, you,
Self-arisen and of great fortune,
Will become tathāgatas,
Knowers of the whole world.
Beyond existence and nonexistence,
Stainless like space,
Profound, never realized
By any reasoning, without abode,
Free of all elaboration,
Yet elaborated through elaborations,
Free of action, deed, and the like,
Abiding in the two truths:
This supreme among excellent vehicles
Is attained if one abides in its way."

Then the Vajra Holder, abiding without elaboration, addressed the Blessed One thus:

"Blessed One, please explain the vow of wisdom unobstructed in the three times, abiding in which bodhisattvas will please the buddhas and bodhisattvas."

When he had made this request, the Blessed One said to the Vajra Holder, who abides without elaboration: "Son of noble family, listen to the vow in which body, speech, and mind engage as one, and which is without undertaking and without activity toward any phenomenon. What is it? It is the complete giving up of one's own body to the buddhas and bodhisattvas. Why is that? Whoever gives up his own body has given up three things. And what are the three things given up? They are body, speech, and mind. Therefore, son of noble family, a bodhisattva should be restrained by the vow of the three, body, speech, and mind. Why is that? Because bodhisattvas do not uphold the training without having fully given up body, speech, and mind."

Then on the following day, having paid homage to the Blessed One Vairocana and empowered himself as Vajrasattva, the wise one should take a vase filled with scented water, consecrate it with the mantra of Trailokyavijaya, place it at the first gate, and with it sprinkle all the people for purification. Then, to purify their minds, he should have them drink pure scented water.

Then Vajrapāṇi addressed the Blessed One thus:

"When the masters of the maṇḍala
Strive to accomplish secret mantra,
At what time in the maṇḍala
Does the great being bestow his blessing?
The time and the measure, O Omniscient One,
Supreme among speakers, please declare."
When he had asked this, the Blessed One
Said to the Vajra Holder:
"For one who undertakes the maṇḍala,
The cord should always be stretched at night."

The second section.

Vajrapāṇi, that master, having paid homage to all the buddhas, should take up a cord of various colors and empower himself as the Blessed One Vairocana.

Stretching the cord toward the east,
He should hold it level with the navel,
Holding it in the air.
Then, having gone to the south,
He should stretch it toward the north.
Then a second cord
He should take up himself, and in that very place,
Recollecting the tathāgatas
And facing east, he should stretch it.
Then, having gone to the south,
He should stretch it toward the north.
Then the intelligent one should return,
And standing in the direction of Nairṛti,
He should stretch it toward the north.
Then he should turn to the right,
And having turned, remaining there,
He should stretch it toward the south.
Then, leaving that place
And standing in the direction of Agni,
The mantra practitioner, abiding in the rite of secret mantra,
Should stretch the cord toward the east.
Then likewise, going to the direction of Īśāna
And facing west,
As far as the direction of Vāyu
He should stretch it on the north.
In this way the mantra practitioner
Should make it square and even all around.
Then, entering the center,
He should divide it into three parts.
Having divided all the ground
Everywhere into three parts,
He should again divide
Each of the parts into three.
One part is to be made
The path for circumambulation;
The remaining two parts
Are to be fixed as the places of the deities.
The intelligent one, in the four directions,
In each direction's portion, the gateway enclosures;
The one who strives in restraint should diligently
Make the places of the chief deities.
When, well divided in sequence,
All these have been made,
In the center is the supreme white lotus,
Which is proclaimed to be the essence.
All the maṇḍalas arising from the essence
Are thereby made to possess an essence.
Its measure is said to be
Sixteen finger-widths and upward.
Eight-petaled, perfectly complete,
With anthers, most excellent;
Between all the petals,
Vajras should be drawn all around.
In the center of its heart,
The supreme Victor, the chief, golden in color,
With blazing topknot and crown,
Encircled on all sides by rays of light,
The protector free of sickness,
Should be drawn abiding in samādhi.
To its east the intelligent one
Should draw, with great care,
The mudrā of the buddhas,
A triangle, entirely pure white,
Upon a lotus, surrounded all around,
Encircled by white rays of light.
To its north the wise
Should draw the Mother of all the protectors,
Golden in color and blazing,
Wearing white garments,
Illuminating everything,
Abiding in samādhi.
On the northern side of the protector,
The hero, the lord Avalokiteśvara,
Seated on a white lotus seat,
Like a conch, a kunda flower, or the moon,
With Amitābha seated upon his head,
His face gently smiling, should be drawn.
To his right, the goddess
Tārā, of great renown,
Virtuous, the dispeller of fear,
Blue-green, of various forms,
With the build of a young woman,
Palms joined, holding an utpala flower,
Encircled all around by rays of light,
Wearing white garments, should be drawn.
To his left, the goddess Bhṛkuṭī,
Holding in her hand the rosary of that mantra,
Three-eyed and with a topknot,
White in color,
Encircled by rays of white,
Yellow, and red, should be drawn.
Close to Bhṛkuṭī
Is the one of great might, holding a lotus,
Of great love, clad in white,
Encircled by rays of light:
The hero should be drawn exactly so,
And his lotus is not yet opened.
Near him the intelligent one
Should draw the vidyā goddess Yaśovatī,
Like gold in color,
Adorned with every ornament,
Holding in her left hand
A lovely sprig of priyaṅgu.
In the retinue of Tārā
The intelligent one should draw Pāṇḍaravāsinī,
With a topknot, wearing white garments,
Holding a lotus in her hand.
Below him the mantra practitioner
Should draw the vidyā king of great strength,
Colored like the rising sun,
Adorned with a white lotus,
With a garland of flames, unbearable,
His hair standing like a lion's mane,
Renowned as Hayagrīva,
Of the wise lord Avalokiteśvara.
To the south of the buddhas' mudrā,
The mudrā of the protecting bodhisattvas,
Renowned as the wish-fulfilling jewel,
Of great fortune,
Fulfilling every hope,
Should be drawn resting on a white lotus.
To the south of the protector, the Great Vairocana,
Is the Vajra Holder,
Who fulfills every hope,
His color like priyaṅgu,
Greenish yellow, or like an emerald,

Light blue, the protector wearing a crown,
The great being, adorned
With every ornament, with vajras
Vast in reach, extending without end,
Should be drawn encircled by rays of light.
To his right, the goddess by name
Called Māmakī,
Adorned with vajra ornaments,
Should be drawn holding a vajra in her hand.
Below her, the goddess
Vajra Point, of great strength,
Surrounded on all sides by messengers,
Her face faintly smiling, should be drawn.
On the left the mantra practitioner
Should draw the one called Vajra Chain (Vajraśṛṅkhalā),
Vajrapāṇi's Vajra Chain,
Surrounded by her own messengers,
Greenish yellow in color like priyaṅgu,
Well marked with the sign of the vajra.
Below Vajrapāṇi
Is the one called Krodhacandra, the Lord,
Who pacifies the great obstructors, the enemies,
The wrathful one renowned throughout the three worlds,
Three-eyed and with four fangs,
His color like a summer rain cloud,
Letting out a sound of fierce laughter,
Adorned with vajra ornaments;
His retinue, surrounding him on all sides,
Are the boundless lords of the wrathful ones;
With a hundred thousand arms
He holds and brandishes various weapons.
Such a great wrathful one
Should be drawn seated upon a lotus.
Going to the west of him,
Many vajra holders,
Holding various weapons in their hands,
Of various colors and of great strength,
Encircled by rays of light,
Should be drawn working for the benefit of all sentient beings.
Below the protector of secret mantra,
In the quarter of Nairṛti, the southwest,
Is the one called Acala, who in his hands
Firmly holds a sword and a noose,
A lock of hair hanging to the left of the crown of his head,
Richly adorned, with one eye squinting,
Abiding within his own light,
Wrathful, seated on a rock,
His face deeply furrowed in wrath,
With the form of a youth, sturdy of build.
Having drawn him, the wise one
Goes to the quarter of Vāyu, the northwest,
And there the Blessed One's
Wrathful one, Trailokyavijaya,
With a garland of flames, unbearable,
The foremost attendant and ornament of Vajrapāṇi,
Seated as though receiving his command,
Abiding like the time of the world's ending,
Should be drawn with diligence.
Thus the drawing of the inner area
Has been explained in full.
Then the mantra practitioner, coming outward,
In the second section,
Within the eastern quarter,
Dividing it into two parts,
Inside the gateway enclosure,
The mantra practitioner should draw the Victor Śākyamuni,
Golden in color and blazing,
Endowed with the thirty-two supreme marks,
The protector clad in saffron robes,
The all-pervading lord who accomplishes the aim of the teaching,
Seated on a white lotus seat;
He should be drawn seated in the manner of teaching the Dharma.
To his right is the goddess
Called Buddhalocanā,
The goddess with a gently smiling face,
With a maṇḍala of light a fathom wide,
Her matchless body utterly serene;
She is the Mother of Śākyamuni.
To her right is the goddess
Renowned as the vidyā Ūrṇā,
Seated upon a lotus,
Her color like a conch, the moon, or kunda jasmine,
Holding a wish-fulfilling jewel in her hand;
She should be drawn bringing every hope to fulfillment.
Then the mantra practitioner, on the left,
Should properly draw the five uṣṇīṣas
Of Śākyasiṃha, the protector,
Radiant and of great diligence:
White Parasol (Sitātapatra), the Victor's Crown (Jaya),
Vijayoṣṇīṣa itself,
Tejorāśi of great fortune,
And the one widely renowned as Vikiraṇa.
These five uṣṇīṣas
Of the mighty Crest of the Śākyas
Should be drawn with close attention,
Each in its own of five places.
Going to the north of these,
One should draw the gods of the Pure Abodes:
Īśvara, Samantakusuma,
Garland of Light,
Manojava, and Far-Renowned;
All of these should be well drawn.
To the right of Ūrṇā,
The Great Uṣṇīṣa, Greatly Arising,
Likewise Upward Arising,
And Anantasvaraghoṣa:
These three uṣṇīṣas alone
Should be properly drawn with close attention.
The five uṣṇīṣas of great power
Should be made white, pale yellow, and yellow;
The other three should be made
White, yellow, and red.
All of them have many rays of light,
And are adorned with every ornament;
Their aspirations are of great power,
Bringing every hope to fulfillment.

Then the mantra practitioner, in the quarter of Agni, the southeast,
Should draw Agni together with the great sages,
Seated within fire,
Marked with three finger-lines of ash,
The hero entirely deep red,
His heart marked with a triangle,
Seated in the midst of blazing flames,
Holding a rosary and a water pot.
Then, in the southern quarter,
Yama, the lord, holding in his hand
A staff, and riding a buffalo,
His color like a summer rain cloud,
Should be drawn surrounded by the mātṛkās, by Kālarātri,
And by the lords of death.
In the quarter of Nairṛti, Nairṛti,
Terrifying, with a sword in his hand, should be drawn.
Then, too, the wise one,
Going swiftly to the northern quarter,
Should draw the king of the gods (Indra),
Seated on Mount Meru and surrounded by gods,
Holding a vajra in his hand, wearing a diadem,
And adorned with every ornament.
Then, to his east,
Varuṇa should be drawn holding a noose in his hand.
Further east of him again,
The Sun should be drawn seated on a chariot.
To his right and to his left
Jaya and Vijaya should be drawn.
To his right, Brahmā,
Four-faced, wearing a topknot,
Devoted to the syllable called oṃ,
Should be drawn seated upon a goose and a lotus.
To the west of him, one should draw
Pṛthivī, the earth goddess, and Sarasvatī,
Viṣṇu, Skanda, and likewise Vāyu,
Śaṅkara, and likewise the Moon;
Placing them in the western quarter,
One should draw them with great care.
Having drawn these, the mantra practitioner then,
With mind free of confusion,
Below the protector of secret mantra,
Should draw the great wrathful one called Aparājita,
And the goddess
Aparājitā,
And the lord of the earth, holding a vase in his hand,
Properly placed, in an attitude of reverence.
The great nāgas Nanda and Upananda
Should be drawn with great care
At the two doorways of Śākyasiṃha, the protector,
Facing outward.
Moreover, whatever mantras and mudrās
Are taught for the maṇḍala
In all the tantras of Śākyamuni,
Those, too, the learned one should draw.
The mantra practitioner then, coming outward,
In the third section, as prescribed,
Should draw the Giver of the Supreme Vajra,
Whose color is like saffron,
With five locks of hair on the crown of his head,
Having the form of a young boy,
Holding in his left hand
A blue utpala bearing a vajra,
Seated on a white lotus seat,
Abiding with a broadly smiling face;
The mantra practitioner should draw him
Encircled all around by rays of excellent character.
To the north of him, the youth
Jālinīprabha, holding in his hand
A net of jewels,
Adorned with ornaments of many kinds,
Seated on a red lotus seat,
Should be drawn gazing at the Victor's son.
To the left of the Giver of the Supreme Vajra
Five female messengers should be drawn:
Keśinī and Upakeśinī,
Citrā and Vasumatī,
And likewise the one called Ākarṣaṇī;
These are the wise female messengers.
Just below them
The five female servants
And the two attendants
Of the youth Jālinīprabha should also be drawn.
Then the mantra practitioner, in the southern quarter,
Should draw the bodhisattva of great renown,
The lord Sarvanīvaraṇaviṣkambhin,
Holding a wish-fulfilling jewel.
Leaving two spaces beside him,
The learned one should draw these eight:
The Wondrous Hero,
Sarvasattvābhayaṃdada, giver of fearlessness to all beings,
Sarvāpāyajaha, who abandons all lower realms,
Likewise Paritrāṇāśayamati, of protecting intent,
The wise Karuṇāmṛdita, tender with compassion,
Maitryabhyudgata, in whom loving-kindness arises,
Sarvadāhapraśamin, who quells all burning torment,
And likewise Acintyamati, of inconceivable intelligence.
Going to the northern quarter,
One should draw Kṣitigarbha, blazing,
Perfect in patience and gentleness.
His seat, the learned one should make
Endowed with every source of treasure,
Adorned with all kinds of jewels;
Upon it, a lotus of four jewels,
Properly placed,
Should be imagined, delightful to the mind;
The bodhisattva of great renown
Should be drawn seated upon it.
His retinue, foremost in good qualities,
Is infinite, utterly beyond count;
Among them are the bodhisattvas
Ratnākara, Ratnapāṇi,
Dharaṇiṃdhara, and
Ratnamudrāhasta,
And Dṛḍhādhyāśaya, of steadfast resolve:
These of great renown should be drawn.
Likewise, the mantra practitioner, in the western quarter,
Should imagine the hero Ākāśagarbha,
Of great splendor, clad in white garments,
Holding a sword in his hand;
The Victor's son should be drawn
Well seated upon a lotus seat.
In the retinue of that great being
The renowned ones should also be drawn.
His retinue, the great heroes,
Are Gaganāmala and Gaganamati,
Viśuddhamati,
Cāritramati,
And the one called Sthiramati.
They should be drawn with great care,
And one should always contemplate them
Together with these heroes, in their proper order.

Then the Lord of Secrets, Vajrapāṇi, having looked at the entire assembly, gazed at the Blessed One Vairocana for a long while without blinking, and spoke forth this inspired utterance in these words:

"In the world, the omniscient ones,
Like the udumbara flower,
Arise only rarely, once in a hundred times,
And at times do not arise at all.
The way of conduct of secret mantra appearing
Is rarer even than that.
Whatever evil has been committed
Over many tens of millions of eons,
All of it is purified
By seeing a maṇḍala such as this;
What need, then, to mention one who abides
In the way of mantra conduct, of boundless renown?
If one recites the protector's secret mantra,
One will reach the unsurpassed state.
Those whose intelligence
Is utterly unwavering in this supreme conduct
Cut off completely the stream of the lower realms,
Suffering and its origin."

At that time that entire assembly and those vajra holders spoke with one voice, in unison:

"Master of the conduct of secret mantra,
Great hero, excellent, excellent!
Whatever you have in mind,
Ask of the peerless Omniscient One.
The other bodhisattvas
Who fully understand the mantra tantra,
And all of us who abide in the fruit of mantra practice,
Shall thereby gain mastery."

Then again Vajrapāṇi spoke thus to the Blessed One:

"What is it that is called color?
Why is this called color?
How should the colors be laid out?
Which should be applied first?
The porticoes, and likewise
The measure of the gateway enclosures,
And how the measure of the gates should be made,
May the Protector please explain.
The food offerings, flowers, unguents, and incense,
And the vases too, please teach in full:
How the disciple is to be led in,
And how he is to be empowered,
The offering of the fee to the master,
And the place of the fire offering, please tell me.
What is the characteristic of secret mantra?
What is it that is held to be samādhi?"

When he had asked this, the Blessed One,
The Sage, lord of all dharmas,
Spoke thus to the Vajra Holder:
"Listen here with a single-pointed mind!
The supreme way of the conduct of secret mantra,
From which the fruit of the Great Vehicle arises,
Whatever you, great being,
Have asked, I shall explain.
The realm of sentient beings clings to it,
And that it is the flavor of the dharmadhātu
The buddhas taught of old;
Therefore it is called color.
The colors should be laid out from within;
The colors are not laid out from outside.
White should be applied first;
After it, red;
Then yellow should be applied;
After that, green is applied;
The innermost of all is black.
Thus is the rite of the colors explained.
The measure of the porticoes, too,
Is equal to the central maṇḍala;
The gateway enclosures also equal it.
The lotus is said to be sixteen finger-widths.
The first gate is said to be
Equal to the maṇḍala of the heart.
Increasing them in succession, O fearless one,
Know the others to be larger than that.
Between the gates
The gatekeepers should be placed.
Samādhi, in brief, is said to be
The mind's perfect contentment.
Great being, as to its distinctions,
Gather your mind to a single point and listen.
The samādhi of all the buddhas
The Victor has called emptiness.
It is attained from fully knowing the mind,
And not from anything else.
Having abandoned all those others,
It is held to be that of the perfect buddhas.
It is called the great emptiness,
That which perfects the gnosis of omniscience.
Therefore, in every respect,
One should always keep emptiness in mind."

Then the Blessed One Vairocana, having conferred together with all the buddhas, taught the samādhis of the śrāvakas, the pratyekabuddhas, and the bodhisattvas, each in its own manner. At that time he also entered into equipoise in the samādhi called the Force of the Power of Being of One Continuum with All Buddhas. Then again the Blessed One spoke to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi:

"Having sat at the seat of awakening,
Because I had vanquished the four kinds of māras,
Then the hosts of gods, Brahmā and the rest,
Joyfully proclaimed
The sound of freedom from all fear,
Calling me 'Great Hero.'
From then on I, the hero,
Have been renowned as the Great Hero.
The unborn that I realized
Has left behind the domains of words,
Is free of every fault,
And is free of causes and conditions.
The gnosis of emptiness, like space,
Free of all darkness,
Utterly stainless, reality itself,
Just as it is, came forth from me.
To abide in the mode of sheer omniscience
Is the defining mark of an omniscient buddha.
This supreme reality of mine
Illuminates the world;
By the power of blessing,
Having shown it in the form of syllables,
Out of compassion for sentient beings
I have explained everything in full."

Then the glorious Vajra Holder,
Filled with wonder, his eyes wide open,
Bowed his head to the Omniscient One
And spoke these words:
"The skillful means of all the buddhas
And their inconceivable wisdom are greatly wondrous!
Having realized the Dharma
Free from all elaboration, the self-arisen ones
Teach what brings to complete fulfillment
Every hope of the whole world.
If any being knows
Such a characteristic of secret mantra,
Abiding well in the two truths,
He becomes an object of worship for the whole world."

Then the Lord of Secrets, Vajrapāṇi, having spoken these verses, gazed at the Blessed One Vairocana with an unblinking gaze and remained silent. Then the Blessed One spoke thus to the Lord of Secrets, Vajrapāṇi: "Moreover, Lord of Secrets, the way of samādhi of the bodhisattvas bound to one more birth, who abide on the ground of a buddha, is this:

Free from doubt,
Having understood the characteristics of the world,
Those who abide on the ground of the purpose
Abide on the ground of a buddha.

"Moreover, Lord of Secrets, the way of samādhi of the bodhisattvas who are lords of the eighth ground is this:

Not apprehending any phenomena at all,
Having abandoned becoming and birth,
Knowing the world to be like an illusion,
The world holds them to be masters of the world.

"Moreover, Lord of Secrets, the śrāvakas who abide on the ground that has an object, having understood arising and perishing, demolish the two extremes, and because they do not apprehend a cause, they do not engage in the wisdom of utter nonentity. This is the way of samādhi of the śrāvakas.

"Lord of Secrets, the pratyekabuddhas, having demolished cause and effect, abide in the way of the inexpressible Dharma and enter the wisdom that thinks, 'All phenomena are inexpressible.' This full understanding of the samādhi of the complete cessation of speech is the way of samādhi of the pratyekabuddhas.

"Moreover, Lord of Secrets, worldly people conceive of results arising from causes and of things arising from karma or ceasing, and through dependence on others a samādhi of emptiness arises in them. This is the way of samādhi of worldly people.

"Lord of Secrets, this is the way of samādhi.
Abiding in it, the buddhas and the pratyekabuddhas,
The protecting bodhisattvas,
And those gentle śrāvakas
Who are renowned as vanquishers of evil,
And whatever worldly gods there are:
Wishing to benefit sentient beings,
The hero then teaches
The way of the rite of the practice of secret mantra."

Then again the Blessed One spoke to the Lord of Secrets, Vajrapāṇi: "Lord of Secrets, listen to the characteristics of the secret mantras." He replied, "Blessed One, I shall do so," and listened to the Blessed One, and the Blessed One said this:

"Lord of Secrets, here,
The secret mantras of the perfect buddhas
Are marked by words, names, and conventional signs.
Because one has gained mastery of those words,
They accomplish every aim of secret mantra.
Some are endowed with words of exhortation;
Some state their own name first.
Those endowed with hūṃ and phaṭ
Are proclaimed to be those of the uṣṇīṣas.
Those that say 'Seize! Devour! Crush!'
And 'Strike! Kill! Liberate!'
The Guide has declared to be the secret mantras
Of the servants and of the wrathful ones.
Those endowed with namaḥ svāhā and the like,
Marked by the realization of samādhi,
With peaceful, thoroughly pure syllables,
Fulfilling every hope,
Are the secret mantras of all the buddhas
And of the protecting bodhisattvas.
The secret mantras of the śrāvakas
Are single, resting on a single basis;
Those of the pratyekabuddhas are likewise.
If their distinction is to be explained,
By the distinction of their samādhi
It is called the purification of karmic life.

"Moreover, Lord of Secrets, as for the characteristic of the secret mantras, they were not made by all the buddhas, nor did the buddhas cause them to be made, nor did they rejoice in their making. Why is that? Because this is the nature of reality of phenomena. Thus, whether tathāgatas appear or do not appear, this nature of reality of phenomena abides from the very beginning; and in the same way this is the secret-mantra nature of reality of the secret mantras.

"Lord of Secrets, whenever a perfectly complete buddha, omniscient and all-seeing, appears in the world, at that time, through various methods and various intentions, for sentient beings of various dispositions, he blesses that nature of reality into various words: into various syllables, various expressions in the usage of particular regions, and various sounds that can be understood. Thus he teaches the way of secret mantra.

"How is it that the tathāgatas bless the way of secret mantra into syllables? Lord of Secrets, whatever words of truth the tathāgatas fully accomplished over many hundreds of thousands of koṭis of niyutas of eons: the four noble truths, the four applications of mindfulness, the four bases of miraculous power, the ten powers of a tathāgata, the six perfections, the seven precious factors of awakening, the four abodes of Brahmā, and the eighteen unshared qualities of a buddha; Lord of Secrets, in brief, whatever is the gnosis of omniscience of the tathāgatas, all of it, having been blessed through the tathāgatas' own power of merit and gnosis, the power of knowing their former aspirations, and the power of the blessing of the dharmadhātu, they fully and properly teach the rite of secret mantra to sentient beings in accordance with their fortune.

"And what is that rite of secret mantra?
a is the gateway to all phenomena, because they are unborn from the very beginning.
ka is the gateway to all phenomena, because they are free from intention.
kha is the gateway to all phenomena, because, like space, they cannot be apprehended.
ga is the gateway to all phenomena, because no going whatever can be apprehended.
gha is the gateway to all phenomena, because utter ruin cannot be apprehended.
ca is the gateway to all phenomena, because they are free from all death and transmigration.
cha is the gateway to all phenomena, because they are like all shadows.
ja is the gateway to all phenomena, because birth cannot be apprehended.
jha is the gateway to all phenomena, because stain cannot be apprehended.
ṭa is the gateway to all phenomena, because pride cannot be apprehended.
ṭha is the gateway to all phenomena, because producing cannot be apprehended.
ḍa is the gateway to all phenomena, because disturbance cannot be apprehended.
ḍha is the gateway to all phenomena, because they are completely free from deception.
ta is the gateway to all phenomena, because suchness cannot be apprehended.
tha is the gateway to all phenomena, because a place cannot be apprehended.
da is the gateway to all phenomena, because discipline cannot be apprehended.
dha is the gateway to all phenomena, because the element cannot be apprehended.
pa is the gateway to all phenomena, because the ultimate truth cannot be apprehended.
pha is the gateway to all phenomena, because, like a bubble, they are without core.
ba is the gateway to all phenomena, because they are free from the path of speech.
bha is the gateway to all phenomena, because no existence whatever can be apprehended.
ma is the gateway to all phenomena, because death cannot be apprehended.
ya is the gateway to all phenomena, because no vehicle whatever can be apprehended.
ra is the gateway to all phenomena, because they are free from all dust.
la is the gateway to all phenomena, because characteristics are not apprehended.
ṣa is the gateway to all phenomena, because they are peaceful by nature.
ṣa is the gateway to all phenomena, because they are dull by nature.
sa is the gateway to all phenomena, because no truth whatever can be apprehended.
ha is the gateway to all phenomena, because a cause cannot be apprehended.

"Lord of Secrets, ṇa, ña, ṇa, na, and ma hold sway over all samādhis. They possess every power, and they swiftly accomplish whatever aims are needed.

"Lord of Secrets, this gateway of samādhi, the way of secret mantra, brings every hope to complete fulfillment. It is the inconceivable fruit of the ripening of all the tathāgatas, endowed with the supreme of all aspects, the definitive meaning of the reality of secret mantra, utterly transcending the three times, and stainless like space.

It abides on the ground of inconceivable mind,
And arises from actions and deeds.
Having attained the ground of conduct,
It bestows inconceivable fruit.
This is the supreme reality,
Fully taught by all the buddhas.
Knowing this rite of secret mantra,
The mantra practitioner will gain accomplishment.
What is called sound is the supreme reality,
The mark of the secret mantras.
If the mantra practitioner holds it in mind,
He will attain the state beyond death and transmigration."

Then the Lord of Secrets, Vajrapāṇi, said this to the Blessed One: "Blessed One, it is wondrous that the Blessed One has taught this inconceivable discourse on the way of the characteristics of secret mantra. It is not shared with any śrāvakas or pratyekabuddhas. Blessed One, those sentient beings who have faith in this way of secret mantra will also come to possess qualities that are not shared. Blessed One, please explain further the rite of the requisites of the maṇḍala."

When he had made this request, the Blessed One spoke thus to the Lord of Secrets, Vajrapāṇi:

"Then that mantra practitioner,
With yellow, white, and red flowers,
Pleasing ones, should diligently
Make offerings to the deities.
Lotus, or else utpala,
Nāgakesara, punnāga,
Campaka, aśoka, tilaka,
Pāṭala, or even śāla:
Flowers such as these,
Pleasing and lovely to behold,
And auspicious, the mantra practitioner,
The learned one, should diligently offer.
Sandalwood, tagara, spṛkkā,
Saffron, or costus too:
Supreme fragrances, most excellent,
Of many pleasing kinds, should be offered.
Aloeswood, or pine resin,
Camphor and sandalwood,
The auspicious resin of the śāla tree,
Or śrīvāsaka too,
And other incense of various kinds,
Auspicious and renowned in the world,
Pleasing ones, the mantra practitioner, according to the rite,
Should offer to the deities.
Then, further, great being,
Listen to the rite of the food offerings.
Milk porridge, rice mixed with curd,
Radishes and pale cakes,
Pleasing gorbö and sugar,
Fine sweets of khaṇḍa sugar,
Pūrikā and kārikā,
Likewise mathuśira,
Phena, aśokavarti,
And parpaṭa should be offered too.
Foods such as these,
And fruits of various kinds,
Hvak and lumps of sugar candy,
Molasses, honey, and butter,
Drinks of various kinds,
And curd and milk should be offered in abundance.
Lamps of various kinds,
Filled to the brim
With clean and most excellent oil,
Richly fragrant, should be offered.
In all directions, parasols and banners
Of various colors should be raised high.
Fine porticoes, too,
Should be offered, hung with bells.
Or else, offerings of the mind,
All of them, should be diligently produced and made.
Then, taking eight vases,
Or sixteen in full,
Pour into them medicines and every kind of jewel,
And fill them with scented water.
Adorn their mouths with sprigs of plants,
And fill their mouths with fruits as well.
Adorned with flowers, incense, and the like,
Well protected,
Their necks tied with cloth,
The learned one should arrange them.
To each of the chief deities
A pair of cloths should be offered;
To the other great beings,
The offering of a single one each is proclaimed.
Having made the offerings in this way,
Summon the disciple before the maṇḍala;
The mantra practitioner should sprinkle him with water,
And give him scent and flowers.
Having had him recollect the tathāgatas,
Have him take up the mind of awakening.
Thereby all of them
Will be born into the virtuous family of the victors.
Bind on them the mudrās
That arise from the dharmadhātu;
Having bound the Dharma wheel as well,
Bless them with Vajrasattva.
Then the one disciplined in vows binds them
With the samaya of all the buddhas.
Then, according to the rite of the mantra,
He should consecrate the cloth three times,
And with a mind filled with compassion
Cover their faces.
Having recited the samaya three times,
On the crown of the head the syllable ra,
With the anusvāra, majestic,
Radiating a garland of flames,
White in color
Like the rising moon, should be placed.
Then, before all the protectors,
Have him cast a flower;
On whichever deity the flower falls,
That one the mantra practitioner should give to him.
At the gate of the first maṇḍala,
In the central part of the gateway enclosure,
Between the two gates of the maṇḍala,
The disciple should be stationed.
For him, abiding there, all the rites
Should be performed well, in proper order.
Having thus properly led in
All the disciples, free of evil,
In order to pacify them,
Perform a fire offering according to the rite.
In front of the maṇḍala of the heart,
Behind the second maṇḍala,
In the central part of the maṇḍala,
One should do it with undistracted mind.
The hearth is proclaimed to be
One cubit in measure;
The rim should be made four finger-widths,
And in the center one should visualize a vajra.
Then, on the mantra practitioner's right side,
The requisites for the fire offering should be placed;
On the left side the disciples
Should be made to sit squatting.
The one disciplined in vows himself sits on the ground,
On a seat spread with kuśa grass.
Or else, taking the finest red pigment,
Of the most excellent kind,
With it the mantra practitioner should draw
