All the places of the fire in triangular form.
With kuśa grass, beginning from the right,
Strewing it all around,
It should be strewn so as to encircle it many times,
And sprinkled with scented water.
Then, imagining Agni, who benefits beings,
Within the hearth,
The one of excellent discipline should give
The supreme ladle offerings with this secret mantra:
namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ agnaye svāhā
Then, for each disciple in turn,
The learned one, with his left hand
Holding the disciple's right thumb,
Should perform the fire offering in equipoise.
With mind settled in equipoise,
And with each burnt offering,
Reciting the secret mantra according to the rite,
He should perform twenty-one fire offerings:
namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ oṃ mahāśāntagata śāntikara praśama dharmma nirajāta abhāva svabhāva dharmma samatā prāpte svāhā
After the fire offering, the mantra practitioner
Should request a fee from the disciples:

Gold and silver and jewels,
Horses and likewise elephants,
Camels, cattle, buffalo, and cloth,
Or any other goods whatsoever.
Then the disciple, respectfully,
Fully endowed with faith,
Having brought forth joy in his mind,
Should offer the fee to the guru.
Or else, in whatever way he can,
He should make that guru fully pleased.
Having done so, guarding himself,
The master calls the disciple and instructs him:
"This is the field of merit,"
For the sake of all sentient beings,
All the protectors have declared.
To the Saṅgha, rich in pure qualities,
All have given;
What is offered to the Saṅgha bears great fruit.
Those wise persons
Who offer gifts to the Saṅgha
Will be born to great and inexhaustible wealth,
Esteemed in the world.
Therefore, all in harmony,
Having brought forth joy in their minds,
For the Saṅgha praised by the buddhas
Should hold a feast of offerings as best they can.

Then the Blessed One Vairocana said to the bodhisattva mahāsattva Vajrapāṇi:

Great being, the empowerment
Taught by the buddhas of the past
I shall now explain in full.
With mind one-pointed, listen well.

Lord of Secrets, for this the master should leave a space of two cubits in front of the root maṇḍala and, on the ground outside it, draw a second maṇḍala, square, with a single gate. In its four corners he should place four vajra holders. Which four? They are Niṣprapañcavihārin, Gaganāmala, Vimalanetra, and Vicitrāmbara. In the center he should set a great eight-petaled lotus with its anthers. On four of the petals he should draw four bodhisattvas who are companions, endowed with the power of their former aspirations: namely, Dhāraṇīśvararāja, the One Possessed of Mindfulness and Vigilance, the One of Beneficent Superior Intention, and the Compassionate One. On the other petals he should draw four emblems. Which four? They are: Engaging in the Distinction of Mind, Fulfiller of Desires, Non-attachment, and Liberation. In the center he should place the form of the inconceivable dharmadhātu. Then he confers empowerment from the crown of the head with four jeweled vases filled with jewels and all the medicines, blessed by Samantabhadra, Maitreya, Sarvanīvaraṇaviṣkambhin, and Sarvāpāyajaha.

On that lotus petal
The mantra practitioner himself places the disciple;
He should worship him with incense and flowers
And present lamps and offering water.
With parasols, victory banners, and pennants,
With the pleasing sound of drums,
And with auspicious, vast, and melodious
Words, he should rejoice well.
In the presence of the protectors
He empowers him with full attention.
With fine incense and flowers
He should once more make offerings to him.
Then, holding a golden probe,
And seated in front of him,
He should speak these words,
Which sink into the disciple's heart:
"Just as the king of physicians of old
Removed the cataracts of the world,
So, my son, the Victors
Have removed your cataract of ignorance."
Then, taking up a mirror,
He should explain the characteristic of phenomena:
"Phenomena are like reflections,
Clear and pure, without turbidity,
Ungraspable and inexpressible,
Arisen from causes and from action,
Without essential nature, without abode."
Thus he should explain their characteristic.
"Having understood this Dharma in this way,
Work incomparably for the benefit of sentient beings,
And you will be born as a child of the buddhas."
Then, between the disciple's feet,
He should place a wheel of Dharma.
Then, in his right hand as well,
He gives a most excellent conch:
"Sounding the unsurpassed conch of Dharma
Far and wide in every direction,
From this day onward, in the world,
Turn the wheel of the protectors.
Without doubt or hesitation,
With a mind free of misgiving,
Proclaim throughout this world
The supreme way of practicing the secret mantra.
If you do so, it is called
Repaying the kindness of the buddhas.
And all those vajra holders
Will protect you in every way."
Then, raising the disciple up,
The mantra practitioner, with a mind
Become compassion, brings him inside
And should teach him the commitments:
"From this day onward, my son,
The sacred Dharma and the mind of awakening
You must never, from now on,
Give up, even for the sake of your life.
You must not be miserly,
Nor do any harm to sentient beings.
These commitments the buddhas
Have taught to you, who keep the excellent observance.
Just as you guard your own life,
So you must guard these."
The disciple, at the guru's feet,
Prostrates with faith and respect,
And with a mind of utter certainty
Should accept them all.

Then again the Lord of Secrets, Vajrapāṇi, said to the Blessed One, "Blessed One, how much merit should we understand to be amassed by a son or daughter of good family who has entered the commitment of this great king of maṇḍalas, Arising from the Essence of Great Compassion?"

When he had asked this, the Blessed One said to the Lord of Secrets, Vajrapāṇi, "However much merit, arising from the Tathāgata, is amassed from the first generation of the mind of awakening onward, the merit amassed by that son or daughter of good family should be understood to be just as much. Lord of Secrets, by this enumeration too you should regard it in this way: those sons and daughters of good family should be known to be heirs born from the speech and the heart of the Tathāgata. In whatever direction those sons and daughters of good family dwell, there the Tathāgata need no longer be greatly concerned. Therefore, Lord of Secrets, sons and daughters of good family who wish to make offerings to the Tathāgata should make offerings to them. Those who wish to see the Tathāgata should look upon them."

Then the vajra holders headed by Vajrapāṇi and the bodhisattva mahāsattvas headed by Samantabhadra spoke with one voice: "Blessed One, from this day onward we shall serve and honor those sons and daughters of good family."

"Blessed One, why is this? It is because those sons and daughters of good family are themselves to be regarded as the buddhas, the blessed ones."

Then again the Blessed One Vairocana looked upon that entire assembly and said to the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi, to those vajra holders, and to the entire assembly, "Sons of good family, there exist, arisen from the vast entry into the boundless maṇḍala of the wheel of speech that is the secret mantra words of the tathāgatas, those that are like a jewel of manifold forms, fulfilling every hope, amassing immeasurable stores of merit, abiding in unobstructed understanding, and endowed with a power unattached throughout the three times."

When he had said this, the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi, those vajra holders, and that entire assembly spoke with one voice: "Blessed One, now is the time for it. Sugata, now is the occasion for it."

Then at that time the Blessed One, by an emanation of his tongue that fulfills all desires, covered every buddha-field entirely and entered into the meditative absorption called Gazing at the Pure Summit of the Vast Crest of the Victory Banner of Dharma. The very moment the Blessed One entered into it, all the tathāgatas spoke this great queen of vidyā-mantras, the One of Great Power, the Great Protection, which pervades the dharmadhātu and sends forth a sound that fully protects the realm of sentient beings without exception:

namaḥ sarbba tathāgatebhyaḥ sarbba bhaya bigatebhyo biśva mukhebhyaḥ sarbbathā khaṃ rakṣa mahā bale sarbba tathāgata puṇya nirjāte hūṃ hūṃ trata trata apratihate svāhā

The moment all the learned buddhas
Had spoken this vidyā-mantra,
Then these buddha-fields
Shook in six ways.
All those bodhisattvas,
Their eyes opened wide in wonder,
In the presence of all the buddhas
Spoke these pleasing words:
"How wonderful! This protection of exceeding power
Has been spoken by all the buddhas
In order to guard all sentient beings,
To protect them and to shelter them.
Those in whose hearts this protection abides
Have nothing to fear.
When this secret mantra of great might is called to mind,
Obstructors and all vināyakas,
And rākṣasas of terrifying form,
All of them will be destroyed."

Then again the Blessed One, having blessed the dharmadhātu as vast, at that time entered into the meditative absorption called Possessing the Essence of the Treasury of the Dharmadhātu. The very moment the Blessed One entered into that meditative absorption, this vidyā-mantra for entering the commitment came forth:

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ asame trisame samaye svāhā

Then, immediately, throughout all the buddha-fields and all the maṇḍalas of the bodhisattva assemblies, there resounded the sound of the vidyā-mantra for entering the commitment, on hearing which the wise will not transgress any of the teachings.

Then again the Blessed One spoke the secret mantras arising from the dharmadhātu:

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ dharmma dhātu svabhāva ātmako'haṃ
This is for blessing oneself as the dharmadhātu.

namaḥ smanta vajrāṇāṃ vajra ātmako'haṃ
This is for blessing oneself as Vajrasattva.

namaḥ smanta vajrāṇāṃ vajra kavaca hūṃ
This is the vajra armor.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ tathāgata cakṣur byavalokāya svāhā
This is the eye of the Tathāgata.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ biśuddha gandhoddhaba svāhā
This is the mantra of the scented ointment.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ mahā maitrya bhyudgata svāhā
This is the mantra of the flowers.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ dharmma dhātvanugata svāhā
This is the mantra of the incense.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ sarbba tathāgata raci spharaṇa ababhāsa gaganottarebhyaḥ svāhā
This is the mantra of the lamp.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ gagana sama sama svāhā
This is the mantra of the offering water.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ gagana nānda spharaṇa biśuddha dharmma nirjata svāhā
This is the mantra of the uṣṇīṣa.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ pracaṇḍa vajra jvala bisphura hūṃ
This is the armor of the Tathāgata.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ jvāla mālin tathāgata raci svāhā
This is the All-Blazing.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ mahā tathāgata jihvasya dharmma pratiṣṭhita svāhā
This is the mantra of the tongue of the Tathāgata.

This is the treasury of mantras to be set out in the maṇḍala.

Fascicle three.

Then again Vajrapāṇi asked the Blessed One Vairocana, "Blessed One, how is it

That when the maṇḍala is drawn,
The obstructors will be pacified?
And when the secret mantra is practiced,
That the vināyakas will be absent?
How are the secret mantras to be accomplished?
And what kind of fruit will they bear?"
When he had asked this, at that time
The Blessed One, the great Vairocana,
Spoke these words:

"Excellent, excellent, great being!
All that you have asked
I shall teach to you.
Because of miserliness indulged in the past,
Obstructors arise from one's own mind.
In order to destroy their cause,
That in which all the conceptions
Of mind and mental factors are abandoned,
And in which all the elaborations
Arising from conceptual thought come to cessation,
Is what is called the mind of awakening.
The mantra practitioner, recalling the mind of awakening,
Recites Acala inwardly;
If he binds Acala's mudrā,
All the obstructors will be destroyed."

Lord of Secrets, now listen to this binding of the wind:

"Transforming oneself into the syllable a,
One recites the syllable ha inwardly.
Stationed in the quarter of the wind,
One should neatly make on the ground
Exactly seven dots of incense
And cover them with an earthen bowl.
One should imagine the bowl, again and again,
As the great Mount Meru.
Above it one should visualize the syllable a,
Together with its anusvāra, spreading wide.
Such a great binding of the wind
Was taught by the victors of the past.
Great being, now listen to that of water too,
With your mind gathered to a single point.
The mantra practitioner should imagine the syllable ra
As wholly red and of great power,
Garlanded with flames, unbearable,
A wrathful one holding a sword in his hand.
Having drawn the form of a cloud on the ground,
He should stamp it with the sword mudrā.
The clouds that have risen from every direction
Will at once be destroyed.
Or else the mantra practitioner, undistracted,
Should strike them with pegs:
The learned one, having become a vajra,
Strikes them all with vajra pegs.
Or again, the pacifying
Of all the obstructors is taught as well.
The mantra practitioner, stationed at the center of the maṇḍala,
Should imagine the secret mantra Acala, of great power,
Utterly unbearable,
Abiding in the maṇḍalas of secret mantra.
Having made an effigy of the obstructor,
He presses the crown of its head with his left foot.
Then it will either be pacified,
Or without doubt it will die.
Having made an effigy of it,
And smeared it with mustard, poison, and blood mixed together,
The mantra practitioner dries it,
And that graha will swiftly burn.
If it is dried over a fire
And smeared with poison, then certainly
Even Brahmā, or even Śakra (Indra),
Would swiftly burn on the spot."

Then Vajrapāṇi said to the Blessed One, "As I understand the meaning of what the Blessed One has said, I see it in this way. Blessed One, when the deities abide in the places of their own maṇḍala, they confer blessing. Abiding just there, they do not transgress the command of the Tathāgata. Why is that? Blessed One, abiding in this way in one's own family is the commitment of all the secret mantras. Bodhisattvas who practice the conduct of a bodhisattva by way of secret mantra, too, accomplish all aims while abiding in their own place."

The Blessed One said, "Lord of Secrets, so it is. It is just as you have said. Lord of Secrets, furthermore:

Whatever color has been taught for any deity,
That itself is its maṇḍala;
Such are the forms of the deities
Taught by the buddhas of the past.

Lord of Secrets, in time to come there will appear sentient beings of feeble intellect and without faith, who will not have faith in this teaching, who are full of doubt and hesitation, who take mere learning as the essence, and who are not inclined toward practice. They are not only ruined themselves but also bring others to ruin. They will say, 'This is found among the non-Buddhists (tīrthikas); it was not taught by the buddhas.' But those foolish people do not know that the Blessed One, the omniscient, who has gained mastery over all phenomena and has fully understood the aims of sentient beings, taught all of these in the past out of the wish to benefit sentient beings.

The Dharma-mark of phenomena,
Those fools do not know it.
The characteristic of all phenomena
Has been truly declared to be emptiness.
The mantra practitioner, ever abiding in that,
Should perform the rites with complete certainty."

This concludes the detailed exposition of the stages for pacifying obstructors.

Then the vajra holders headed by Vajrapāṇi and the bodhisattvas headed by Samantabhadra prostrated to the Blessed One Vairocana and, wishing to speak secret mantras in words that would express, each in his own words, how they had realized the gateway of the pure dharmadhātu here in this great maṇḍala Arising from Great Compassion, they made a request to the Blessed One.

Then the Blessed One Vairocana blessed those bodhisattvas and those vajra holders into the unfailing nature of reality and said, "Sons of good family, speak the secret mantra words that purify the realm of sentient beings, in accordance with how you have realized the dharmadhātu."

Then, at that time, the bodhisattva Samantabhadra entered into the meditative absorption called the Domain of the Buddha's Adornment and spoke the secret mantra of unobstructed power:

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ samantānugati baraja dharmma nirjāta mahā mahā svāhā
This is Samantabhadra's.

Then the bodhisattva Maitreya entered into the meditative absorption called the Universal Arising of Great Loving-kindness and spoke his own heart mantra:

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ ajatanjayā sarbba satva āśayānugata svāhā
This is Maitreya's.

Then again the bodhisattva Ākāśagarbha entered into the meditative absorption called the Pure Domain and spoke the secret mantra:

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ ākāśa samatānugata bicitraṃ bara dhara svāhā
This is Ākāśagarbha's.

Then the bodhisattva Sarvanīvaraṇaviṣkambhin entered into the meditative absorption called the Fortitude of Great Compassion and spoke the secret mantra:

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ āsatāhṛta aṅbhudgata trāṃ trāṃ rāṃ rāṃ svāhā
This is Sarvanīvaraṇaviṣkambhin's.

Then the bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara entered into the meditative absorption called Looking Everywhere and spoke his own heart mantra, together with those of his retinue:

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ sarbba tathāgata abalokita kāruṇā māyā ra ra ra hūṃ jaḥ svāhā
This is Avalokiteśvara's.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ jaṃ jaṃ sa svāhā
This is Mahāsthāmaprāpta's.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ kāruṇoṅbhabe tāre tāriṇi svāhā
This is the goddess Tārā's.

namaḥ samanta buddhānāṃ sarbba bhaya trāsani hūṃ sphoṭaya svāhā
This is Bhṛkuṭī's.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ tathāgata biśaya saṃbhabe padma mālini svāhā
This is Pāṇḍaravāsinī's.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ hūṃ khada bandha sphoṭaya svāhā
This is Hayagrīva's.

Then the bodhisattva Kṣitigarbha entered into the meditative absorption called the Domain of Indestructible Vajra Discernment and spoke the secret mantra:

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ ha ha ha sutanu svāhā
This is Kṣitigarbha's.

Then the bodhisattva Mañjuśrī Kumārabhūta entered into the meditative absorption called the Miraculous Display of the Buddha's Blessing and spoke his own heart mantra:

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ he he he kumāraka bimukti sathasthiti smara smara pratitana svāhā
This is Mañjuśrī's.

Then the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi entered into the meditative absorption called the Invincible and spoke his own heart mantra, together with those of his retinue:

namaḥ smanta vajrāṇāṃ caṇḍa mahā roṣaṇa hūṃ
This is Vajrapāṇi's.

namaḥ smanta vajrāṇāṃ triṭa triṭa jayanti svāhā
This is Māmakī's.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ hūṃ bandhaya bandhaya moṭaya moṭaya vajroṅbhabe sarbbatra apratihate svāhā
This is the Vajra Chain's.

namaḥ smanta vajrāṇāṃ hrīḥ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā
This is the Chief of the Wrathful Moon's.

namaḥ smanta vajrāṇāṃ sarbba dharmma nirbhedni vajra suci barade svāhā
This is the Vajra Point's.

namaḥ smanta vajrāṇāṃ hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ jaṃ jaṃ svāhā
This is that of all the vajra holders.

namaḥ smanta vajrāṇāṃ he he kiñcirāyasi gṛhṇa gṛhṇa khāda khāda paripūraya sapratijñāna svāhā
This is that of all the attendants.

Then, at that time, the Blessed One Śākyamuni entered into the meditative absorption called the Source of Jewels and spoke his own heart mantra, together with those of his retinue:

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ sarbba kleśa niśuddhāna sarbba dharmma baśita prāpta gagana sama sama svāhā
This is Śākyamuni's.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ barade bara prāpte hūṃ
This is that of the ūrṇā.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ baṃ baṃ hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā
This is that of all the uṣṇīṣas.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ dhriṃ dhriṃ riṃ riṃ jri jri svāhā
This is that of the wrathful one called Aparājita.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ aparājita jālanati jhaṭita svāhā
This is Aparājita's.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ prithibyai svāhā
This is the Earth's.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ biṣṇabe svāhā
This is Viṣṇu's.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ rudrāya svāhā
This is Rudra's.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ bāyabe svāhā
This is Vāyu's.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ sarasvatyai svāhā
This is Sarasvatī's.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ rākṣasādhipataye svāhā
This is Nairṛti's.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ baibasatvāya svāhā
This is Yama's.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ mṛtaye svāhā
This is Mṛtyu's, the lord of death.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ kālarātriye svāhā
This is Kālarātri's.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ matribhyaḥ svāhā
This is that of the mātṛkās.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ śakrāya svāhā
This is Śakra's.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ apāṃpataye svāhā
This is Varuṇa's.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ ādityāya svāhā
This is the Sun's.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ prajapataye svāhā
This is Brahmā's.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ candrāya svāhā
This is the Moon's.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ mebhaśaniye svāhā
This is that of Nanda and Upananda.

Then again the Blessed One Vairocana, wishing his teaching to be accomplished as meaningful, spoke the queen of vidyā-mantras, the mother of all the buddhas and bodhisattvas:

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ gagana bara lakṣaṇe gagana sama sarbbodagata tisara saṃbhabe jvala namo amoghānān svāhā
This is that of Gaganalocanā, the mother of all the buddhas and bodhisattvas.

Then again the Blessed One Vairocana, in order to pacify all obstructors, entered into the meditative absorption called the Source of Splendor and spoke these secret mantras that crush the great obstructors:

namaḥ smanta vajrāṇāṃ caṇḍa mahā roṣaṇa sphoṭaya hūṃ trag ha mā
This is the noble Acala's.

namaḥ smanta vajrāṇāṃ ha ha ha bismaye sarbba tathāgana biṣaya saṃbhaya trailokya bijaya hūṃ ja svāhā
This is Trailokyavijaya's.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ hetu pratyaya bigate dharmma nirājata hūṃ
This is that of the śrāvakas.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ tha
This is that of the pratyekabuddhas.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ sarbba buddha bodhisatva hṛdayaṃ nibeśani namaḥ sarbba bidyā svāhā
This is the common heart mantra of all the buddhas and bodhisattvas.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ loka loka karāya sarbba deba nāga yakṣa gandharbā asura garuḍa kinra mahoraga hṛdayan nyakariṣaya bicitra gati svāhā
This is the common heart mantra of the gods and the others.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ sarbathā bīmati bikiraṇa dharmma dhātu nirjāta saṃ saṃ ha svāhā
This is that of all the bodhisattvas.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ dundānta mahā roṣaṇa khāṭaya sarbbān tathāgata ājñāṃ kuru svāhā
This is that of the gatekeeper called Durdānta.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ he mahā pracanḍa abhimukha grihṇa khādaya kiñcirāyasi samaya manusmara svāhā
This is that of the gatekeeper Abhimukha.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ sarbbatra anugate bandha sīmāṃ mahā samaya nirjāte smaraṇe apratihate dhaka dhaka cara cara bhandha bhandha daśa diśan sarbba tathāgata anugate prabara dharmma labṅha bijaye bhagabati bikuri bikule lelu bupri svāhā
This is that of sealing the great boundary.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ a
This is that of awakening.
ā
This is that of conduct.
oṃ
This is that of perfect buddhahood.
a
This is that of nirvāṇa.

namaḥ samanta vajrāṇāṃ hā
This is Trailokyavijaya's.

namaḥ smanta vajrāṇāṃ hāṃ
This is that of the noble protector Acala.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ a
This is Sarvanīvaraṇaviṣkambhin's.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ sa
This is Avalokiteśvara's.

namaḥ samanta buddhānāṃ
This is Vajrapāṇi's.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ manomā
This is Mañjuśrī's.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ ga
This is Gaganalocanā's.

namaḥ samanta buddhānāṃ raṃ
This is that of the dharmadhātu.

namaḥ samanta buddhānāṃ khāṃ
This is that of the Great Hero.

namaḥ samanta buddhānāṃ jaṃ
This is that of the Lord of Waters.

namaḥ samanta buddhānāṃ taṃ
This is the goddess Tārā's.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ
This is Bhṛkuṭī's.

namaḥ samanta buddhānāṃ paṃ

namaḥ samanta buddhānāṃ saṃ
This is Mahāsthāmaprāpta's.

namaḥ samanta buddhānāṃ haṃ

namaḥ samanta buddhānāṃ yaṃ
This is Yaśodharā's.

namaḥ samanta buddhānāṃ saṃ

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ
This is Jālinīprabha's.

namaḥ samanta buddhānāṃ bha
This is Śākyamuni's.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ hāṃ nyaṃ ruṃ
This is that of the three uṣṇīṣas.

namaḥ samanta buddhānāṃ laṃ
This is that of the White Parasol.

namaḥ samanta buddhānāṃ śaṃ
This is Jayoṣṇīṣa's.

namaḥ samanta buddhānāṃ saṃ

namaḥ samanta buddhānāṃ triṃ

This is Vikiraṇa's.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ hūṃ

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ
This is the Earth's.

namaḥ samanta buddhānāṃ kili
This is Keśinī's.

namaḥ samanta buddhānāṃ dili
This is Upakeśinī's.

namaḥ samanta buddhānāṃ mili
This is Citrā's.

namaḥ samanta buddhānāṃ hili
This is Vasumatī's.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ hasanāṃ
This is that of the Wondrous One.

namaḥ samanta buddhānāṃ rasanāṃ
This is that of the Giver of Fearlessness to All Beings.

namaḥ samanta buddhānāṃ darśanāṃ
This is that of the Purifier of All Lower Realms.

namaḥ samanta buddhānāṃ bihasanāṃ
This is Paritrāṇāśayamati's.

namaḥ samanta buddhānāṃ ṭhaṃ
This is that of the Manifest Arising of Great Loving-kindness.

namaḥ samanta buddhānāṃ yaṃ
This is that of the One Tormented by Compassion.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ
This is that of the Pacifier of All Torment.

namaḥ samanta buddhānāṃ

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ traṃ

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ ṣaṃ

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ ṭaṃ

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ aṃ

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ ḍhaṃ

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ ṅhriṃ śriṃ haṃ braṃ
This is that of the attendants.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ kṣa da ha ra yaṃ kaṃ
This is that of the bodhisattvas as they have been described.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ mano māye dharmma saṃbhabe bibhabe kaṭhane saṃsane
This is that of the gods of the Pure Abodes.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ kraṃ keri
This is that of the rākṣasas.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ hrīḥ h
This is that of the ḍākinīs.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ yakṣa bīrya dhari
This is that of the yakṣas.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ mici mici
This is that of the piśācīs.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ guṃ guṃ iṃ iṃ
This is that of the bhūtas.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ raṭaṃ raṭaṃ braṃṭaṃ braṃṭaṃ
This is that of the asuras.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ garalaṃ biralaṃ
This is that of the mahoragas.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ hasanāṃ bīasanāṃ
This is that of the kinnaras.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ aticalārana manomai
This is that of humans.

"Lord of Secrets, I have explained the secret mantras of all these and the others. Now, among them, listen with a tranquil mind to the heart of all the secret mantras:

The syllable a is truly declared
To be the heart of all mantras.
In it all the secret mantras abide;
Thus are the mantras ascertained."

From The Manifest and Complete Awakening of the Great Vairocana, Its Magical Display and Blessing, this concludes the detailed exposition of the treasury of common secret mantras.

Then again the Blessed One said to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi, "Lord of Secrets, listen to how secret mantras are accomplished and what the fruit of accomplishing them is:

Syllable joined with syllable,
And likewise basis turned into basis,
With strict restraint, inwardly,
One should perform a hundred thousand recitations."

"The syllable is the mind of awakening;
The second is what is called sound.
The basis is the placing of one's own deity;
This is done at the site of one's own body.
The second basis should be known
As the perfect Buddha, supreme among two-legged beings.
The mantrin should imagine him abiding
Upon a perfectly pure disk of the moon.
In its center one should set out well
The syllables in their proper sequence.
Then press them down with the words of drawing back in,
And thoroughly purify life-force and exertion (prāṇāyāma).
What is called life-force is explained as wind;
Exertion is what is called mindfulness.
Having restrained these two,
One should first carry out the approach well.
Then for one month the mantrin,
Under strict restraint, should perform the recitation.
The preliminary practice of secret mantra
Proceeds from the one basis into the other.
All the buddhas of great renown
Have explained this as the preliminary practice.
From then on, offering whatever little one has,
Flowers, incense, and so forth,
In order to attain buddhahood itself,
One should at that time also dedicate it to awakening.
The mantrin, fearless in this way,
Should perform the recitation in the second month.
Then, at the time of the full moon,
That person should enter into accomplishment.
On a mountain peak, in a cowshed,
Likewise at the confluence of rivers,
At a crossroads, at a shrine of a single liṅga,
At a shrine of the mātṛkās, or at a peaceful place,
One should carefully draw the maṇḍala
And make it entirely a vajra enclosure.
Having then guarded the site there,
One should apply oneself diligently to accomplishment.
There, at midnight,
Or else at the time the Sun rises,
Portents such as these will occur,
And one endowed with intelligence should recognize them:
The sound hūṃ, the sound of drums,
Likewise the quaking of the Earth,
Or else pleasing words;
If these resound from the sky,
By those signs one should know
That success has followed according to one's wish.
The buddhas, supreme among two-legged beings,
Have rightly declared this to be its fruit.
One who abides thus in the way of mantra
Will certainly become a buddha.
Therefore, in every way,
One should always keep the mantra itself in mind.
The buddhas of the past, of great power,
Have also taught that it is to be kept in mind."

From The Manifest and Complete Awakening of the Great Vairocana, Its Magical Display and Blessing, this concludes the chapter on the stages of accomplishing worldly attainments.

Then again the Blessed One Vairocana, looking upon the entire assembled retinue, spoke these words of Dharma, which fulfill all hopes and bring to perfection the wisdom that ascertains the gateway of the boundless three times:

"Stainless like space, devoid of essential nature,
It bestows wisdom of manifold kinds in many ways.
By its essential nature it is ever emptiness;
Dependent on conditions, it is profound and hard to see.
Above all, to beings it constantly
Grants the supreme fruit they have conceived.
Just as space, a sphere of activity free of attachment,
Has become the abode of all beings,
So this perfectly pure nature of reality
Brings forth the prosperity of all three realms of existence without exception.
Any wise one of supreme birth who practices this
Will come to share one sphere of activity with all the Victors.
The nature of reality, just as the Victors have taught it,
Is utterly pure and illuminates all;
There is no other state harder to find than that.
This vast Dharma is profound and never diminishes;
Unequaled, it benefits the world:
Practice it, abandoning conceptual thought entirely."

Then, having spoken those verses, the great Blessed One Vairocana looked upon the entire assembled retinue and said to Vajrapāṇi and the other vajra holders, "Sons of good family, teach the words for accomplishing attainments, which are the power and blessing of the realm of reality of each of you, by which beings, in this very life, having found joy and contentment, will come to experience bliss."

When he had said this, the vajra holders bowed to the Blessed One Vairocana and said, "We would follow your Dharma command just as you have given it. Yet we make this request of the Blessed One himself: out of love for us, may the Blessed One himself teach the words for accomplishing attainments. Why is that? Blessed One, for us to explain in the presence of the Blessed One what we ourselves have realized is exceedingly difficult. That being so, for the sake of the welfare, benefit, and happiness of beings to come, may the Blessed One himself please explain it well and fully."

When they had made this request, the Blessed One Vairocana spoke thus to all those vajra holders: "Excellent, excellent, sons of good family! In this Dharma and Vinaya taught by the Tathāgata there is one quality of great benefit, namely, a sense of shame. For a son of good family or a daughter of good family who possesses a sense of shame, two things come about very swiftly in this very life. What are the two? Not doing deeds that should not be done, and being praised by the holy ones. There are two more: understanding what had not been understood, and attaining the company of the buddhas and bodhisattvas. There are two more: becoming firmly established in moral discipline, and attaining birth among gods and humans. Therefore, keep this well in mind. I shall now explain the words of application for accomplishment by which bodhisattvas who practice the conduct of a bodhisattva by way of secret mantra will swiftly attain the accomplishment of secret mantra from the secret mantras: the words of application that bring the practice of secret mantra to success."

"In this, first of all, one must have seen the maṇḍala, have received the guru's permission to practice secret mantra, have generated the mind set on awakening, be endowed with faith and free of avarice, be compassionate and disciplined, be skilled in analyzing dependent origination, have taken up the vows, and abide in one's own training.
Skilled in means and very steadfast,
One knows what is timely and what is untimely;
Fearless, learned, and generous,
Patient, with a mind of loving-kindness,
Thoroughly diligent in the conduct of secret mantra,
Skilled in the meaning of the reality of secret mantra,
And delighting in the practice of meditative concentration:
Such a practitioner is auspicious for accomplishment.

"Lord of Secrets, the lord of the desire realm has a vidyā-mantra called Intoxicating. With it he makes all the gods who dwell in the desire realm faint with intoxication. He also displays delightful places filled with many and various flowers, and he magically emanates enjoyments and pleasures of every different kind, which he gives also to the gods of the heaven Controlling Others' Emanations, and enjoys himself as well.

"Likewise, the god Maheśvara (Śiva) has a vidyā-mantra called Swift as Thought. With it he accomplishes the needs of beings in the great trichiliocosm world system, and he magically emanates all kinds of pleasures and enjoyments, which he gives also to the gods of the realm of the Pure Abodes, and enjoys himself as well.

"Likewise, for example, a magician by means of mantras displays pleasure groves, lakes, rivers, people, and things of every different kind. Likewise, for example, the asuras display the illusions of secret mantra. Likewise, for example, through secret mantras poison, fever, and the like are made to cease. Likewise, the goddesses called mātṛkās send contagious disease upon beings by means of secret mantra. Likewise, for example, by means of secret mantra the heat of fire is removed and it is made cool. Sons of good family, through these words of example you should come to have faith in the power of secret mantra.

"What, then, is this power of secret mantra? It does not arise from the secret mantras, it does not come in from substances, and it is not the performer's focusing on the act. Nevertheless, sons of good family, it does not go beyond the arising of the nature of reality that is the blessing of secret mantra, because it transcends the three times, and because it comes about from profound and inconceivable dependent origination. Therefore, sons of good family, having understood the realization of the inconceivable essential nature through the nature of reality, you should always, without interruption, follow the way of secret mantra."

Then again the Blessed One entered the meditative absorption called Possessing the Essence of the Pure Adornment of the Inconceivable Abode of the Tathāgata, which blesses the basis of the powers that are unattached in the three times. As soon as the Blessed One had entered that absorption, from the sounds that make known the entire realm of reality, as much as there is, there came forth the power of the abode of the realm of reality, the power equal to the unequaled, arisen from the resolve of the perfectly complete Buddha, arisen from the single tone of the four states, pervading the entire realm of reality and become equal to space:
namaḥ sarbba tathāgatebhyo biśva mukhebhyaḥ sarbbathā a ā aṃ aḥ
As soon as these essences of the perfectly complete Buddha had come forth, then again, from the gateways that proclaim the entire realm of reality, the sounds of the tones of knowledge, marked with the words of the essence of the perfectly complete Buddha, resounded one after another.

The bodhisattvas, hearing this,
Opened eyes filled with wonder,
And to the Omniscient One, free of all ills,
They addressed these sweet words:
"How wonderful! Through the practice of secret mantra
The buddhas, supreme among two-legged beings,
Have brought forth the great and vast wisdom
By which all this is pervaded.
Therefore, in every way,
With an inner self made wholly pure,
One should always strive earnestly
In the essence praised by the buddhas."

Next comes the accomplishment of the essences of the perfectly complete Buddha. In a pleasure garden, in a temple, in a cave, or wherever the mind finds solitude, one should cultivate the awakening mind and remain there until the signs appear. Then, taking up any one of those essences, one should accomplish the basis until one is free of doubt. Then, placing the essence at the heart, one should accomplish the mind: gazing upon it as utterly pure, stainless, firm, completely unmoving, nonconceptual, like a mirror, and exceedingly subtle, one should continue with the practice of constant familiarization until one sees one's own body as the body of one's own deity.

"The second basis is the perfectly complete Buddha. Within that very mirror disk he is seated upon a great king of lotuses, absorbed in a meditative absorption as if dwelling in a cave, encircled by his topknot, his crown ornament, and infinite rays of light, free of all thought and conceptualization, peaceful from the very beginning like space. One should imagine the sound abiding in him, and recite in meditative equipoise.
Then the preliminary approach
Is said to be done a hundred thousand times.
Then in the second month
One should offer flowers, scents, and so forth,
And bring about benefit of many kinds
For sentient beings;
Completely setting aside gain and loss,
One should recite in a further month.
There, at that time, this application
Should be contemplated in meditative equipoise:
'By the power of my merit
May all sentient beings be happy!
May all of them be free of illness!
Or else may they have the perfection of desires
That the Tathāgatas have praised!
Or else may all sentient beings
Have all their hopes completely fulfilled,
Accomplished without hindrance,
In a manner free of obscuration!
May the suffering of animals
Devouring one another never arise!
May food, drink, and the like
Come to the hungry ghosts without hindrance!
Whatever sufferings the beings in hell endure,
Unbearable and of every kind,
By the power of my merit
May they swiftly be pacified!'
Generating great compassion
For all suffering beings,
One should contemplate again and again
That accomplishment and others as well.
In order to accomplish things as one has contemplated them,
With the three places of blessing
One should bless all of these,
And recite the mantra mentally:
'Through the power of my merit,
The power of the Tathāgatas' blessing,
And the power of the realm of reality,
By benefiting sentient beings,
Whatever aims have been contemplated,
Here, in the realm of beings without exception,
May they all, each as is fitting,
Come about without hindrance!'"

Then at that time the Blessed One spoke the queen of vidyā-mantras, whose power is like space, called the Arising of the Sky Treasury:
namaḥ sarbba tathāgatebhyo biśva mukhebhyaḥ sarba tad khaṃ uṭgate spharaṇa imaṃ gagana khaṃ svāhā
"If one recites this three times, all aims, just as one has contemplated them, will come about without hindrance.
Then, at the time of the full moon,
That person should enter into accomplishment.
On a mountain peak, in a cowshed,
At a river confluence, or in a charnel ground,
At a crossroads, or at the foot of a solitary tree,
At a shrine of the mātṛkās, or in a peaceful place,
Listen with a gentle mind to the maṇḍala
That makes all obstructors swoon.
Square on every side,
With a single door and with a border,
All in the color yellow:
Having drawn this supreme vajra maṇḍala,
In the manner of a chain of vajras
The vajras should be linked one to another.
At the door draw two door guardians,
Hard to Tame and Abhimukha,
With red eyes and wrathful,
Drawn angry and menacing.
In the corners tridents
Should be drawn with care.
In the center draw a lovely vajra seat
Facing in every direction,
And upon it a great lotus
With eight petals and with anthers.
Having paid homage to all the Victors
And made aspirations,
Binding the vajra mudrā
Of the wise Vajrapāṇi,
Having set up protection there, one should sit there,
And, taking up the purified substance,
With an inner self made wholly pure,
One should recite throughout an entire night.
There, at midnight,
Or else at the time the Sun rises,
The substance becomes transformed,
Like the splendor of a garland of flames.
If the mantrin himself then takes it up,
He will travel through the sky.
He lives for an eon, of great splendor;
That heir of the Victors dies when he wishes,
And goes to other world systems too,
A lord who takes on various forms.
Through accomplishing the mantra that arises from the substance
He makes glorious offerings.
This is declared to be the accomplishment
Of substance that does not decline.

"Lord of Secrets, taking this procedure as their reason, the Tathāgatas, the arhats, the perfectly complete buddhas dwelling in all world systems, who have realized and understood the perfection of skillful means, though they know that very procedure to be empty of intrinsic nature, through the power of the perfection of skillful means designate the unconditioned by designations. And so that beings may dwell happily in this very life by way of transformation, they reveal with certainty to beings the great power and blessing of the realm of reality. Through this, those beings become delighted in mind and also attain long life; they play and enjoy themselves with the five qualities of sense pleasure, make offerings to the buddhas, the blessed ones, and accomplish this path, which runs counter to the whole world.

"The Tathāgatas and bodhisattvas, too, seeing this purpose, explain as they please the rite of the way of practicing secret mantra. Why is that? Because what is not attained even by striving over many eons and making the effort to undertake austerities, that is attained in this single life by bodhisattvas who practice the bodhisattva conduct by way of secret mantra.

"Lord of Secrets, further, if a bodhisattva who practices the bodhisattva conduct by way of secret mantra takes a tathāgata's banner, a sword, a parasol, sandals, a wish-fulfilling jewel, eye ointment, or bezoar, performs three hundred thousand recitations, and accomplishes it in that way, it will be accomplished accordingly.

"Lord of Secrets, further, if a son of good family or a daughter of good family who is endowed with skillful means accomplishes the things the mind desires, they will be fully accomplished solely through the mere power of their own mind.

"Lord of Secrets, those foolish people who expect a fruit from a cause, Lord of Secrets, do not truly know the defining characteristic of secret mantra in the secret mantras."

"Why is that?
The cause is not said to be an agent,
Nor does the fruit exist within it.
When a cause is empty of being a cause,
How could it ever become a cause?
Therefore the fruit of secret mantra
Should be known as free of cause and of action.
When one truly touches
The meditative absorption of signlessness,
Then the mantrin obtains
Accomplishment that truly arises from the mind."

Then again Vajrapāṇi said to the Blessed One, "Blessed One, please explain also the words for accomplishing as attainments the bases of the perfectly complete Buddha, through which sons of good family and daughters of good family may delight in this very life and abide experiencing bliss, without contradicting the realm of reality. Why is that, Blessed One? The realm of reality is an inconceivable realm, and this too has been taught by the Tathāgatas, the arhats, the perfectly complete buddhas. Blessed One, that being so, since bodhisattvas practice the bodhisattva conduct by way of secret mantra, they need to come to understand the realization of the indivisible realm of reality."

When he had made this request, the Blessed One said to Vajrapāṇi, "Excellent, excellent, Lord of Secrets! Lord of Secrets, that you think to ask the Tathāgata about this matter is excellent as well. Therefore listen, and keep it well in mind, and I shall explain it to you."

Saying, "Excellent, Blessed One," the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi listened to the Blessed One, and the Blessed One said this to him: "Lord of Secrets, here there is the accomplishment called A. In a temple, in a cave, or in any place at all, one should place the A on all one's limbs and perform three hundred thousand recitations. Then, at the time of the full moon, having made offerings as far as one's means allow, one should recite until the bodhisattva Samantabhadra, or Mañjuśrī, or any one of the great vajra holders comes and strokes one's head or says, 'Excellent!' Then, if one bows and presents a guest offering, one will attain the meditative absorption of not forgetting the awakening mind.

"Likewise, if one cultivates lightness of mind and lightness of body and recites, one's body will become pure and one's mind will become pure. If one sets out the A and recites, one's ears will become pure. If one wishes to hold on to life, one should join the A with the breath of life and contemplate it at the three times of day; one will then live long. If one places the anusvāra dot upon that same A, it draws out all poisons, both stationary and moving.

"Then, if one wishes to bring a person of splendor under one's control:
Make the syllable A into oneself,
And likewise from PA make the lord of men;
On the one place a lotus,
And on the other place a conch;
If they look each other in the face,
At that very moment he comes under control."

Then the Blessed One Vairocana, looking upon the entire assembled retinue, said to the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi, "Vajrapāṇi, there is a teaching called Arisen from the Heart of the Tathāgatas: the extensive chapter on the music of conduct that plays through acts and activities, gathered into the complete gathering of the four elements, abiding in the king of mind, all-pervading like space, abounding in seen and unseen fruits, directly evident to all śrāvakas, pratyekabuddhas, and bodhisattvas, fulfilling all the hopes of those who practice the bodhisattva conduct by way of secret mantra, endowed with various activities, and beneficial to all sentient beings. Listen to it, keep it well in mind, and I shall explain it to you.

"Vajrapāṇi, what then is this music of conduct, abounding in fruits that bring acts and activities to accomplishment, and directly evident to all embodied beings?
One who knows the rite should first
Carry out the reality of the self in sequence.
The mantrin, seated as before,
Having called the Tathāgata to mind,
Should make himself the syllable A,
With the anusvāra, and expanded,
All a deep and lovely yellow,
Marked with a square and with a vajra.
Abiding there, the mantrin should fully
Imagine the lord of all buddhas.
This, all the buddhas have declared,
Is the reality of the self.
When the mantrin is free of doubt
About the reality of the self,
Then he becomes one who brings
Benefit to the whole world.
Endowed with wonders of every kind,
He abides in the state of a magician.
When the mantrin rests in equipoise
And existence narrows greatly,
All the karma accumulated through ignorance
Since beginningless time is exhausted.
When he cultivates that mind,
The unsurpassed awakening mind,
Then too the mantrin, the yogin,
Like a lotus in the water,
Is not stained by the concepts of karma
That bring forth fruits of virtue and nonvirtue.
If he transforms this very self
Into the supreme Victor, what need is there to speak of it?"

Fascicle four.

Then the Blessed One Vairocana entered the meditative absorption called Vajra Play Victorious over the Four Māras, and spoke these vajra syllable-words, which are victorious over the four māras, liberate from the six realms of existence, and bring the wisdom of omniscience to perfection:
namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ a bīra hūṃ khaṃ
Then again the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi, those vajra holders, the bodhisattvas Samantabhadra and the rest, and that whole assembled retinue, every one of them filled with wonder, gazing without blinking, bowed their heads to the Omniscient One and spoke these words:

"You who are the treasured wealth
Of the protecting bodhisattvas and of all the buddhas!
Through this, those buddhas,
Protecting bodhisattvas,
Pratyekabuddhas, and śrāvakas,
The wise ones, here in this world,
Having attained the unsurpassed wisdom of the buddhas,
The incomparable wisdom,
Displaying miraculous powers of every kind,
Range across the whole face of the earth.
Therefore, the extensive rites
Of each of these, and all the arrangements
Of the various activities,
Please explain in this teaching.
Through that, sentient beings, rejoicing
In the unsurpassed Great Vehicle
Of the practice of secret mantra,
Will come to abide in the reality of this very life."
When they had made this request, the Blessed One
Spoke these words in a gentle voice:
"All of you, in harmony, one-pointedly,
Rest in equipoise and listen to this!"

At that time he blessed the ground as a great vajra, and wishing to reveal the essence of awakening from his own body, he spoke:
"A should be known as supreme;
It possesses the Mahendra maṇḍala.
Both outwardly and inwardly
It should be known as the vajra maṇḍala.
Seated there, one should contemplate everything;
This is declared to be the seat of yoga.
A is the most excellent breath of life,
Declared supreme for summoning.
When it is pressed down by the anusvāra,
It becomes the dispeller for all embodied beings.
When the mantrin for one month,
Having bound the seal of the vajra holder,
Recites at the three times of day,
The city of ignorance will be destroyed.
Even by gods and asuras
He cannot be split; he becomes utterly firm.
In order to accomplish gain for oneself,
Whatever rites of increase there may be,
All of these the mantrin should always
Perform in the center of the maṇḍala.
Golden in color and blazing,
Wearing a topknot and a crown ornament,
The perfect Buddha absorbed in meditative absorption:
This is called the basis of Mahendra.
A vajra, a lotus, a sword,
And likewise what is called clear,
Gold, and earth,
Or else a wish-fulfilling jewel:
Such substances as these
One should accomplish by meditating on them as Mahendra.
I shall also explain the pressings down;
Listen to this in meditative equipoise.
In brief, the mantrin should imagine
Mount Meru with eight peaks.
Upon it one should place a lotus,
And after that, a vajra.
Upon that the yogin places
The supreme syllable, the chief;
If he presses it down on someone's crown,
That one will remain unmoving.
Again, if one recites a hundred times over medicine
And a person drinks it,
The illnesses he suffers
That arise from former karma will be driven out.
Then further, gentle one,
Hear of the supreme syllable VA.
Like snow, milk, and conch,
It arises from the center of the navel.
A white lotus with its petals:
In its center abides
The supreme protector, utterly peaceful,
Like the light of the autumn moon.
Its maṇḍala is wondrous,
So the perfect buddhas have declared.
One should imagine it all white,
Encircled by nine concentric rings,
Pacifying every torment,
Abiding within gathered mist and dew.
Like milk, like garlands of white pearls,
Like crystal and the light of the moon,
Streams of water pour down
On every side, all around:
If the mantrin imagines this in his mind,
He will be freed from every torment.
In meditative equipoise one should accomplish
These things here in this maṇḍala:
Fresh butter, milk, garlands of pearls,
Likewise a bull, or crystal,
Curd, or else water:
Such substances one should accomplish
For the accomplishment of various attainments.
Life becomes long,
Form becomes greatly wondrous,
One is freed from all illnesses,
One's good fortune becomes supreme,
One becomes able to retain with a keen mind,
And wisdom becomes utterly stainless:
Through these substances of accomplishment
All of these are swiftly accomplished.
This glorious maṇḍala
Is declared to bring pacification.
When it is made together with the anusvāra,
It becomes supreme for dispelling.
The syllable RA, truly supreme,
Is called the light of all luminous things.
Through it, whatever karma there may be,
Even that arising from the five acts of immediate retribution,
If the yogin contemplates it
In deep meditative equipoise, will all be exhausted.
Lovely and all deep red,
It abides in the form of a triangle,
Peaceful, wreathed in garlands of flame;
One should place it at the heart, marked with the sign of the triple junction.
The yogin, seated there,
Should imagine RA together with the anusvāra.
With it, the fearless one
Accomplishes those things as is fitting."

With the Sun and all the planets,
And likewise, with focused mind, with fire,
The substances for bringing under control,
Or else for causing enmity,
For drying up and for killing,
And likewise for separating and for harming:
If one applies this together with fire,
One will accomplish all of these.
The syllable called ha, holy and supreme,
Is wind, from which all things truly arise.
Cause, action and result,
And that from which seeds are born:
If this is applied together with the anusvāra,
It destroys all of these.
Its form, too, is explained:
Black in color, of great radiance,
Wreathed in garlands of unbearable flame,
Sweeping everything away on every side.
The wise one should imagine it
Abiding in a maṇḍala between the eyebrows.
That maṇḍala is dark blue, like a crescent moon,
With a crest, and banners fluttering.
The mantra practitioner should imagine
The utterly unchanging syllable abiding there.
In the center of that maṇḍala,
For one who merely sees it,
The things that accomplish the welfare of sentient beings
Are at that time accomplished.
Going through the sky,
Or the bases of miraculous power,
The divine eye and the divine ear,
Which the mantra practitioner likewise attains,
Invisibility and all the rest,
And whatever divisions have been taught:
The mantra practitioner in meditative equipoise,
Abiding in the maṇḍala, accomplishes them all.
Here, at this heart of awakening,
The bodhisattvas of great renown,
By not apprehending any cause at all,
Gain victory over Māra.
Since there is no cause, there is no result either,
And action, too, does not exist;
Since these three things are absent,
One attains the wisdom of emptiness.
The perfect Buddha of great power
Has correctly explained its form:
Joined with the anusvāra,
khaṃ is called the supreme of emptiness.
With the mudrā of the sword
One surely accomplishes what is to be accomplished.
Sword, noose and wheel,
Iron arrow and likewise hammer:
When one is equipped with these implements,
One accomplishes the supreme state.

Then again the Blessed One Vairocana declared to Vajrapāṇi, the Lord of Secrets: "Lord of Secrets, when a bodhisattva who practices the conduct of a bodhisattva by way of secret mantra has transformed himself into the very nature of the syllable called a, no longer looks to any things, whether outer or inner, regards clods of earth, stones and gold as equal, and has abandoned faults such as attachment and aversion, as well as sinful actions,
Then at that time he becomes pure,
Like the Sage, the perfect Buddha.
He is capable of all activities
And has abandoned every fault.
Then, devoted to the other mantra yoga,
Knowing the ritual procedure,
Wishing to benefit sentient beings,
One should perform the rite of the syllable called va.
With a mind born of compassion,
Focusing on the suffering of the world,
That protector should fill
His own body entirely
With a stream of white,
Like snow or cow's milk.
Then, having generated certainty,
From all the pores
Of all his limbs
He should send forth streams of utterly stainless water.
With that water this whole earth,
Without remainder, should be entirely filled.
Whoever drinks this water,
Or even touches it with the hand,
All of them will surely
Attain awakening; of this there is no doubt.
The syllable called ra throughout the limbs,
Wreathed in peaceful garlands of flame,
Radiating on every side:
This one should imagine in deep meditative equipoise.
Then, if the yogin sends outward
A blaze that illuminates everything,
Through the emanations of ra
The mantra practitioner does whatever he wishes.
The syllable called ra in the upper body,
The syllable called va at the navel:
Blazing fire and streaming water
Are then displayed at one and the same time.
The sufferings of the cold hells
Are utterly pacified by ra;
The mantra practitioner, abiding in the way of mantra,
Pacifies heat and the like by va as well.
The syllable called ha, together with a banner,
Should be placed beneath the syllable called ra.
By it even people laden with sin
Swiftly accomplish the rites.
The mantra practitioner, seated upon Mahendra,
Should undertake the rite of water;
Dispelling and all the rest
He will perform; of this there is no doubt.
Wind is declared to go everywhere;
It is also what destroys all things.
The rites that arise from the divisions
Of activities of many different kinds
Should be performed by this ritual
In the center of the maṇḍala of form.
If one thoroughly purifies the mind and recites, the mind will become completely pure. If one trains the body in lightness and recites while walking, one will attain the bases of miraculous power. If one sits squatting, sets out the syllable called a, and recites for one month, one will attain the divine ear. Lord of Secrets, such states of accomplishment and the rest arise from the mind. Lord of Secrets, see how, through mere contemplation alone, out of the many different formations of things that are without form, the activity of the seeds of all things arises from mere verbal utterance.
Lord of Secrets, furthermore, in conformity with all the tathāgatas and in harmony with the intentions of all sentient beings, fully delighting sentient beings, he abides like a reflection before the bodhisattvas who perform all activities and practice the conduct of secret mantra. That Tathāgata is free of conceptual thought and has wholly transcended the domain of the mind; yet although he has left behind time, place, deed, action, Dharma and what is not Dharma,
He bestows the state of omniscience
That arises from the practice of secret mantra.
Therefore one should accomplish
The siddhi of omniscience and the supreme state."
Then the glorious Vajradhara,
Filled with wonder, his eyes wide open,
At that time brandished his vajra,
Its light rays flying out like sparks,
And, having illuminated
This entire buddha field,
He addressed these sweet words
To the Sage, the lord of all dharmas:

"Though the entry into mantra has been explained,
That entry is still not understood.
Where secret mantra comes from
And where it goes, O Lord,
Please explain all of this to me.
There is no supreme state apart from you;
Just as the ocean is for all the waters,
So in you all dharmas abide."
When he had said this, the Blessed One declared to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi:
"Great being, the abode
Of the maṇḍala is said to be the mind.
If one knows that the heart
Is the abode of secret mantra, one will attain the result.
White, yellow or red:
Whatever the mind conceives,
And the discernment of it,
That is the activity of mind.
Mind, satisfied and become certain,
Is what is called the heart.
The secret mantras abiding there
Will bestow vast results.
There one should imagine a lotus
Of eight petals, with its stamens,
And on it the syllable called a, wreathed in garlands of blaze.
The excellent one abiding there,
Peaceful, flickers on every side.
The Lord, possessed of manifold forms,
Like a thousand flashes of lightning,
Displays light on every side,
Like one abiding within a mirror,
Like the image of the moon in water;
In all things he abides before one.
The mantra practitioner, dwelling in the abode of secret mantra,
Having thus understood the mind,
Then, within emptiness,
In the center of the expanse of the brain,
Should imagine there the syllable called a,
Marked with the anusvāra.
Seeing it stainless and pure,
Like crystal, the moon or snow:
This is the peaceful body whose nature is Dharma,
Called the ground of all.
From it the siddhis of secret mantra,
Of manifold forms, are attained.
If one sees the abode of the Tathāgata,
One attains the higher realms and liberation through awakening.
Placing in the eyes the syllable called ra,
Blazing like a lamp,
Bending the neck slightly,
And joining the tongue to the palate,
Gazing into the center of the heart,
In equipoise one should look at the mind:
Stainless, utterly pure,
Ever abiding like a mirror.
This itself, the Victors of the past
Declared, is the true reality of mind.
On the path illuminated by the mind,
Consciousness becomes manifest.
Then the mantra practitioner
Will see the perfect Buddha, the lord of two-footed beings.
Having seen him, the mantra practitioner
Will constantly attain siddhi.
Then, transforming it as above,
The syllable to be fully contemplated,
The syllable called la with the anusvāra,
Should be imagined in the place of the eyes.
If one sees all this as empty,
One will attain the state that does not decline.
Mighty wisdom,
Or else the five superknowledges,
The siddhi of becoming a vidyādhara,
And becoming long-lived and youthful:
If one wishes to attain these,
As long as one has not performed the approach of this,
So long will one not attain them.
Arising from the wisdom of secret mantra,
This is that very supreme wisdom,
The protector of bodhisattvas,
The precious treasure of all the buddhas.
Buddhas, bodhisattvas
And the gentle śrāvakas as well,
Having attained the supreme wisdom of the buddhas,
Wisdom beyond compare,
Through it abide all over the earth,
And they speak of it there."
This concludes the extensive chapter on the reality of accomplishing siddhi from The Manifest Awakening, Emanation and Empowerment of the Great Vairocana.

Then again the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi said this to the Blessed One: "Blessed One, please explain the empowerment of the deity's form, by which bodhisattvas who practice the conduct of a bodhisattva by way of secret mantra, focusing on the deity's form and transforming themselves into the form of their own deity, will attain siddhi without any doubt."
When he had said this, the Blessed One Vairocana declared to the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi: "Lord of Secrets, it is excellent, excellent that you ask me about this matter. Therefore listen well and keep it firmly in mind, and I shall explain it to you."
"Blessed One, so be it," he replied, and the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi listened to the Blessed One. The Blessed One then declared: "Lord of Secrets, the form of the deity is of three kinds: syllable, mudrā and image. The syllable, in turn, is of two kinds: sound and the awakening mind. The form of the mudrā is also of two kinds: with form and without form. Lord of Secrets, the form of the deity is likewise of two kinds: completely pure and not completely pure. The completely pure is of the nature of nonconceptuality, free of all signs. The not completely pure is form with signs, that is, color and shape. Through these two kinds of deity form two kinds of purpose are achieved: through the one with signs, siddhi with signs; through the signless, even siddhi with signs will be achieved.
The supreme Victors hold that through what has signs
Comes the siddhi that has signs;
By abiding in the signless,
One can accomplish what has signs as well.
Therefore, in every way,
One should rely on the signless."
This concludes the chapter on ascertaining the samādhi of the deity from The Manifest Awakening, Emanation and Empowerment of the Great Vairocana.

Then again the Blessed One declared to the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi: "Lord of Secrets, a bodhisattva who practices the conduct of a bodhisattva by way of secret mantra and wishes to accomplish the signless samādhi should reflect as follows: From where have signs arisen? Have they arisen from my body, or from my mind, or from my mentation? Now, the body is born of karma; like grass, wood or clods of earth, it has by its own nature no activity. It should be regarded as like an image made by craft, something inert, a mere external shape. If a man, enraged at such a crafted image, were to destroy it, burn it or cut it with fire, poison, weapons, water or a vajra, it would feel not the slightest displeasure; and even if one were to honor it with food, drink, washing, ointments, incense, garlands, clothing, sandalwood, camphor and the many other kinds of requisites of gods and humans, it would not become pleased with that man.

Why is that? Whoever makes offerings to, or does harm to, that form which is empty of essential nature, out of false conception born of the error of fully grasping it with his own conceit, is of a childish nature. Lord of Secrets, in this way one should cultivate mindfulness that follows the body in the body as empty of essential nature.
Lord of Secrets, furthermore, one should reflect that the mind is without essential nature, free of all signs, and empty of essential nature. Lord of Secrets, in the mind there are no three times. One should reflect: 'What is free of the three times is by its essential nature free of signs.'
Lord of Secrets, furthermore, ordinary childish beings conceive the mentation as having signs; this is a mere verbal designation of false imagination. They do not understand that what is unreal is unborn.
Lord of Secrets, when a bodhisattva who practices the conduct of a bodhisattva by way of secret mantra reflects in this way, he will attain the signless samādhi. Lord of Secrets, when he comes to abide in the signless samādhi, then the secret mantras spoken by the Tathāgata will turn toward him and incline to him."
This concludes the chapter on the signless samādhi from The Manifest Awakening, Emanation and Empowerment of the Great Vairocana.

"Lord of Secrets, furthermore, I shall explain the secret of recitation.
By oneself alone, singly, or one by one,
One should recite mentally,
Or else in a whisper, in two ways;
This the supreme yogin should examine.
The mantra practitioner should not recite
In any other way, with its limbs left deficient.
The four-limbed recitation that I have taught,
Through outer and inner application,
Is worldly;
That which has a focus is unsurpassed.
Restrained by the words of withdrawal,
With a mind that follows the deity,
Reciting in a whisper is supreme;
This has been taught for the one that has a focus.
Know the mental one to be supramundane:
Withdrawal and the rest are wholly abandoned.
Making oneself one with the deity,
With a mind that holds them as not different,
One should make one's nature indivisible;
It is not to be done otherwise.
The count of secret mantra, three hundred thousand,
That I have generally taught
Is the number of recitations taught
For the pure mantra practitioner,
An embodied one free of sin;
It is not to be done otherwise."
This concludes the chapter on the reality of worldly and supramundane recitation from The Manifest Awakening, Emanation and Empowerment of the Great Vairocana.

Then again the Blessed One Vairocana, having looked upon the assembled retinue complete in every respect, and looking also with his two eyes of loving-kindness and great compassion upon all realms of sentient beings, entered into the samādhi called Source of Nectar. As soon as the Blessed One had entered into absorption, from all his limbs there came forth the queen of vidyā-mantras called the Powerful One Unattached in the Three Times:
tadyathā gaganasame apratisame sarbatathāgatasamatānugagate gaganabaralakṣaṇe svāhā
"Son of noble family, this queen of vidyā-mantras has a sphere of activity that is nondual with the body of all the tathāgatas.
Empowered by it, the buddhas
And the bodhisattvas of great renown
Attain the unattached nature of reality
Wherein suffering comes to cease."
Then the Blessed One Vairocana, bringing all the tathāgatas to mind, empowered both himself and Vajrapāṇi as primordially unborn, and declared to Vajrapāṇi and the other vajra holders: "Sons of noble family, listen to the extensive chapter on the practice of the maṇḍala called the Turning of the Wheel of Syllables, which, through the deeds of the buddhas, is present to each of the bodhisattvas who practice the conduct of a bodhisattva by way of secret mantra."
Then again Vajrapāṇi bowed to the Blessed One Vairocana, descended from that vajra lotus seat in the manner of a vajra descending, and praised the Blessed One:
"Homage to you, awakening mind!
Homage to you, source of awakening!
To you, the form of conduct, and to the grounds
And the perfections, I pay homage.
Homage to you who make the beginning!
Bowing to emptiness, I pay homage."
Having praised the Blessed One in these words, the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi said this to the Blessed One: "Blessed One, Lord of the Dharma, out of compassion for us, please explain those qualities which, if we possess them, will enable us to act for the benefit of sentient beings and to bring the practice of secret mantra to completion just as you have taught it."
When he had said this, the Blessed One Vairocana declared to the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi:
"I am the one proclaimed
The protector of the world, the first in the world.
I teach the Dharma that is unsurpassed,
Primordially peaceful, and without equal."
Then, having spoken those verses, the Blessed One performed an empowerment of such a kind that, empowered by it, those bodhisattvas and those vajra holders would see the Blessed One, seated upon the supreme and sacred seat of awakening, free of elaboration like space, in accordance with the karmic ripening that has the characteristic of the yoga nondual in conduct.

At that time, from the limbs of the Blessed One and from the whole body of the Tathāgata, there came forth this, which is like the life breath of all that is worldly and beyond the world, of śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas, of intention and of what is to be done in meditative concentration, and of all who strive to accomplish siddhi; which has become the sustenance of all syllables, become their life breath, become their refuge, and become their ally:
namaḥ smantabuddhānāṃ a
"Sons of noble family, this, empowered by all the tathāgatas, is the deed of the buddhas present to each of the bodhisattvas who practice the conduct of a bodhisattva by way of secret mantra: all dharmas enter by way of the syllable called a. Lord of Secrets, therefore a bodhisattva who practices the conduct of a bodhisattva by way of secret mantra, who wishes to see the tathāgatas, who wishes to make offerings, who wishes to realize the awakening mind directly, who wishes to keep company with bodhisattvas, who wishes to act for the benefit of sentient beings, who longs for siddhi, and who wishes to attain omniscience in all its aspects in every way, should apply himself to this heart of the tathāgatas."
Then, as to what the Blessed One Vairocana taught concerning the arrangement of the rite of this king of maṇḍalas, the Arising from the Heart of Great Compassion, the determination of the places of the deities, the emanation of samādhi, the practice of secret mantra and what is to be visualized: the master, abiding in the syllable called a, the gateway of omniscience, takes the cord and, having bowed to all the buddhas, stretches the cord along the eastern side and goes around clockwise; facing north he stretches the cord, and, facing west, he turns it and lays down the lines.
Then, empowering himself as Vajrasattva Vajrapāṇi with his mudrā or with the syllable called va, he should enter inside and draw the heart maṇḍala. Likewise for the second maṇḍala: empowering himself in this way as primordially peaceful, the form of nondual yoga, the very essence of the form of the Tathāgata, and then making manifest the abiding of the Tathāgata, he stretches the cord. Of that outer maṇḍala he should set two thirds as the place of the deities and make one third the place of the retinue. The remaining two maṇḍalas should likewise be divided and the places of the deities set out.
Then again, empowering himself as the Blessed One Vairocana with his mudrā, and empowering the vast dharmadhātu, he should first take the white pigment and empower it as follows:
"This is the pure dharmadhātu,
Which purifies the realm of sentient beings;
This is the self, the Tathāgata,
Who has abandoned every fault."
When the wise one has reflected thus,
There he should imagine the syllable called ra,
Peaceful in hue, with a garland of flames,
Like a conch, the moon or a kunda flower.
Then the second pigment,
The red, he should take, and diligently
Imagine the syllable called a,
Blazing, together with the anusvāra:
The Victor, holy and hard to overcome,
Like the sun when it first rises.
Then the mantra practitioner should take the third pigment,
Yellow in color.
Then the wise one should imagine the syllable called ga
At that time, according to the rite.
Likewise the Sage of golden light,
Golden in color and blazing,
Abiding in samādhi, destroyer of sin,
Illuminates all around.
Then the pigment like emerald,
Which liberates from saṃsāra:
There he should place the syllable called va.
The great Sage of the heart of awakening,
The hero, green in color,
Makes all fearless everywhere.
Then that black pigment
The wise one should take,
And there imagine the syllable called hā,
Radiating garlands of flame,
Unbearable like the fire at the end of time:
Vajrapāṇi, adorned with pointed tips,
Threatens in every way
All the hostile hosts of Māra.
Then the Blessed One Vairocana at that time arose from that samādhi and entered into the samādhi called Infinite Victory. As soon as the Blessed One Vairocana had entered into that samādhi, he brought forth this queen of vidyā-mantras called Unobstructed Power Everywhere, which arises from the domain of all the tathāgatas:
namaḥ sarbbatathāgatebhyaḥ sarbbamukhebhyaḥ asamebarame acale gagane smaraṇe sarbatrānugate svāhā
Then, having empowered the rite of the colors, one should read or bow to the Blessed Mother Prajñāpāramitā and recite this queen of vidyā-mantras eight times. Then one should rise, circumambulate the maṇḍala and enter inside; and, focusing on the practitioner through the power of great love and great compassion, one should empower oneself with the syllable called va as the Vajrasattva of activity, and, together with the bestowal of the sacred vajra, draw the maṇḍala Arising from the Heart of Great Compassion.
Now, this is the arrangement of the inner maṇḍala. In the center is the Blessed One, the Great Vairocana, seated on a white lotus, with a topknot of hair and a crown, wearing a lower garment of silk or cotton and an upper garment of fine silk, golden in color and wreathed in garlands of flame. He is drawn as a mudrā, as a form or as a seed syllable:
a
In the east is the essence of the dharmakāya of all the buddhas, the syllable called a together with the anusvāra:
aṃ
In the direction of Īśāna, the northeast, one should draw the mother of all the buddhas, the Blessed Lady Gaganalocanā, or else draw the syllable called ga:
ga
In the direction of fire, the southeast, one should draw the mudrā of all the bodhisattvas, the wish-fulfilling jewel, or the syllable called ka:
ka
In the north one should draw Padmapāṇi with his retinue, together with the bodhisattvas bound to one more birth, as the lotus mudrā or the syllable called sa:
sa
In the south, leaving three parts, one should draw the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi with his retinue, as the vajra mudrā or the syllable called va:
va
Leaving three parts beyond that, one should draw all the vajra holders as their mudrās or syllables.

Their seed syllable is:
hūṃ
Then, in the direction of Nairṛti, the southwest, below the Blessed One Vairocana, one should draw Acala, seated on a rock, holding a sword and a noose in his hands, wreathed in garlands of flame, threatening all obstructors, as his mudrā or his syllable. His seed syllable is:
haṃ
In the direction of the wind, the northwest, one should draw the one called Trailokyavijaya, the destroyer of the great obstructors, with garlands of upward-blazing flame, like the form of the fire at the time of the great destruction, who terrifies even the terrifying, holding in his hand a vajra that radiates light, as his mudrā or his syllable. His seed syllable is this:
ha
Then the four great protectresses are arranged in the four directions. In the east one should draw the great protectress called Fearless, golden in color, wearing white clothing, her face slightly wrathful, holding a staff in her hand, as her mudrā or her syllable. Her seed syllable is this:
ca
In the north one should draw the protectress called Remover of All Fears, white in color, holding a sword in her hand and wearing white clothing, blazing, free of all fear, as her mudrā or her syllable. Her seed syllable is this:
pa
In the west one should draw the protectress called Hard to Tame, the color of a red aśoka flower, wearing red clothing, smiling, gazing upon the maṇḍala of the complete retinue, as her mudrā or her syllable. Her seed syllable is this:
sa
In the south one should draw the great protectress called Vajra Tamer of the Untamed, black in color, her face wrinkled in wrath, wearing a black lower garment, with a topknot and a diadem, illuminating with her own blaze the whole world system complete in every respect, holding a staff in her hand, eliminating the great obstructors, as her mudrā or her syllable. Her seed syllable is this:
kṣa
All of these, too, together with their retinues and attendants, should be drawn seated on white lotuses.
When the intelligent one has arranged them thus,
The mantra practitioner devoted to mantra
Goes out according to the rite,
And in its proper place in the second section
Should draw the King of Sages,
Who brings delight to the Śākya clan:
The Protector, wearing saffron robes,
Endowed with the thirty-two supreme marks,
The god who acts for the sake of the teaching,
Who grants fearlessness to all sentient beings.
When the mantra practitioner sets out his mudrā,
It is the alms bowl, the robes and so forth;
Or else the wise one sets it down as a syllable.
This is its sacred rite:
bha
Then, in the outer maṇḍala, empowering himself as the essential nature of the dharmadhātu, with a mind that has become the awakening mind, he leaves three parts, recollects the Blessed One Vairocana three times, takes that same pigment, and in the third section on the east draws, as his mudrā or his syllable, the Bestower of the Sacred Vajra in youthful form, holding in his left hand a vajra atop a blue utpala, adorned with all ornaments, wearing a lower garment of silk or cotton and an upper garment of fine silk, colored like saffron, with five locks of hair on the crown of his head. His mantra is this:
namaḥ smantabuddhānāṃ maṃ
To his right one should draw Jālinīprabha in youthful form, endowed with the finest of all aspects, holding in his hand a net of jewels or a hook, as a mudrā or a seed syllable. His seed syllable is this:
cha
Then in the south one should draw the bodhisattva called Sarvanīvaraṇaviṣkambhin, golden in color, with a topknot and a diadem, holding a wish-fulfilling jewel in his hand, as his mudrā or his syllable. His seed syllable is this:
ā
