Then in the north one should draw the bodhisattva called Kṣitigarbha, green in body color, holding a lotus in his hand, adorned with all ornaments, as his mudrā or his syllable. His syllable is this:
ī
In the west one should draw the bodhisattva called Ākāśagarbha, endowed with the finest of all aspects, wearing white clothing, blazing, holding a sword in his hand, as his mudrā or his syllable. His seed syllable is this:
ī
Then the mantra practitioner devoted to the dharmadhātu,
Having taken his seat,
With a mind become the awakening mind
Whose nature is the dharmadhātu,
Remaining facing the east,
Binds the vajra mudrā,
And then, having become the vajra of activity,
Should diligently make offerings.
With an all-pervading mind,
Having displayed the mudrā called
The Pledge of All the Buddhas, the Protectors,
He should recite the secret mantra three times.
The disciples, pure and according to the rite,
Are called before the maṇḍala
And made to go for refuge to the Three.
Then the awakening mind is generated as well.
He should bind all of them
With those mudrās whose nature is the dharmadhātu.
Then he binds the mudrā of the wheel of Dharma,
And, transforming their form with his mind,
Covers their faces with a cloth.
With a mind that has become compassion,
So that they may complete the accumulations for awakening,
He should not let their hands be empty.
This sacred pledge
He proclaims into the ears of all of them;
Then he himself binds them
To the pledge of the buddhas.
With a mind become the awakening mind,
He then has them cast a flower.
Wherever the flower falls,
That the mantra practitioner bestows on him.
For the sake of the pledge for all embodied beings,
The rite should be performed in this way.
Then the glorious Vajradhara
Again asked that Lord:
"O Protector, supreme among speakers,
Please teach the rite of empowerment."
When he had requested this, the Blessed One,
Devoted to the dharmadhātu,
Spoke thus to Vajradhara:
"For the sake of mastery over all dharmas,
I shall explain the supreme rite;
With mind gathered to one point, listen to the rite.
Lord of Secrets, the master should empower himself as the form of the Tathāgata with his mantra or his mudrā. Then he should call the disciples, place them upon the great king of lotuses whose essential nature is the dharmadhātu, bind the mudrā of the Source of All Limbs, and confer empowerment upon them from the crown of the head with water from vases of four kinds of jewels, empowered by the four great bodhisattvas.

The wise one, having placed the syllable a
Together with the anusvāra upon the crown of the head,
Should place the one called a at the heart,
And place the one called ra at the chest.
Or else, place a everywhere.
Blazing with a golden color,
With topknot and crown,
Seated upon a white lotus seat,
The supreme Victor abides in meditative absorption.

This concludes the extensive chapter on the arrangement of the wheel of letters.

Then the Blessed Vairocana looked upon the assembled retinue, complete in every respect, and spoke thus to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi, the Lord of Secrets: "Lord of Secrets, there is an extensive arrangement of mudrās that are the ornaments of the tathāgatas, the tathāgata ornaments that are the signs of realizing the dharmadhātu. When bodhisattvas adorned with them wander in saṃsāra, then, because in every realm of beings they are marked with the sign of the great awakening of all the tathāgatas, gods, nāgas, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas, kinnaras, mahoragas, humans and nonhumans will give way before them, and those beings will also heed their words. Listen to it, and bear it thoroughly and well in mind, and I shall explain it to you."

When he had spoken thus, Vajrapāṇi said to the Blessed One, "Blessed One, now is the time for it. Sugata, now is the occasion for it."

Then, at that moment, the Blessed One entered into the meditative absorption called Empowerment of the Abode of Unobstructed Power. As soon as the Blessed One had entered into it, there arose the queen of vidyā-mantras, possessed of Unobstructed Power Everywhere, possessed of power equal to the unequaled, who brings about entry into the samaya, and who is endowed with the three powers:

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ a same trisame samaye svāhā

Lord of Secrets, this queen of vidyā-mantras is the one through which all the tathāgatas teach perfectly, which keeps one from transgressing the boundaries of the path of the three dharmas, and which brings the grounds and the perfections to completion. Its mudrā is this: join the two hands with the palms hollowed, and raise the two thumbs.

This seal is the great mudrā
Of all the buddhas, the protectors.
The samaya of all the buddhas
Abides firmly in this mudrā.

The intelligent one should make
Both of the hands into fists,
Place the two thumbs inside,
And raise the two index fingers.
This seal is the great mudrā,
The full practice of the dharmadhātu.

The mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ dharmmadhātusvabhāva ātmako 'haṃ

Likewise, turn back all the fingers of the two hands, interlace them, and touch the two thumbs together.

This glorious mudrā
Is proclaimed the Wheel of Dharma.
With it the protectors, the lords of the world,
Turn the wheel in full.

The mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ vajra ātmako 'haṃ

Likewise, join the two hands with the palms together in homage, and place the two index fingers upon the two thumbs, making it like a sword.

The sword seal is the great mudrā
Of all the buddhas, the protectors.
With it one cuts through
The innate view of the transitory collection.

The mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ mahākhaḍga viraja dharmma sandarśika sahaja sadkāya dṛṣṭi cchedaka tathāgatādhimukti nirjāta virāga dharmma nirjāta hūṃ

Likewise, join the two hands with the palms hollowed, and wrap the two index fingers around the two thumbs, making it like a conch.

This supreme and glorious mudrā
Is proclaimed the Conch of Dharma.
With it, in these worlds,
The buddhas and bodhisattvas
Teach the stainless Dharma
That leads to peace, to nirvāṇa.

The mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ sarbbathā oṃ

Likewise, from the joined palms extend all the fingers of the two hands, making it like a bell; touch the tips of the two thumbs and the two little fingers together, and touch the two index fingers to the middle fingers.

This supreme and glorious lotus
Is the indestructible vajra seat
Of all the buddhas, the protectors.
Upon it the guides attain awakening,
And the Victors and all their heirs
Are born from it and attain awakening.

The mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ a

Likewise, make the two hands into fists, raise the two middle fingers side by side, bend the two index fingers and place them at the two sides, making it like a vajra. Raise the two thumbs and the two little fingers and set them so, and put the two ring fingers inside the hands.

The great mudrā, the vajra seal,
Destroys the city of ignorance.
This wisdom of the buddhas
Is revealed, empowered by itself.

The mantra is: namaḥ smanta vajrāṇāṃ hūṃ

Likewise, make the two hands into fists, raise the middle fingers side by side, and bend the two index fingers from the sides and join them.

The uṣṇīṣa mudrā is a great seal;
It is proclaimed the Uṣṇīṣa.
Merely by binding it,
One becomes a supreme Victor.

Its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ hūṃ hūṃ

Make the right hand into a fist and place it between the eyebrows.

This mudrā the holy Victors
Have proclaimed the Ūrṇā, the curl between the brows.
Merely by binding it,
One becomes a supreme Victor.

Its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ a haṃ jaḥ

The wise one, abiding in the manner of yoga,
Should hold the two hands level with the navel
In the manner of holding an alms bowl;
This is proclaimed the alms bowl mudrā.
This seal belongs to Śākyamuni
And is called the Great Alms Bowl.

Its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ bha

Likewise, raise the right hand upward, as in granting fearlessness.

Through it fearlessness
Is granted to all sentient beings.
When one binds that great seal,
It is proclaimed the mudrā of fearlessness.

Its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ sarbbathā jina jina bhayanāśana svāhā

Likewise, lower the right hand, as in granting the supreme boon.

This is the seal of granting the supreme,
So the protectors of the world have proclaimed.
Merely by binding it,
The supreme Victors will bestow the highest.

Its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ varada vajra ātmako 'haṃ svāhā

Make the right hand into a fist, extend the index finger, and make the brow like a wrathful frown.

This great mudrā, this great seal
Of all the buddhas, the protectors,
Is called Threatening Māra;
It destroys all who bring ruin.
Merely by binding it,
The utterly terrible hosts of Māra,
Obstructors and other vināyakas as well
Will flee, without any doubt.

Its mantra is this: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ mahābalavati daśabala tejodbhave mahāmaitryaṅhyudagate svāhā

Likewise, make the right hand into a fist, extend the two middle fingers and the two ring fingers, and place the thumb near them.

This is the eye, arisen from compassion,
Of the buddhas, the protectors.
The wise one should place it upon the eyes;
Through it one gains the eye of a buddha.

Its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ gagana vara lakṣaṇa karuṇamāya tathāgata cakṣuḥ svāhā

Likewise, join the two hands together and bind a fist with the fingers interlaced; raise the two index fingers, bend them, touch them together and make them round.

This is known as the supreme noose;
It destroys all who are hostile.
When the mantra practitioner binds it,
The hostile are bound forever.

Its mantra is this: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ he he mahāpāśa prasarodārya satvadhātu vimocaka tathāgata adhimukti nirajata svāhā

Likewise, join the two hands together and make fists; raise the index finger and bend its third joint, and let the rest remain in the manner of a bracelet.

This is explained as the hook
Of all the buddhas, the protectors.
Great-souled bodhisattvas
Who dwell on the ten grounds,
And others with hostile minds as well,
It summons them all.

Its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ a sarbbatra apratihata tathāgata aṃkuśa bodhicārya paripūraka svāhā

From the hook mudrā, bend the two middle fingers and join them right there. This is the mudrā of the Tathāgata's heart, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ jñānodbhava svāhā

From that same mudrā, raise the two ring fingers and keep the rest as they were. This is the mudrā of the Tathāgata's navel, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ amṛtoddhava svāhā

From that same mudrā, raise the little fingers and keep the rest as they were. This is the mudrā of the waist, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ tathāgata gati saṃbhava svāhā

Likewise, join the two hands with the palms hollowed; bend the two index fingers and put them inside, and do the same with the two ring fingers. Bend the thumbs and the two little fingers slightly, and raise the two middle fingers side by side. This is the mudrā of the Tathāgata's treasury, and its mantra is: namaḥ samanta buddhānāṃ raṃ raṃ raṃ svāhā

From that same mudrā, take out the two ring fingers, raise them and set them so. This is the mudrā of sealing the great boundary, and its mantra is this: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ velupuri vikuri vikule svāhā This is the sealing of the great boundary.

From that, bend the two middle fingers and raise the two thumbs. This is the mudrā of great protection, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ tathāgatebhyaḥ sarbbabhayevigatebhyo viśvamukhebhyaḥ sarbathā haṃ khaṃ rakṣa mahābale sarbbatathāgata puṇya nirjāte hūṃ hūṃ traṭa traṭa apratihate svāhā This is the great protectress called Unbearable.

Likewise, extend the two index fingers and put the two thumbs inside. This is the mudrā called Blazing All Around, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ jvālamālini tathāgata arci svāhā This is Blazing All Around.

Likewise, join the two hands with the palms hollowed, and wrap them from the sides with the two index fingers. This is the mudrā of the Tathāgata's armor, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ pracaṇḍa vajra jaya visphura hūṃ This is the Tathāgata's armor.

From that same mudrā, join the two index fingers and the two thumbs together. This is the mudrā of the Tathāgata's tongue, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ tathāgata jihva siddhi dharmma pratiṣṭhite svāhā This is the Tathāgata's tongue.

From that same mudrā, bend the two index fingers and the two ring fingers and join them inside. Raise the two thumbs and bend them slightly, so that their tips face the middle fingers. Extend the two little fingers, and keep the two thumbs as before. This is the mudrā of the Tathāgata's great mouth, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ tathāgata mahāvāktre vijñāna mahodaya svāhā This is the Tathāgata's mouth.

From that same mudrā, release the index fingers, put them inside, make them crooked, and hold them pointed. This is the mudrā of the Tathāgata's fangs, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ tathāgata mahādaṃṣṭra sarasarāgra samaprabhāka sarbbatathāgata viṣaya saṃbhava svāhā These are the Tathāgata's fangs.

From that same mudrā, raise and hold the two index fingers, and bend their third joints. This is the mudrā of the Tathāgata's specific knowledges, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ acinta andhuta rūpa pāvaga saṃprati vidyā viśuddha svara svāhā These are the Tathāgata's specific knowledges.

Likewise, join the two hands together with the palms hollowed, put the two thumbs and the two little fingers inside, and touch them together. This is the mudrā of the ten powers, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ daśabala gadhara hūṃ saṃ jaṃ svāhā These are the mudrā and the mantra of the ten powers.

From that same mudrā, bend the two index fingers and join them to the tips of the two thumbs. This is the mudrā of the applications of mindfulness, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ tathāgata smṛti satva hita abhyuṅgata gagana sama sama svāhā These are the mudrā and the mantra of the applications of mindfulness.

From that same mudrā, hold the two ring fingers upon the thumbs. The mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ sarbbadharmma samatā prāpta tathāgata anugata svāhā These are the mudrā and the mantra that bring realization of the sameness of all phenomena.

Likewise, join the two hands together, wrap the two middle fingers with the two index fingers, and keep the rest as they were. This is the mudrā of Samantabhadra's wish-fulfilling jewel, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ samantānugata virāja dharmma nirjāta mahā mahā svāhā These are the mudrā and the mantra of the wish-fulfilling jewel.

From that same mudrā, bend the two index fingers inside the two middle fingers and set them so; the rest are as before. Its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ aciteñjaya sarbbasatvāśayānugata svāhā These are the mudrā and the mantra of Maitreya.

From that same mudrā, put the two thumbs inside. The mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ ākāśa samatānugata vicitraṃ vara dhara svāhā These are the mudrā and the mantra of Ākāśagarbha.

From that same mudrā, put the two ring fingers and the two little fingers inside, and join the two index fingers together with the two middle fingers. The mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ ata satvahita abhyuṅgata traṃ traṃ raṃ raṃ svāhā These are the mudrā and the mantra of Sarvanīvaraṇaviṣkambhin.

The fifth fascicle.

Likewise, join the two hands together and open all the fingers so that they are like a bell. Join the two thumbs and the two little fingers together, and make the rest like a lotus. The mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ sarbbatathāgata avalokita karuṇāmāya ra ra ra hūṃ jaḥ svāhā These are the lotus mudrā and the mantra of Avalokiteśvara.

Likewise, join the two hands with the palms hollowed, and make them like an unopened lotus. This is the lotus mudrā, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ jaṃ jaṃ sa svāhā These are the lotus mudrā and the mantra of Mahāsthāmaprāpta.

Likewise, make the two hands into fists and raise the two index fingers to a point, and place the two thumbs near them as well. The mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ tāre tāreṇi karuṇoṅbhave svāhā This is that of the goddess Tārā.

From that same mudrā, twist the two index fingers and raise them; the rest are as before. The mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ sarbbabhāva santrāsani hūṃ sphoṭaya svāhā This is that of Bhṛkuṭī.

Likewise, join the two hands with the palms hollowed, and put the two ring fingers and the two thumbs inside. The mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ tathāgata viṣaya sambhava padma mālīnī svāhā This is that of Pāṇḍaravāsinī.

From that same mudrā, raise the two thumbs upward, and set the tips of the two index fingers near them, separated by a gap of about a barleycorn. The mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ khāda bhañjasphoṭaya svāhā These are the mudrā and the mantra of Hayagrīva.

From that same mudrā, raise the two ring fingers and the two little fingers upward as well, and keep the rest as they were, in the manner of a clenched fist. Its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ ha ha ha sunatanu svāhā These are the mudrā and the mantra of Kṣitigarbha.

Likewise, join the two hands with the palms hollowed, wrap the two ring fingers with the two middle fingers, and bend the two index fingers and set them upon the two thumbs; the rest are as before. The mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ he he kumāraka vimukti pathe stiṣṭhate smara smara pratijñāna svāhā These are the mudrā and the mantra of Mañjuśrī.

Make the left hand into a fist and bend the index finger. This is the hook mudrā of Jālinīprabha, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ he he kumāra yagata svabhāva sthita svāhā These are the mudrā and the mantra of Jālinīprabha.

From that same mudrā, make all the fingers crooked. This is the utpala mudrā of the youth Vimalaprabha, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ he he kumāra vicitra gati kumāra pratijñāmanusmara svāhā These are the mudrā and the mantra of Vimalaprabha.

Likewise, make the right hand into a fist, and raise the index finger and the middle finger upward side by side. This is the mudrā of Keśinī, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ he he kumāri kleśa cchedaya ājñānān smara pratijñāna svāhā These are the mudrā and the mantra of Keśinī.

Likewise, make the right hand into a fist and raise the middle finger upward, making it like the point of a spear. This is the spear-point mudrā of Upakeśinī, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ baṃndhaya ajñā he kumārike svāhā These are the spear-point mudrā and the mantra of Upakeśinī.

Make the two hands into fists, extend the two middle fingers, and join the two index fingers at their sides. This is the staff mudrā of Citrā, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ he kumārike ājñāna pura vidvaṃsani svāhā

Likewise, make the left hand into a fist and raise the ring finger and the little finger. This is the banner mudrā of Vasumatī, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ he sphura jñāna ketu svāhā These are the banner mudrā and the mantra of Vasumatī.

Make the right hand into a fist, raise the index finger, and bend its tip. This is the hook mudrā of Ākarṣaṇī, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ ākarṣaya sarbbaṃ kuru ājñāna kumārasya svāhā These are the hook mudrā and the mantra of Ākarṣaṇī.

Likewise, make the two hands into fists, raise the two index fingers, and bend their third joints. The mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ avismayaniye svāhā These are the mudrā and the mantra of the female attendants.

Likewise, make the right hand into a fist, raise the middle finger, and bend its third joint. This is the vajra mudrā of the Wondrous One, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ vimaticchedaka svāhā These are the vajra mudrā and the mantra of the Wondrous One.

Likewise, raise the right upper arm and make the hand as in granting fearlessness. This is the mudrā of granting fearlessness, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ abhayandāta svāhā These are the mudrā and the mantra of granting fearlessness.

Make that same hand as though drawing something up from below. This is the mudrā of Apāyajaha, the Abandoner of the Lower Realms, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ abhyuṅdhara satvadhātuna svāhā These are the mudrā and the mantra of Apāyajaha.

Place that same hand at the chest. This is the mudrā of Paritrāṇāśayamati, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ he mahā mahā smara pratijñā svāhā These are the mudrā and the mantra of Paritrāṇāśayamati.

Make that same hand as though holding a flower. The mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ satve vajroddhava svāhā These are the mudrā and the mantra of Maitryabhyudgata.

Place that same hand at the heart and bend the middle finger inward. The mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ karuṇodbhava svāhā These are the mudrā and the mantra of Karuṇāmṛdita.

Likewise, make the right hand in the manner of granting the supreme boon. The mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ he he varada vara prāpta svāhā This is that of the Pacifier of All Torment.

Make that same hand in the manner of holding a wish-fulfilling jewel. The mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ sarbbaśa paripūraka svāhā These are the mudrā and the mantra of Acintyamati.

Likewise, make the two hands into fists, and bring out and extend the two middle fingers. This is the flower mudrā of Kṣitigarbha, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ ha ha ha vismaye svāhā These are the mudrā and the mantra of Kṣitigarbha.

Make the right hand into a fist and extend three fingers. This is the mudrā of Ratnākara, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ he he mahā mahā svāhā These are the mudrā and the mantra of Ratnākara.

From that same hand, extend the ring finger and raise it upward. The mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ radbhava svāhā These are the mudrā and the mantra of Ratnapāṇi.

Likewise, cross the two hands and join them back to back. With the left thumb hook the right little finger, and likewise with the right hook the left, and make the rest like a vajra. This is the mudrā of Dharaṇiṃdhara, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ dharaṇī prāpta svāhā These are the mudrā and the mantra of Dharaṇiṃdhara.

The mudrā of the One with the Jewel Mudrā in His Hand is the five-pointed vajra, as before. The mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ ratna nirjāte svāhā These are the mudrā and the mantra of the One with the Jewel Mudrā in His Hand.

From that same mudrā, spread the fingers apart. This is the mudrā of the One of Firm Intention, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ vajra saṃbhava svāhā These are the mudrā and the mantra of the One of Firm Intention.

Making the two hands in the same way is the sword mudrā of Ākāśagarbha, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ gagana nānta cara svāhā These are the mudrā and the mantra of the noble Ākāśagarbha.

The wheel mudrā, made as before, is the mudrā of Gaganamati, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ cakravarti svāhā These are the mudrā and the mantra of Gaganamati.

The conch mudrā, made as before, is the mudrā of Viśuddhamati, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ dharmma saṃbhava svāhā These are the mudrā and the mantra of Viśuddhamati.

The lotus mudrā, made as before, is the mudrā of Cāritramati, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ padma mālāya svāhā These are the mudrā and the mantra of Cāritramati.

The utpala mudrā as before, made unopened, is the mudrā of Gaganāmala, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ jñānodbhava svāhā These are the mudrā and the mantra of Gaganāmala.

The vajra, made as before, is the mudrā of Sthiramati, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ vajra kāra svāhā These are the mudrā and the mantra of Sthiramati.

Likewise, join the two hands together and put the two ring fingers inside. Raise the middle fingers, the two thumbs and the two little fingers, and bend the two index fingers at the sides of the middle fingers, keeping them apart by about a barleycorn so that they do not touch. This is the mudrā of Vajrapāṇi, the vajra, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta vajrāṇān caṇḍa mahāroṣaṇa hūṃ This is that of Vajradhara.

From that same mudrā, put the two thumbs and the two little fingers inside. This is the mudrā of Māmakī, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta vajrāṇāṃ triṭa triṭa jayanti svāhā This is that of Māmakī.

Likewise, join the two hands back to back, interlace the fingers with one another, twist them and bind them tight, pressing down the right thumb with the left thumb, and bind them. This is the mudrā of Vajraśṛṅkhalā, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta vajrāṇāṃ hūṃ bandha bandha moṭaya moṭaya vajrodbhave sarbbatra pratihate svāhā These are the mudrā and the mantra of Vajraśṛṅkhalā.

From that same vajra mudrā, bend the two thumbs slightly, and turn the two index fingers toward them as well. This is the vajra mudrā of Krodhacandra, the chief, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta vajrāṇāṃ hrīḥ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā This is that of Krodhacandra, the chief.

Likewise, make the two hands into fists and raise the two index fingers side by side. This is the mudrā of Vajra Point, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta vajrāṇāṃ sarbbadharmma nidana vajrā sūci varade svāhā This is that of Vajra Point.

Likewise, make the two hands into fists and place them at the heart. This is the mudrā of Vajramuṣṭi, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta vajrāṇāṃ ṭaya vajra saṃbhave svāhā This is that of Vajramuṣṭi.

Making the left hand into a fist, raising its index finger upward, and threatening as though in wrath is the mudrā of Hard to Tame, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta vajrāṇāṃ durdānta mahāroṣaṇa lādaya sarbban kuru tathāgata ājñāna svāhā This is that of Hard to Tame.

Make the right hand into a fist, in the manner of striking.

This is the hammer mudrā of Abhimukha, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta vajrāṇāṃ he abhimukha mahācaṇḍa grīhṇa khādaya kiñcirayasi samayamanusmara svāhā This is that of Abhimukha.

Making the hand in the manner of holding an alms bowl, as before, is the alms bowl mudrā of Śākyamuni, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ sarbbakleśa nisūdana sarbbadharmma paśīta prāpta gagana sama svāhā This is that of Śākyamuni.

The ūrṇā mudrā is as before.

Join the tips of all the fingers of the right hand and place it upon the crown of the head. This is the mudrā of all the uṣṇīṣas, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ vaṃ vaṃ hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā These are the mudrā and the mantra of all the uṣṇīṣas.

Make the left hand into a fist and extend the middle and index fingers, placing the thumb upon the tips of the ring finger and little finger. Put the index and middle fingers of the right hand into the left palm, and make the thumb as before as well. This is the mudrā of the protector Acala, the sword placed in its sheath.

That of Trailokyavijaya is the vajra, as before.

Likewise, join the two hands together and put the two ring fingers and the two little fingers inside. Raise the two middle fingers side by side, bend the two index fingers and place them at the third joints of the middle fingers, and make the two thumbs like eyes. This is the mudrā of the Mother of the buddhas and bodhisattvas; it is the uṣṇīṣa.

Extend the left hand with the palm facing down, make the right hand into a fist, and hold the index finger like a parasol. This is the mudrā of Sitātapatra, the White Parasol: the parasol. khaṃ

That of Jayoṣṇīṣa, the Victorious Uṣṇīṣa, is the sword, as before. śaṃ

That of Vijaya is the wheel, as before. siṃ

That of Vikiraṇa is the hook: make the right hand into a fist, bend the index finger, and set it so. hūṃ

That of Tejorāśi is the uṣṇīṣa, as before. The mantra is: tṛṃ

The mudrā of Mahodgata
Is known as the vajra mudrā. uṃ
The mudrā of Abhyudgata
Is the excellent lotus, as before. tuṃ
For Anantasvaraghoṣa,
I have explained, it is the conch. ruṃ

Making the right hand into a fist and placing it between the eyebrows is the ūrṇā mudrā, the wish-fulfilling jewel.

That of the Eye is the uṣṇīṣa, as before, but distinguished by being marked with a vajra. ga

Placing the right hand at the heart in the manner of holding a lotus, and extending the left hand as though slapping with the palm, is the mudrā of Aparājita. The mantra is: dhiṃ dhiṃ riṃ riṃ jiri jiri

Make the two hands into fists, and from raising the two thumbs upward, bend them. This is the mouth mudrā of the one called Aparājita. aparajita jayanti ṭite svāhā

Showing the right hand at the cheek is the mudrā of Īśvara.

From that same mudrā, hold the middle finger and index finger as though twisted. This is the mudrā of Samantakusuma.

From that same mudrā, put the thumb inside. This is the mudrā of Raśmimālin.

From that same mudrā, make the index finger and little finger as though holding a flower. This is the mudrā of Swift as Thought.

Place the thumb of the right hand upon the ring finger, extend the three, the index finger, middle finger and little finger, and place them above the ear; do the same with the left hand. This is the mudrā of Proclaimer of Sound.

Joining the two hands together, touching the tips of the two thumbs to make a circle, and bending the tips of the remaining fingers is the vase mudrā.

Likewise, making the right hand in the manner of granting fearlessness and placing the thumb inside is the mudrā of the One Without a Frown.

Make that same hand as in granting fearlessness, and place the thumb at the second joint of the little finger. This is the general mudrā of the sages; their order should be as is fitting.

Likewise, joining the two hands together, putting the two index fingers and the two little fingers inside, gathering the rest together and holding them raised is the club mudrā of Yama.

Turning the right hand face down and making it like a bell is the mudrā of Mṛtyu, the Lord of Death: the bell.

Likewise, making the left hand into a fist and raising the index finger and middle finger is the mudrā of Kālarātri.

From that same mudrā, raising the middle finger upward is the mudrā of Raudrī: the trident.

From that same mudrā, making it in the manner of holding a lotus is the mudrā of Brahmā.

From that same mudrā, joining the index finger to the third joint at the back of the middle finger is the short spear mudrā of Kaumārī.

From that same mudrā, place the index finger at the tip of the thumb. This is the wheel mudrā of Vaiṣṇavī.

From that same mudrā, raise the thumb upward. This is the mudrā of Vārāhī: the hammer.

From that same mudrā, make the hand like a vessel. This is the mudrā of Cāmuṇḍī: the skull cup.

The sword mudrā of Nairṛti is as before. The wheel mudrā of Viṣṇu is also as before.

Likewise, raising the two hands from the left side and circling them is the cloud mudrā of Nanda and Upananda.

From that same mudrā, raising three fingers upward is the trident mudrā of Śaṃkara.

From that same mudrā, making those fingers straight is the mudrā of Umāpati.

The mudrā of the Moon is the lotus, as before, but it should be made white in particular.

Place both of those hands side by side, palms turned up, and bend the two index fingers and the two ring fingers at the sides. This is the mudrā of the Sun: the chariot.

Join both of those hands together palm to palm, and make the middle fingers and the two ring fingers like a bow. This is the mudrā of Jayā and Vijayā.

The banner mudrā, as before, is that of Vāyu.

Likewise, turn the left hand up toward the navel, raise two fingers, and hold it so; turn the right hand toward it, and making it as though playing a vīṇā is the mudrā of Sarasvatī: the vīṇā.

The mudrā of Varuṇa is the noose, as before.

From that same mudrā of Sarasvatī, bend the index finger. This is the mudrā of the asuras, and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ garalayaṃ svāhā

From that same mudrā, extend the ring finger. This is the mudrā of the gandharvas, and its mantra is: viśuddha svara vahine svāhā

From that same mudrā, bend the index finger.

This is the mudrā of all the yakṣas, and its mantra is: yakṣeśvara svāhā

From that same mudrā, join the tips of the thumbs and the little fingers. This is the mudrā of the yakṣiṇīs, and its mantra is: yakṣa vidyādhārī svāhā

From that same mudrā, extend the middle finger. This is the mudrā of the piśācas, flesh-eating spirits, and its mantra is: piśāce gati svāhā

Bend that same middle finger and hold it so. This is the mudrā of the piśācīs, and its mantra is: mici mici svāhā

Likewise, join the two hands together and hold them with the two thumbs raised upward. This is the mudrā of all the planets (grahas), and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ graheśvarya prāpta jyotirmaya svāhā

From that same mudrā, join the middle fingers and the thumbs, turned back. This is the mudrā of all the lunar mansions (nakṣatras), and its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ nakṣadra niranādanīye svāhā

From that same mudrā, bend the two ring fingers and place them in the palm. Its mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ rākśasādhipataye svāhā This is the mudrā and mantra of the rākṣasas.

Place the right hand at the mouth so that it touches the tongue. This is the mudrā of the ḍākinīs, and its mantra is: namaḥ samanta buddhānāṃ

Lord of Secrets, these and others like them are the countless mudrās of the tathāgatas, arisen from the tathāgatas' resolve, which have become the signs of the bodhisattvas. Yet, Lord of Secrets, however many movements of the limbs there may be, all of them should be understood as extensive mudrās; and however many movements of the tongue there may be, all of them should be understood as the teaching of mantra.

Therefore, Lord of Secrets, bodhisattvas who practice the bodhisattva conduct by way of the gateway of mantra should, with a mind endowed with the awakening mind, abide on the ground of the Tathāgata and draw the maṇḍala. Should they do otherwise, they would deceive the buddhas and the bodhisattvas, and awakening as well; and because they have transgressed the sacred commitment, they will certainly go to the lower realms.

From The Manifest Awakening of the Great Vairocana, His Miraculous Manifestation and Empowerment, the extensive chapter on the sequence of the mudrās.

Then the Blessed One spoke to the Lord of Secrets, Vajrapāṇi: "Lord of Secrets, listen to the Dharma discourse on the gateway that enters everything. Lord of Secrets, here the bodhisattva should abide in the syllable called a and accomplish every aim.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ aḥ namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ saḥ namaḥ smanta vajrāṇāṃ vaḥ ka kha ga gha ca cha ja jha ṭa ṭha ḍa ḍha ta tha da dha pa pha ba bha ma ya ra la va śa ṣa sa ha ṅa ña ṇa na

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ a namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ sā namaḥ smanta vajrāṇāṃ vā kā khā gā ghā cā chā jā jhā ṭā ṭhā ḍā ḍhā tā thā dā dhā pā phā bā bhā mā yā rā lā vā śā ṣā sā hā dā ñā ṇā nā

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ aṃ namaḥ samanta buddhānāṃ saṃ namaḥ smanta vajrāṇāṃ paṃ kaṃ khaṃ gaṃ ghaṃ caṃ chaṃ jaṃ jhaṃ ṭaṃ ṭhaṃ ḍaṃ ḍhaṃ taṃ thaṃ daṃ dhaṃ paṃ phaṃ baṃ bhaṃ maṃ yaṃ raṃ laṃ vaṃ śaṃ ṣaṃ saṃ haṃ ṅaṃ ñaṃ ṇaṃ naṃ

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ aḥ namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ saḥ namaḥ smanta vajrāṇāṃ vaḥ ka kha ga gha ca cha ja jha ṭa ṭha ḍa ḍha ta tha da dha pa pha ba bha ma ya ra la va śa ṣa sa ha ṅa ña ṇa na i ī u ū e ai o au

"Lord of Secrets, thus this is the Dharma discourse on skill in the way of letters. It is the rite of abiding in the way of mantra, the rite of taking up the deity; it is blessed with the blessing of all the tathāgatas, it is skill in the way of the perfectly awakened buddhas, and it is the music of practicing the bodhisattva conduct. The buddhas, the blessed ones, of the past, present and future have taught it, teach it, and will teach it. Lord of Secrets, I have not seen any among the countless buddhafields in which this Dharma discourse, the gateway that enters the tathāgatas in all their aspects, is not taught.

"Therefore, Lord of Secrets, bodhisattvas who practice the bodhisattva conduct by way of the gateway of mantra, if they wish to know the rites of mantra, should apply themselves diligently to this Dharma discourse on the gateway that enters everything. When ka, ca, ṭa, ta and pa are joined with a first, a last and a middle, then, by entering the host of meditative absorptions, they bring about the attainment of awakening, of the conduct, and of perfect buddhahood together with nirvāṇa. Therefore, in the rites of mantra, those letters that bear the designations 'first' and so on should be understood as occurring together with a first, a last and a middle, and one should apply them to each and every word of the mantras as one wishes.

When they are understood by the intellect,
They are the unsurpassed and supreme state.

"Thus this is a single wheel; revolving all around like a wheel, it is the wheel of letters. If a mantra practitioner knows this wheel of letters, he will always shine with splendor, just as the Blessed One Vairocana does when he turns the wheel."

The sequence of the way of letters, the Dharma gateway that enters all aspects.

Then again the Blessed One, having gazed upon all the realms of sentient beings with the eye of a tathāgata, in order to fulfill completely his vow to rescue all sentient beings without exception by this very display of the inexhaustible array of the dharma realm and by this accomplishment of the conduct of mantra, entered into the meditative absorption called Possessing the Essence of the Array of the Tathāgatas' Equality, the Empowering Might of the Treasury of the Dharma Realm. As soon as the Blessed One had entered into absorption, at that very moment, from all his pores, the gateways that bring about the deliverance of every realm of sentient beings without exception, there came forth bodies born of the dharma realm, which, in accordance with their wishes, gratify each and every sentient being individually, in the forms, colors, shapes, voices, gaits, conduct and definitive words of those who experience the fruits of action, lifespan and karmic maturation and of those who cause them to be experienced.

Having come forth, they made it known with a single voice, pervading the dharma realm, throughout the countless world systems equal to space, and proclaimed this verse on the coming forth of the tathāgatas:

"That characteristic of dharmas from which
All dharmas without exception,
The buddhas and the śrāvakas,
The protectors who are pratyekabuddhas,
The heroic bodhisattvas,
And likewise the supreme victors,
Sentient beings and the vessel world,
Arise in sequence as designations,
And from which the arising and abiding
Of dharmas always come forth:
This path, endowed with wisdom and means,
Free from doubt and hesitation,
And well taught by the perfect buddhas,
Is the path that you should cultivate."

Then those tathāgata bodies that had arisen from the dharma realm filled the entire dharma realm completely with clouds of emanations that made their own bodies known, and by the mere power of the mind of the Blessed One Vairocana they blessed the arising of buddhas, one after another in succession, from all his pores, and then entered each into its own place in the dharma realm.

Then again the Blessed One spoke to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi: "Lord of Secrets, there is the arrangement of the secret maṇḍala, the contemplation of the places of the deities as properly established, marked with their seed syllables. Listen to it, and keep it well and thoroughly in mind, and I shall explain it to you."

When he had spoken thus, the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi listened to the Blessed One, and the Blessed One said to him:

"First the mantra practitioner
Should set out that maṇḍala upon his own body.
From the feet up to the navel
He should imagine it as earth.
Then above that, as far as the heart,
The one diligent in vows should imagine water.
Above the water is that of fire,
And above the fire is wind.
Having imagined this, the wise one
Should then set up the image on the ground."

Then Vajrapāṇi, having entered the ground of the body, speech and mind of the Blessed One Vairocana, looked upon the equality of dharmas, and, considering the people of the future, spoke this king of vidyā-mantras that cuts off all doubts:

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ asamapati dharmma dhātu kārya gati gatānāṃ sarvvathā aṃ khaṃ aṃ a saṃ sa haṃ ha raṃ ra vaṃ va svāhā hūṃ mahā svāhā raṃ ra svāhā

As soon as the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi had spoken this great king of mantras, all the tathāgatas dwelling in the world systems of the ten directions stretched out their right hands, placed them on the crown of Vajrapāṇi's head, gave their approval with "Excellent!" and spoke these words:

"Son of noble family, excellent, excellent, that you, having entered the ground of the body, speech and mind of the Blessed One Vairocana, have spoken this king of mantras out of the wish to make it manifest to the bodhisattvas who abide in equality in the way of mantra in every respect! Why is that? The Blessed One Vairocana, the Tathāgata, the arhat, the perfectly complete Buddha, seated at the seat of awakening, examined the dharma realm by means of these twelve words and defeated the four māras. These words, arisen from the dharma realm, came forth from the three places, and the evil māras were subdued. Then the Blessed One, having attained the equality of body, speech and mind, attained a measureless body, because space is measureless; and likewise he also attained measureless speech and mind. Thus, being endowed with measureless wisdom and having gained mastery over all dharmas, he teaches the Dharma on the basis of these very twelve words, the king of mantras. Son of noble family, because you too have entered the equality of the body, speech and mind of the Blessed One Vairocana, you appear just like a perfect buddha.

"Ask the omniscient, perfect Buddha,
The Blessed Vairocana,
For the supreme words of mantra practice,
And he will declare the rite in full.
We too, in the past, by means of these,
Realized unsurpassed awakening
And taught the incomparable Dharma
Of the path by which beings go to nirvāṇa;
The perfect buddhas dwelling
In the world systems were our witnesses."

Then glorious Vajradhara,
His mind supremely delighted,
Blessed by the perfect buddhas,
Spoke these words:
"These dharmas are without characteristics,
Without essential nature, without abode,
Liberated from action and from life,
And are held to be like the perfect buddhas.
Through the skillful means of the protectors,
Who wield the sovereignty of compassion,
By their emanations, these characteristics
Of dharmas come to be realized."

Then the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi, having spoken that verse of inspired utterance, looked upon the beings of the future, and, in order to cut off doubts about this great maṇḍala arisen from the essence of great compassion, made this request of the Blessed One Vairocana:

"For the sake of all sentient beings,
O Omniscient One, free from all ailment,

You who cut off every doubt,
O Guide, this is what I ask of you:
What is to be done before the maṇḍala?
May the Guide declare it.
Great Sage, of how many kinds
Are the master and the disciple said to be?
What sort of ground should be sought,
And how should it be purified?
How does the ground become pure,
And how is it made stable?
By what is the disciple's purpose served?
May the Guide declare the purification too.
What are the signs of purity like,
And by what are they to be protected?
By what is the ground to be blessed,
And by what is it first examined there?
To lay out the lines on the portions of the ground,
How many kinds of thread are needed?
How many kinds of offering are needed?
What sort of flowers and incense,
And how should flowers and incense
Be offered? May the Guide tell this.
The food offerings and the rites of fire offering,
With which rites, and how, are they performed?
And the seats of the deities,
Tell for whom, and with what, they are made.
Their colors, shapes and forms as well,
May the Protector tell them in their order.
The deities and the mudrās,
And the measure of the seats, please explain.
From where have the mudrās arisen,
And why are they called mudrās?
How many kinds of empowerment are needed?
How many kinds of sacred commitment are there?
How does the mantra practitioner,
Diligent in the practice of mantra,
Become a bodhisattva and omniscient?
How does he come to see the truth?
How many kinds of accomplishment are needed?
Please declare the time of accomplishment as well.
Having accomplished it, how does one travel through the sky,
And how does one become invisible?
And, O Lord, without casting off this body,
How does one obtain the body of a god?
The various emanations,
From where do they arise for him?
Sun and moon, fire and the directions,
The lunar mansions, the planets and the stars,
The moments and the instants,
Old age, death and decline,
And all ill omens,
How is it that they do not arise for him?
How is the mantra practitioner never parted
From the Buddha, chief of the two-footed?
The rites of fire offering and the fires,
How many kinds of satisfying offering are needed?
And the individual essential nature
Of the deities, may the Guide tell.
Of the worldly mantras
And likewise of the transcendent ones,
The measures, the fruits and the numbers,
The distinctions of meditative absorption,
Where does each kind of karma ripen,
And how does it not ripen?
When will the mantra practitioners
Be liberated from karma and from life?"

Then the omniscient, perfect Buddha,
The Blessed One free from all ailment,
Spoke these words to Vajradhara:
"Arising from the root of great compassion,
The arrangement of the secret maṇḍala,
The precise determination of the deities,
The supreme state of the Great Vehicle,
Fully taught by the perfect buddhas:
Great hero, what you have asked
Is excellent indeed.
To you, Vajrapāṇi of great strength,
I shall explain it in brief.
The things to be done before the maṇḍala,
Listen to them with a gentle mind.
Wishing for their individual rites,
One who knows the method should, as is fitting,
Abide in his own meditative absorption
And recite, in proper order,
The king of vidyā-mantras of great might,
From which the twelve-limbed words arise.
The master who has realized and knows the mantras and mudrās
Should be known to be of two kinds;
By the distinction of profound and not profound
His characteristics should be known.
The one who knows the profound vastly
Places no trust in embodied things;
He is the supreme son of the buddhas,
Having wholly given up the things of this life.
The second, intoxicated
By the things of this life, has a reference point.
The buddhas, supreme among the two-footed,
So that he may draw
All the worldly maṇḍalas,
Give him their permission and confer empowerment as well.
By the distinction of timely and untimely,
Disciples should be known to be of four kinds:
One is to be accomplished at once,
One is accomplished in two stages, and one is neither.
The disciple complete in every characteristic
Is the most excellent of the victors.
The ground that is to be known first
Should be explained as the ground of mind.
The purification was taught before;
By that it should be purified completely.
When it is free of faults,
The ground of mind is without fear;
Then it is pure,
Having wholly given up every fault.
Because it sees manifest awakening,
Therefore it is called stable.
Purifying the ground in any other way,
It will not become pure.
Whatever mantra practitioner
Purifies the ground while abiding in conceptual thought,
Because he has abandoned the awakening mind,
O Lord of Secrets, it is not cleansed.
Therefore, having given up conceptual thought,
One should purify every ground.
Of the extensive rite of the maṇḍalas
That I have taught,
What was taught at the very beginning
The deluded do not understand:
As long as conceptual thought,
Which has become the cause of suffering, is not given up,
There is no buddha in this world,
Nor is anyone to be called omniscient.
The disciples should be diligently
Purified with the awakening mind.
Protection should be performed by the protector Acala
Or by Trailokyavijaya, victor over the three realms.
When the disciple, by his focus,
Never wavers at all,
Then, like stainless space,
He becomes perfectly pure.
The first blessing of the ground
Is said to be done by the perfect buddhas;
The second is by the power of the mind;
Here it is not done otherwise.
The threads are taught to be of four kinds:
White, yellow, red,
And black, so they are declared;
The fifth is taught to be like space.
Holding it in the sky,
One should examine the maṇḍala there.
With the second kind of thread
One should lay out the maṇḍala on the ground.
The seat of all the buddhas
And of their wise sons
Is the supreme lotus born of mind,
Auspicious and praised by the worlds.
The pratyekabuddhas and the arhats,
Renowned for their partial knowledge,
Should be known to have as their seat
A petal grown from the lotus.
Whatever worldly gods there are,
Brahmā and the others renowned in the world,
Their seat is a red lotus,
Praised by kings.

Those of the ordinary deities are as befits them,
Or follow the distinctions of their ground.
Offerings should be known to be of four kinds:
Prostration with palms joined together,
Likewise love and compassion,
And the worldly flowers and incense.
The flowers that arise from the fingers
Should be offered to all the protectors.
Binding the mudrā from which all limbs arise,
Cultivate the awakening mind,
And to each and every protector
And to their sons, perform it with diligence.
These are flowers without fault,
Most fragrant and lovely,
Trees of the dharma realm
That have arisen to honor the victors;
Transformed by the power of meditative absorption,
They are blessed by mantra.
Arising from the dharma realm,
Many vast and sublime clouds
Then fall continually like rain
Before the eyes of the buddhas.
For the other, ordinary deities
These flowers are proclaimed;
One who knows his own mantra tantra in full
Should offer them as is fitting.
Likewise flowers and scented ointments
Should be imagined as is fitting.
Joining the thumb and the ring finger
Is said to be the auspicious mudrā;
Taking up the flower with it,
Offer it from the region of one's own heart.
For the worldly deities,
It is said, from the level of the navel.
Incense, with the Vajramuṣṭi
Or else with the lotus garland,
Should be held up in the air
And offered to the protectors and saviors of the world;
And besides those, to the worldly
Deities in their proper order.
By the distinction of outer and inner,
The fire offering should be known as twofold.
Liberation from karma and from life,
And the seed arising once again:
The fire offering performed by one who wishes for this work
Is called the inner fire offering.
The outer one has three components,
Has three places, and abides in three;
Accomplishing the work of the three kinds of rite,
It is the supreme worldly fire offering.
Those who, not knowing the rite of fire offering,
Perform it in some other way,
Have abandoned the knowledge of mantra;
Such fools will not obtain the fruit.
The mantras of the tathāgatas,
And likewise those spoken by the perfect buddhas,
Should be known as white and yellow.
The mantras of all the Vajrapāṇis
And of Lokeśvara
Are white, according to the distinctions of the rites.
Square on every side,
And then, in order, circular,
Triangular, and like a half moon:
These are the shapes, well explained.
Forms should be known to be of two kinds,
Female and male.
Or else, everywhere, the colors,
Shapes and forms of all
Arise from inconceivable wisdom;
Therefore they are inconceivable.
The wisdom of the Knower of wisdom
Becomes diverse for the sake of beings.
However great the extent of the mind,
Just so great should be understood
The seats and the mudrās,
And the measure of the deities, in their order.
From that from which the buddhas arise,
From that the mudrās arise as well.
Disciples born of the Dharma
Should be sealed with the seal of the Dharma.
Therefore, in brief, the dharma realm
Is taught to be the sign and the mudrā.
Empowerment should be known to be threefold;
Therefore, listen with care.
The empowerment conferred by mudrā,
Free of rites and activities,
The empowerment of the protectors:
That is supreme, and it is the first.
The second is truly taught to be brought about
Through rites and activities.
The third should be known as mental,
Free from place and time.
Having pleased the guru well,
Whatever beneficial words he speaks:
That this empowerment is supreme
The perfect buddhas have proclaimed.
In brief, according to the buddhas,
Those who hold the sacred commitment are taught to be of five kinds.
The one who has seen this maṇḍala
Is called the first knower of the sacred commitment;
To him the mudrās are not shown,
Nor should the mantras be given.
By virtue of the awakening mind,
He takes up the mantras and the mudrās;
This one, called "seeing the maṇḍala,"
Should be known as the second holder of the commitment.
One who sees the maṇḍala and the mudrās,
And is also engaged in the rites and activities,
But has not been given the transmission by the guru,
Is also said to know the sacred commitment.
One to whom the mudrās and the drawing of the maṇḍala
Are then taught according to the rite
Is also a mantra practitioner who lacks the empowerment
From which secret wisdom arises.
The mantra practitioner empowered by the rite
In the center of the secret maṇḍala
Should be known as the fifth knower of the sacred commitment;
No other is called a holder of the commitment.
When one of excellent discipline
Cultivates the mind for the sake of awakening,
The mantra practitioner who does not apprehend the three spheres
Is a bodhisattva.
Having generated the courage of skillful means,
When he applies himself to the aims of beings,
Having attained a conduct without any reference point,
He should be known as the supreme victor.
Whoever, toward the dharmas that are peaceful from the very beginning,
That have no essential nature,
Abides with a mind like Mount Meru,
He sees the truth.
This is true, this is genuine,
And in the same way it does not deceive;
Just as the buddhas see the truth,
So did I see it in the past.
Whoever has attained the awakening mind,
What accomplishment other than that does he need?
Yet apart from that, accomplishments
Are held to be of five kinds:
Following the conduct,
Likewise subduing the grounds,
The worldly superknowledges, held to be five,
Buddhahood, and whatever else there is.
As long as karma, passing
From one continuum to another, is not exhausted,
And its ripening has not ripened,
That is held to be the time of the accomplishments.
In brief, when at one and the same time
Karma and birth have been cast away,
Then too the mantra practitioner obtains
Accomplishment truly arisen from the mind.

The sixth fascicle.

Traveling through the sky once accomplished
Is just like a fearless illusion;
Bewildering others with the net of mantra,
It is just like a great magical display.
That which has no body and no speech,
Just like a city of the gandharvas,
Is what is called becoming invisible;
He abides there in that.
Just as, in the dream of one asleep,
One goes to the Heaven of the Thirty-Three
Without casting off the body,
And yet the body itself did not go there,
So too, through yoga itself,
Mantra practitioners who abide in mantra,
Through deeds of merit,
Obtain a body like a rainbow.
Arising from body, speech and mind,
Mantra is like a wish-fulfilling jewel;
From it the aims one has conceived
Come forth even without deliberation.
Just as space in all the directions
Is not stained by any conditioned domain,
So too the mantra practitioner
Is not stained by any domain of conceptual thought.

When all of this, as mere perception,
Has been cultivated in this way,
When it is thoroughly cultivated, that mantra practitioner
Rejoices together with the perfect buddhas.
The fire offering, which the Buddha,
Chief of the two-footed, taught as twofold,
Is the outer and the inner,
Together with the satisfying offering.
Having fully known the individual
Natures of the deities,
He has well taught the measures
Of the worldly mantras:
Īśvara and the other mighty ones,
Whatever deities are honored,
The mantras and mudrās of great power
That they have made
Mostly bestow their fruit at once;
That is the measure that is taught.
Yet, being subject to arising and perishing,
They have no stability.
The transcendent mantras
Have no original maker.
Having cut off all karma and birth,
The victors free from the three faults,
The pratyekabuddhas who are like the rhinoceros,
And likewise the śrāvakas of the victors,
And the bodhisattvas too,
Taught of old the measure of mantra:
It wholly transcends the three times;
It arises from dependent origination,
Bears fruits both seen and unseen,
And arises from speech, mind and body.
The measure of the worldly fruit
Is taught to be a single eon;
The fruit of the mantras
Of the perfectly complete Buddha transcends the eons.
The meditative absorption of the buddhas, the great sages,
And of the victors' sons
Is signless and pure;
The worldly one is accompanied by signs.
In this very ripening
The fruit of karma comes to ripen.
When accomplishment is attained,
Then karma too is turned back.
Because the mind has no essential nature,
Having given up causes and effects,
Liberated from karma and from life,
It becomes like space.

"Lord of Secrets, furthermore, there is the arrangement in the form of mudrās, set out in sequence, the blessing of the presence of the deities, the perfect awakening by which the buddhas of the past manifestly and perfectly awakened, for the sake of the aims, benefit and happiness of the bodhisattvas who have realized the five meditative absorptions, who practice the bodhisattva conduct by way of the gateway of mantra, who have realized the dharma realm and made it their domain, and who have vowed to deliver the realm of sentient beings without exception. Listen to it, and keep it well and thoroughly in mind, and I shall explain it to you."

Saying, "Blessed One, I wish to hear it just as you say," Vajrapāṇi listened to the Blessed One, and the Blessed One spoke to him as follows:

"Lord of Secrets, as for what comes first, the sequential arrangement of the maṇḍala of the perfectly complete Buddha: there is the one called Arisen from the Essence of Great Compassion, from which countless worldly and transcendent maṇḍalas have arisen, more secret even than the secret. Listen to the rite for drawing it.

"On a ground square on every side,
With a single gate and a terrace,
Marked with excellent vajras,
In the center an eight-pointed vajra;
Upon it draw a lotus,
Made elaborate together with its seed syllables.
Above that a great lotus,
Beautified with the mudrā of the anusvāra,
Its eight petals well complete,
With its anthers, most excellent.
On all the petals
The twelve-limbed words are arrayed.
In their center sits the Protector,
The perfect Buddha, chief of the two-footed,
Encircled on every side
By the retinues of the eight maṇḍalas.
This maṇḍala arisen from compassion
Should be known as supreme;
The maṇḍalas according to the scriptures,
The rites, the bodies, the accomplishments,
And the arrangements too, one with a gentle mind
Should know to have arisen from this.

"Lord of Secrets, furthermore, the maṇḍala of the tathāgatas:

"Like the disk of the moon, its color
Like a conch; in its center
The triangle of all the buddhas
Rests upon a white lotus,
Inscribed with the sign together with the anusvāra
And marked with the sign of the vajra.
Without need of deliberation, all around,
Rays should be made, twofold;
Arising from the Lord of mantras,
They go forth into the ten directions.

"Lord of Secrets, furthermore,
The maṇḍala of the lord Avalokiteśvara,
Listen to it with certainty and with care.
On a ground square on every side,
In the center place something like a conch;
Above it a lotus
Should be drawn in full with its seed syllables.
Above that again a great vajra,
And above the vajra a great lotus,
All marked with seed syllables;
Apply each one's own seed syllable well.
Tārā and Bhṛkuṭī,
Likewise Pāṇḍaravāsinī,
The vidyā Bhogavatī,
And Mahāsthāmaprāpta too, with care,
All the glorious ones and the female attendants,
In the center of this maṇḍala,
The signs of their mudrās, those who have gained mastery,
Should be drawn with special care.
The place for Hayagrīva
Is set in the form of a triangle,
Inscribed with the excellent signs of the maṇḍala,
Its color like the rising sun;
Together with the vidyā kings,
The wise one should arrange it.

"Lord of Secrets, furthermore, I shall explain your own maṇḍala in brief.

"On a ground square on every side,
Marked with the signs of the vajra,
All yellow and pleasing,
In the center a lotus firmly set.
What should be made in its center
Is a vase like the light of the moon,
Marked with the signs
And adorned with the anusvāra.
Above that again the great wind,
Its color like the rain clouds of summer;
The mantra practitioner makes it with waving banners,
Adorned with the anusvāra as well.
Above that again a triangle,
Of most terrifying form,
Like the fire of destruction,
Inscribed with the sign of the three-pronged design,
Massed with garlands of flame, fearsome to behold,
Like the form of the rising sun.
In its center a great lotus,
Like the light of the fire at the end of time.
Upon the great lotus a vajra,
Radiating garlands of flame,
Resounding with the sound hūṃ,
Marked with the sublime seed syllables.
Hero, this maṇḍala of yours
Has been taught by the chief of the victors.
Māmakī and Śṛṅkhalā,
And likewise those arisen from the vajra family,
Vajrāṅkuśa and Vajra Point,
The mighty kings of mantra,
All of them should be included
Within the great maṇḍala.
So that the rites may be fully accomplished,
The mudrās, maṇḍalas, seed syllables,
And the distinctions of color, in their order,
Should be made as is fitting.

Then, furthermore, there are these great Vajradharas,
Who are taught as the chief ones:
Stainless Space, Encircled by Vajras,
Vajra Fang, the Gentle One,
The Renowned One of great fortune,
Vajra Protector, Holder of the Supreme,
Peaceful Vajra, Great Vajra,
Blue Vajra, the Other Vajra,
Vajra Lotus, the Wide-Eyed,
Excellent Vajra, Vajra Itself,
Abiding Free of Elaboration,
And Moving through Infinite Space.
Their maṇḍalas, it is rightly taught,
Are white, yellow, red,
And black.
Then, furthermore, the forms of the mudrās:
Three-pronged and single-pronged,
And also two-pronged and not two-pronged,
Like a garland, and likewise others;
They are to be known from the distinctions of color.
All of these are of great power,
And are endowed with victory.
The maṇḍala of the protector Acala
Is held to be of wind and of fire.
Below my seat,
In the direction of Nairṛti, it should be drawn.
Furnished with his own seed syllable as its sign,
The sword mudrā should be well arranged,
Or else a noose may be made;
The wise one should draw it.
And for Trailokyavijaya, the Victor over the Three Worlds,
Specifically, in the center of the wind maṇḍala,
Endowed with the emblem of the vajra bearer,
Set in three places.

Lord of Secrets, furthermore, there is the arrangement of the maṇḍala of the forms of the mothers of the bodhisattvas. There, first of all, the maṇḍala:

It is gold in color and square,
Marked with the signs of the vajra;
This is the sublime maṇḍala.
Then, furthermore, the form of the goddess:
In its center is a great lotus,
Yellow throughout and ablaze.
There too the uṣṇīṣa is to be placed,
Set in three parts;
Leaving aside the middle part,
There the Eye should be drawn,
Abiding in the midst of her own blazing light.
In brief, it should be furnished with the seed syllable.
For all the bodhisattvas
The Victor taught the wish-fulfilling jewel.
Its maṇḍala, it is taught,
Is of water, with doors on every side,
Utterly stainless, perfectly peaceful,
Fulfilling every hope.
Then, gentle one, listen also
To the maṇḍala of Śākyasiṃha.
There, the one called Mahendra,
Adorned with yellow color,
Perfectly square and even;
In its center a vajra, as before;
Upon it a lotus
Should be placed, yellow throughout;
Upon that a great alms bowl,
Marked with a vajra and ablaze;
Or else the robes and the ringed staff
Should be placed in order.
The five uṣṇīṣas that have been proclaimed:
Know their mudrās as follows.
For Sitātapatra, make a parasol.
For Jayoṣṇīṣa, the Victor's uṣṇīṣa, wise one,
The sign should be placed as a sword,
Made to radiate rays of light.
Vijaya's is a wheel;
Vikiraṇa's is held to be a hook.
That of Tejorāśi of great fortune
Is held to be an excellent uṣṇīṣa.
Mahodgata's is a vajra;
Abhyudgata's is held to be a lotus.
The conch is Anantasvaraghoṣa's,
To be known from the distinctions of color.
The ūrṇā's is the wish-fulfilling jewel.
For the Eye, listen also to this:
An uṣṇīṣa, yellow throughout,
Is marked with the signs of the vajra.
Aparājitā has
A hand holding a wondrous lotus.
The one called Aparājita
Dwells on a wide-open dark lotus.
Those known as the gods of the Pure Abodes,
Whose sphere is the realization of purity:
Gentle one, listen to the arrangement
Of the characteristics of their mudrās.
The hand of courage, the hand of virtue,
And likewise the smiling hand,
The hand holding flowers, and the hand of space:
These should be drawn according to the full rite.
The earth goddess Pṛthivī's is a vase,
In a water maṇḍala marked with a vajra.
The hand of devotion is Agni's;
It should be drawn together with the sages:
Kāśyapa and Gautama,
Mārkaṇḍa and Garga,
Vasiṣṭha and Aṅgiras,
Abiding within the place of fire.
With the Vedas in their hands, carefully,
Draw them in their order.
Yama's staff is to be drawn
Within the wind maṇḍala.
Mṛtyu's is a bell;
Kālarātri's mudrā is a banner.
Raudrī's is a trident;
Brāhmī's is remembered as a lotus;
Kaumārī's is a short spear;
Vaiṣṇavī's is a wheel;
Yama's consort is known by a mallet;
Bhairava's consort's is a human skull.
These are to be included
Within the wind maṇḍala,
Surrounded by vultures, crows,
Dogs, and jackals as well.
One who wishes the rites to be accomplished
Should draw them as is fitting.
For Nairṛti, draw a sword;
For Viṣṇu, make a wheel;
Skanda's is a short spear.
Nanda and Upananda are two clouds,
Both together with lightning,
Their forms dark blue, with water;
At the two sides of the door
Of Śākyasiṃha's maṇḍala they are to be set.
Śaṃkara's is a trident;
Umā's is taught to be a raised trident.
A white vase together with a lotus
Is remembered as the mudrā of the Moon.
The Sun's is said to be a chariot
Resting upon a Mahendra maṇḍala.
Jayā and Vijayā
Have bows bearing the Mahendra sign.
In the direction of the wind, for the Vāyus,
A banner mudrā should be drawn.
Sarasvatī's is a vīṇā.
Varuṇa, the Waters, is drawn holding a noose;
The maṇḍala of that great being
Should be known to be of water.
It should be planted with the seed syllables
And marked with the signs.
In the maṇḍala of Śākyaketu,
With these and other signs,
The maṇḍalas are made in their order;
The mudrā placed in their center
Should be furnished with the seed syllables.
Furthermore, listen carefully
To the maṇḍala of Varadavajra.
Perfectly square and even,
Marked with the signs of the vajra;
In its center is to be made
A maṇḍala arisen from fire.
In its center the mudrā to be placed
Is a beautiful utpala, as it is called,
Of wise Mañjughoṣa,
Made radiant by his own mantra.
Its place, according to the rite,
Is planted with his own seed syllable.
The outer rim, all around,
Should be covered with utpalas.
In its center are to be drawn
These heroes, in their order.

There, the mudrā of Jālinīprabha is a hook. That of the youth Ratnamukuṭa is a jewel. That of the youth Vimalaprabha is an unopened utpala.

Those who are known as the messengers
Of wise Mañjughoṣa,
Fearless one, should be known
As is fitting.
The mudrā of Keśinī
Should be known to be a sword.
Upakeśinī's is a trident;
Citrā's is taught to be a staff;
Vasumatī's is said to be a banner;
Ākarṣaṇī's is a hook.
All of these are to be marked
With the signs of the utpala.
These are the mudrās
Of the female attendants as described.
In the southern direction, with care,
The mudrā of Sarvanīvaraṇaviṣkambhin of great diligence
Is to be drawn well:
It is what is called the wish-fulfilling jewel,
Abiding within a fire maṇḍala,
Together with his retinue, in their order.
In the elaboration of the retinue,
These mudrās should be known.

The mudrā of Adbhuta is a single-pronged vajra upon a vase. That of Abhayaṃdada is the hand granting fearlessness. That of Sarvāpāyajaha should be made as a hand drawing upward as if from below. That of Paritrāṇāśayamati is the hand of compassion placed at the breast. That of Mahāmaitryabhyudgata is a hand holding a flower. That of Karuṇāmṛḍitamati is a hand placed at the heart with the middle finger bent inward. That of Sarvadāhapraśamita is a hand in the boon-granting gesture with a stream of water trickling from the fingers. The hand of the one endowed with inconceivable intelligence, Acintyamati, holds a wish-fulfilling jewel. All of these too are to rest upon lotuses and be included within the maṇḍala.

In the northern direction, the mudrā of Kṣitigarbha:
Abiding within a Mahendra maṇḍala,
The seat is to be placed as before.
Upon it is a great lotus,
Possessing every color and ablaze;
Upon that a great banner,
At whose tip a great jewel is set.
That is the supreme form;
Here it is taught as the form of the mudrā.
His retinue is countless,
The chief's qualities are infinite;
There, their mudrās as well
Are to be carefully produced and drawn.

The mudrā of Ratnākara is a three-pronged vajra resting upon a jewel. That of Ratnapāṇi is a single-pronged vajra resting upon a jewel. That of Dharaṇiṃdhara is a two-pronged vajra resting upon a jewel. The mudrā of Ratnamudrāhasta is a five-pronged vajra resting upon a jewel. That of Dṛḍhādhyāśaya is a crossed vajra resting upon a jewel. All the mudrās are to be included within the maṇḍala.

In the western direction, the mudrā of Ākāśagarbha:
In a lovely water maṇḍala,
Upon a great seat of a white lotus,
In its center a sword and a noose,
The blade supreme in the keenness of its edge,
Planted with his own seed syllables:
The learned one should place them there.
His retinue is to be drawn
As the forms of their mudrās, in their order.

There, the mudrā of Stainless Space is a wheel together with its marks, in the center of a wind maṇḍala. The mudrā of Gaganamati is a conch in the center of the maṇḍala. The mudrā of Viśuddhamati is a white lotus resting in the center of a wind maṇḍala. The mudrā of Cāritramati is an utpala placed upon a conch in the center of a wind maṇḍala. The mudrā of Sthiramati, too, rests upon a vajra lotus in the center of a wind maṇḍala.

From The Manifest and Complete Awakening of the Great Vairocana, His Miraculous Manifestation and Empowerment, this is the chapter on the secret maṇḍala.

Then again the Blessed One taught the rite for entering the secret maṇḍala:

The mantrin, thoroughly trained,
Learned in the secret maṇḍala,
Should then burn away all the disciple's wrongdoing
In accordance with the rite.
So that it may not arise again,
His very life should also be burned.
And once he has been burned and turned to ash,
He should be revived once more.
With the syllable, the immutable, burn;
With life, revive him once more.
Once all has been revived with life,
He is wholly pure and stainless.
The words of the twelve limbs
Should be set upon him at that very moment.
This pledge of the protecting bodhisattvas
And of the buddhas
Is honored by the buddhas, the śrāvakas,
And the worlds.
Whoever knows this pledge
That abides in the center of the secret maṇḍala
Has entered into all the tantras
And has mastery over every maṇḍala.
That mantra holder is like me;
The one who keeps the pledge is to be treated as no other.

This is the chapter on entering the secret maṇḍala.

Then the Blessed One Vairocana looked upon the maṇḍala of the entire assembly and said to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi: "Lord of Secrets, for bodhisattvas who practice the conduct of a bodhisattva by way of secret mantra, there are eight secret mudrās together with their secrets, the rites by which they know themselves to have become stable in the form of the deity, and, having known this, abiding in the manner of their own deity, they attain the siddhis. They bless the place of the deity, are marked with their own secret mantras, are endowed with maṇḍalas, and are to be performed in the yoga of one's own deity. What are these eight mudrās?

"Join the two hands so that they are hollow, and spread the two little fingers and the two index fingers like flames. This is the mudrā of the arising of the Blessed One's own splendor. Its maṇḍala is triangular, marked with a triangle pattern and ablaze. The secret mantra is namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ raṃ ra svāhā.

"From that same mudrā, bend the two index fingers and hold them upon the thumbs like a banner. This is the mudrā of the Blessed One's indestructible vajra. Its maṇḍala resembles a blazing syllable, with a blazing vajra. The secret mantra is namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ vaṃ va svāhā.

"From that same mudrā, extend the middle and ring fingers, and keep the rest as before. This is the mudrā of the lotus womb. Its maṇḍala resembles the disk of the moon and bears the sign of the lotus. The secret mantra is namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ saṃ sa svāhā.

"From that same mudrā, put the two little fingers underneath. This is the mudrā of the ornament of the array of the Buddha's voice. Its maṇḍala is like the full moon, with banners, and marked with the double dot of the visarga.

The secret mantra is namaḥ samanta buddhānāṃ haṃ ha svāhā.

"Likewise, join the two palms so that they are hollow, separate the two thumbs, and make the rest slightly cupped. This is the mudrā of the arising of all the limbs of the Blessed One. Its maṇḍala is a vase resembling the moon, marked with a vajra. The secret mantra is namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ aṃ a svāhā.

"From that same mudrā, bend the two middle fingers and keep the rest as they were. This is the mudrā of the Blessed One's dhāraṇī. Its maṇḍala resembles a rainbow and is endowed with all the signs; a vajra banner is raised. The secret mantra is namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ buddhadhāraṇi smṛtibaladhāraṇīkari dhara dhara dhāraya dhāraya sabhagavate ākārāvāti asame svāhā.

"From that same mudrā, put out the two middle fingers, bend the two little fingers with the two middle fingers, and join them at the crown of the head. This is the mudrā of the Blessed One's abiding Dharma. Its maṇḍala has all the colors of the sky and is marked with the double dot of the visarga. The secret mantra is namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ aveda vedye a svāhā.

"From that same mudrā, hold the two middle fingers crossed over each other. This is the mudrā of the Blessed One's holding of yoga. Its maṇḍala is space, especially blue, bearing the anusvāra, and majestic. The secret mantra is namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ mahāyoga yogīnī yogeśvarā khañjarike svāhā.

"Lord of Secrets, these are the secret mudrās. Since they are the utmost secret of the tathāgatas, do not give them to anyone at all other than those who have been empowered, who are of noble family, who are enthusiastic, steadfast in their commitments, respectful toward the guru, grateful, scrupulously clean, and who have no regard even for their own body and life."

From The Manifest and Complete Awakening of the Great Vairocana, His Miraculous Manifestation and Empowerment, this is the chapter on the eight secret mudrās.

Then again, at that time, the Blessed One, the Great Vairocana, looked upon the beings of the future and entered into the samādhi called Following All. As soon as the Blessed One had entered into it, this buddhafield became level like the palm of the hand. It was adorned with archways and porticoes richly ornamented with the five kinds of jewels, made vast by raised banners of many colors, and spread with canopies of great jewels. Jeweled bells, white yak-tail whisks, and many silks and cloths were hung there, and jeweled pillars were erected in the eight directions. It was filled with lotus ponds brimming with fragrant water possessing the eight qualities, resounding with the calls of geese, ruddy geese, and swans, and adorned in the eight directions with trees bearing the various flowers of every season. It was well encircled with garlands and threads of the five kinds of jewels. Its ground was pleasant to the touch and exceedingly soft, like soft kācalindika cloth, and many kinds of delightful and melodious sounds of drums and cymbals resounded there. It came to possess seats, dwellings, and mind-born celestial palaces gathered through the merit of many bodhisattvas.

There also arose a great king of lotuses, born from the inclination of the tathāgatas and marked with the sign of the realization of the dharmadhātu. In its center abided the body of the Tathāgata, intent upon the dharmadhātu, satisfying sentient beings in accordance with their intentions and inclinations. From all the limbs of that tathāgata there arose bodies endowed with irresistible power, born from inclination toward the ten powers, adorning themselves with many kinds of colors and shapes, and produced through the merit of the perfections of generosity, moral discipline, patience, diligence, meditative concentration, and wisdom over many hundreds of thousands of koṭis of niyutas of eons. Having arisen, they made their voice heard throughout all world systems and spoke this inspired utterance:

"How wonderful! The wisdom and means
Of all the buddhas are inconceivable.
Having realized what has no abode,
They teach a Dharma that has an abode.
Those for whom the characteristic
Of the nature of dharmas is not found,
Through that knowledge attain the attainment
That all the guides have attained."

When they had spoken that inspired utterance, they entered once again into the inconceivable dharmakāya of the Tathāgata.

Then the Blessed One said to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi: "Son of noble family, listen to the inner maṇḍala. Lord of Secrets, the ground of the body is to be blessed with the blessing of the mantra and mudrā whose nature is the dharmadhātu, because it is pure by nature. Then, having blessed oneself as the Vajrasattva of action, one should imagine the following. Free of all dust, free of the faults that are the thorns of a self, a sentient being, a life principle, a nourisher, a human being, one born of Manu, an agent, and so on, it is square with four gates, the western gate always open, and adorned with archways and terraces. In its center has arisen a great, mind-born king of lotuses, eight-petaled, with its stalk, its anthers fully developed and exceedingly beautiful. In its center, again, imagine the body, speech, and mind of the Tathāgata, transcending all worlds, having perfectly gone beyond the ground of mind and attained the ground of mind, bestowing the special fruits that are desired.

"To its east is the tathāgata Ratnaketu. To the south is the tathāgata Saṃkusumitarājendra. To the north is the tathāgata Dundubhisvara. To the west is the tathāgata Amitābha. In the southeastern quarter is the bodhisattva Samantabhadra. In the northeastern quarter is Avalokiteśvara. In the southwestern quarter is Mañjuśrī Kumārabhūta. In the northwestern quarter is Maitreya. On all the anthers draw the mothers of the bodhisattvas, the six perfections, and the samādhis. Below, draw the vidyā-mantras and the hosts of wrathful ones. On the stalk is Vajrapāṇi. All the rest is the great ocean.

Lamps, flowers, and incense,
Food offerings and fragrant smoke, arisen from the mind:
For the sake of the pledge of embodied beings,
The mantrin should imagine them all.
And on all the ground below,
The countless gods and the rest
In this maṇḍala of the great being
The mantrin should carefully generate and draw.
Having thoroughly purified his own body
